==================================================
FILE: README.md
==================================================
<img src="assets/glow_icon_breath.svg" width="300" alt="Glow Icon" />

A user-friendly flow analysis pipeline with pretty UI and Active Learning capabilities.

## Overview

Glow is designed to streamline flow cytometry data analysis. It provides an interactive interface for loading FCS files, managing metadata, and performing gating (Live/Dead and Singlets). It features an **Active Learning** system that learns from user feedback to improve automated gating suggestions over time based on Cell Line characteristics.

### Installation

### Linux

1. Clone the repository.
2. Create the environment:
   ```bash
   conda env create -f environment.yml
   conda activate glow_env
   ```

   *(Or install dependencies listed in `glow/data_loading` and `glow/gating` manually).*

### Windows & Mac

For Windows and Mac users, a standalone app will be provided—no manual installation required. Please check the releases or contact the maintainers for the latest installer.

## Usage

### 1. Starting the Application

#### Linux

Run the Streamlit UI:

```bash
streamlit run ui/app.py
```

#### Windows

Run the provided `.exe` application (no command line required).

#### Mac

Run the provided app bundle (no command line required).

### 2. Workflow

1. **File Loading & Metadata**:

   * Select "Enter Directory Path" to scan a folder of `.fcs` files.
   * **Or select "Upload Files / Drag & Drop Folder"** to upload from your local machine.
   * For metadata, you have two options:
     - **Upload your metadata spreadsheet** (e.g., CSV or Excel) and the app will read it.
     - **Manually input all metadata** in the UI.
   * When entering metadata, make sure to provide **Cell Line** and **Experiment Name** (these are important for distinguishing conditions; the same condition for different samples should have consistent names).
   * Click "Generate Preview" to review and edit metadata (Sample Name, Replicate, **Cell Line**, etc.).
   * Confirm to load files.
   * The loader can also automatically detect cell line info from file names.
2. **Live/Dead Gating**:

   * The app attempts to Auto-Gate using one of the options: **a pre-trained MLP model**, Density-based selection, Gaussian Mixture Model (GMM), or DBSCAN-based clustering on the left side.
   * **Accept Auto**: If the suggestion is good, click "✅ Accept Gate". This logs the live and dead cells.
   * **Manual Gate**: Click "❌ Reject / Manual Gate".
     * Click roughly **5 points** on the plot around your population.
     * Confirm the selection. This logs the custom polygon for selection. There could be a lag in manual selection, be patient.
3. **Singlet Gating**:

   * After Live gating, the app discriminates Singlets vs Doublets.
   * **Default Strategy**: Uses either **a pre-trained MLP model** or Gaussian Mixture Model (GMM) on user-selected channels (e.g., FSC-A vs FSC-H) on the left side.
   * **Accept Auto**: If the suggestion is good, click "✅ Accept Singlets". This logs the singlet and doublet cells.
   * **Manual Detection**: Revert to manual polygon selection if the auto detection fails.
4. **Final Data Settings (Export)**:

   * Once Singlets are accepted, the export options appear.
   * **Channel Selection**: You can modify this to include/exclude specific channels.
   * **Export**: Save the "Gated Population" (Live + Singlets) or generate a full annotated dataset. You can also change the names of file to download here.

5. **Analysis**:

   * After exporting or selecting your data, you can perform further analysis within the app:
   * **Transfection Efficiency Gate**: Specialized gating for transfection efficiency assessment. You can do gating for binary or just binary.
   * **2D Quadratic Gating**: Use quadratic boundaries for more complex population separation in 2D plots.
   * Additional analysis tools and visualizations are available to support your experimental needs.


6. **Plotting**:

   * Glow supports a variety of plotting options to help visualize your data:
   * **1D Stack Plot**: Visualize distributions across samples or conditions in a stacked format.
   * **2D Running Means**: Display trends and relationships between two variables using running means in 2D plots.
   * **Bar Plots**: Summarize and compare data across groups or conditions with customizable bar plots.
   * Additional plotting features are available for in-depth data exploration.

### 3. Canvas Customization

Glow provides a canvas feature where you can customize the appearance of your plots, all plots are able to added to canvas to tune or directly downloaded as svg to be further modified in illustrator easily:

* Change fonts to match your presentation or publication style.
* Select different color palettes for better visualization and accessibility.
* Adjust other visual elements to tailor the output to your needs.

FILE: check_glow_utils.py
==================================================
import numpy as np
import sys
import os

sys.path.append(os.getcwd())

from glow.utils import safe_log10, safe_log

def test_safe_log10():
    print("Testing safe_log10...")
    
    # CASE 1: Basic Positive
    arr1 = np.array([1, 10, 100])
    res1 = safe_log10(arr1)
    assert np.allclose(res1, [0, 1, 2]), f"Failed Positive: {res1}"
    print("  Positive: OK")
    
    # CASE 2: Negative with NaN replacement (Default)
    arr2 = np.array([-1, 0, 10])
    res2 = safe_log10(arr2)
    # Expected: [NaN, NaN, 1]
    assert np.isnan(res2[0]), "Failed Nan Replace (Neg)"
    assert np.isnan(res2[1]), "Failed Nan Replace (Zero)"
    assert np.isclose(res2[2], 1.0), "Failed Positive Mixing"
    print("  NaN Replace: OK")
    
    # CASE 3: Clipping
    arr3 = np.array([-100, 0, 100])
    res3 = safe_log10(arr3, min_val=0.1)
    # Expected: log10(0.1), log10(0.1), log10(100) -> -1, -1, 2
    assert np.allclose(res3, [-1, -1, 2]), f"Failed Clipping: {res3}"
    print("  Clipping: OK")
    
    print("safe_log10 PASSED")

def test_safe_log():
    print("\nTesting safe_log (ln)...")
    
    arr = np.array([np.e, 1, 0, -1])
    res = safe_log(arr, replace_with_nan=True)
    
    assert np.isclose(res[0], 1.0)
    assert np.isclose(res[1], 0.0)
    assert np.isnan(res[2])
    assert np.isnan(res[3])
    print("  Basic: OK")
    
    # Clipping
    res_c = safe_log(arr, min_val=1.0)
    # log(e)=1, log(1)=0, log(clip(0->1))=0, log(clip(-1->1))=0
    assert np.allclose(res_c, [1, 0, 0, 0])
    print("  Clipping: OK")
    
    print("safe_log PASSED")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    try:
        test_safe_log10()
        test_safe_log()
        print("\nALL TRIALS SUCCESSFUL")
    except ImportError as e:
        print(f"Import Error (likely missing numpy): {e}")
    except AssertionError as e:
        print(f"TEST FAILED: {e}")
        sys.exit(1)
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"An error occurred: {e}")
        sys.exit(1)

==================================================
FILE: training/extract_samples.py

import os
import random
import shutil
import argparse
import sys

def extract_samples(source_root, dest_root):
    """
    Scans source_root for directories containing .fcs files.
    Picks one random .fcs file > 1MB from each directory.
    Copies it to dest_root with renamed format {folder_name}_{file_name}.fcs.
    Replaces spaces with underscores.
    """
    
    # Ensure destination exists
    if not os.path.exists(dest_root):
        try:
            os.makedirs(dest_root)
            print(f"Created destination directory: {dest_root}")
        except OSError as e:
            print(f"Error creating destination directory: {e}")
            return

    print(f"Scanning source: {source_root}")
    print(f"Destination: {dest_root}")
    print("-" * 50)

    count = 0
    folders_processed = 0

    # Walk the directory tree
    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(source_root):
        
        # Filter for valid FCS files in this current directory
        fcs_candidates = []
        
        for file in files:
            if file.lower().endswith('.fcs'):
                file_path = os.path.join(root, file)
                
                # Check size > 1MB (1 * 1024 * 1024 bytes)
                try:
                    if os.path.getsize(file_path) > 1 * 1024 * 1024:
                        fcs_candidates.append(file)
                except OSError as e:
                    print(f"Could not check size for {file_path}: {e}")

        if fcs_candidates:
            folders_processed += 1
            
            # Randomly pick 1 file
            chosen_file = random.choice(fcs_candidates)
            source_path = os.path.join(root, chosen_file)
            
            # Get folder name logic
            # os.path.basename(root) gives the current folder name
            folder_name = os.path.basename(root)
            
            # If root is the source_root and it has files, folder_name is source_root's name
            # This is generally what is requested.
            
            # Sanitize names (replace spaces with underscores)
            safe_folder_name = folder_name.replace(" ", "_")
            safe_file_name = chosen_file.replace(" ", "_")
            
            # Construct new filename
            # chosen_file ends with .fcs, so we don't need to add it again unless we stripped it
            # Requirement: {folder_name}_{file_name}.fcs
            # If filename is "Sample 1.fcs", safe is "Sample_1.fcs"
            # Result: "Folder_Sample_1.fcs"
            
            new_filename = f"{safe_folder_name}_{safe_file_name}"
            dest_path = os.path.join(dest_root, new_filename)
            
            # Copy
            try:
                # Check if file already exists to avoid overwriting or duplicate work if run multiple times
                if os.path.exists(dest_path):
                    print(f"Skipping (exists): {new_filename}")
                else:
                    shutil.copy2(source_path, dest_path)
                    print(f"Extracted: {new_filename} (from {folder_name})")
                    count += 1
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Error copying {source_path}: {e}")
                
    print("-" * 50)
    print(f"Completed. Found FCS candidates in {folders_processed} folders.")
    print(f"Successfully copied {count} files to {dest_root}.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Extract random FCS samples > 1MB from subfolders.")
    parser.add_argument("source_dir", help="Path to the root folder containing your FCS file data structure")
    parser.add_argument("--dest_dir", default="training/source_fcs", help="Destination folder (default: training/source_fcs)")
    
    args = parser.parse_args()
    
    # Check source
    if not os.path.exists(args.source_dir):
        print(f"Error: Source directory '{args.source_dir}' does not exist.")
        sys.exit(1)
        
    extract_samples(os.path.abspath(args.source_dir), os.path.abspath(args.dest_dir))

FILE: training/train_data_gen.py

import streamlit as st
import os
import glob
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import plotly.express as px
import plotly.graph_objects as go
import cytoflow
import sys

# Ensure project root is in path so we can import 'glow'
current_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
project_root = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(current_dir, '..'))
if project_root not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, project_root)

from glow.gating.manager import GatingManager

# Configure Page
st.set_page_config(page_title="Training Data Generator", layout="wide")

SOURCE_DIR = "training/source_fcs"
OUTPUT_DIR = "training/labeled_data"

# Ensure directories exist
os.makedirs(SOURCE_DIR, exist_ok=True)
os.makedirs(OUTPUT_DIR, exist_ok=True)

def load_fcs(filepath):
    """Load FCS file into Cytoflow Experiment"""
    try:
        tube = cytoflow.Tube(file=filepath)
        import_op = cytoflow.ImportOp(tubes=[tube])
        experiment = import_op.apply()
        return experiment
    except Exception as e:
        st.error(f"Error loading {filepath}: {e}")
        return None

def manual_gate_ui(experiment, x_channel, y_channel, key_prefix, info_text=""):
    """
    Reusable Manual Gating UI.
    Returns: vertices (list of tuples) or None
    """
    st.markdown(f"### {key_prefix} Gating: {x_channel} vs {y_channel}")
    if info_text:
        st.info(info_text)

    # Log Scale Toggle
    col_log, _ = st.columns([1, 4])
    with col_log:
        is_log = st.checkbox("Log Scale", value=True, key=f"{key_prefix}_log")

    # Prepare Data for Plotting
    df_viz = experiment.data.copy()
    
    # --- Density Calculation (Fast 2D Histogram Method) ---
    color_col = None
    try:
        if is_log:
             # Safe Log10 for density calculation
             x_data = df_viz[x_channel].copy()
             y_data = df_viz[y_channel].copy()
             # Replace 0 or negative with NaN for log
             x_data[x_data <= 0] = np.nan
             y_data[y_data <= 0] = np.nan
             x_data_log = np.log10(x_data)
             y_data_log = np.log10(y_data)
        else:
             x_data_log = df_viz[x_channel]
             y_data_log = df_viz[y_channel]

        # Remove infs/nans for calculation
        mask = np.isfinite(x_data_log) & np.isfinite(y_data_log)
        x_clean, y_clean = x_data_log[mask], y_data_log[mask]
        
        if len(x_clean) > 0:
            # Fast density via histogram
            H, xedges, yedges = np.histogram2d(x_clean, y_clean, bins=100)
            
            # Map density back to points
            x_bins = np.digitize(x_clean, xedges) - 1
            y_bins = np.digitize(y_clean, yedges) - 1
            
            x_bins = np.clip(x_bins, 0, H.shape[0]-1)
            y_bins = np.clip(y_bins, 0, H.shape[1]-1)
            
            # Assign density values
            df_viz['Density'] = 0.0
            df_viz.loc[mask, 'Density'] = H[x_bins, y_bins]
            color_col = 'Density'
    except Exception:
        color_col = None

    # Use a fraction of points for smoother plotting if large
    if len(df_viz) > 5000:
        df_plot = df_viz.sample(n=5000, random_state=42)
        st.caption(f"Plotting 5,000 representative events (out of {len(df_viz)}) for performance.")
    else:
        df_plot = df_viz

    # Setup Plot
    fig = px.scatter(
        df_plot, 
        x=x_channel, 
        y=y_channel, 
        color=color_col,
        color_continuous_scale='Viridis',
        opacity=0.8, 
        render_mode='svg',
        log_x=is_log,
        log_y=is_log,
        title=f"{key_prefix} Setup"
    )

    if color_col:
        fig.update_traces(marker=dict(size=8)) # Increased size for easier clicking
        fig.update_layout(coloraxis_colorbar=dict(title="Density"))

    # State Keys
    pending_key = f"{key_prefix}_pending"
    input_key = f"{key_prefix}_input"
    
    # Initialize pending if not exists
    if pending_key not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state[pending_key] = []

    # --- Pending Gate Visualization (Immediate Feedback) ---
    # Add traces for the polygon being drawn
    if st.session_state[pending_key]:
        p_verts = st.session_state[pending_key]
        # Close loop for visual
        p_path_x = [v[0] for v in p_verts] + [p_verts[0][0]]
        p_path_y = [v[1] for v in p_verts] + [p_verts[0][1]]
        
        fig.add_trace(go.Scatter(x=p_path_x, y=p_path_y, mode="lines+markers", 
                                line=dict(color="cyan", width=4, dash='dash'), 
                                marker=dict(size=8, color="cyan"),
                                name="New Selection (Pending)"))

    # Improved Plot Layout (White Background, Black Axes) matches reference
    fig.update_layout(
        plot_bgcolor='white',
        paper_bgcolor='white',
        xaxis=dict(showgrid=True, gridcolor='lightgrey', zeroline=True, zerolinecolor='lightgrey', color='black', tickfont=dict(color='black'), fixedrange=True),
        yaxis=dict(showgrid=True, gridcolor='lightgrey', zeroline=True, zerolinecolor='lightgrey', color='black', tickfont=dict(color='black'), fixedrange=True),
        font=dict(color="black"),
        dragmode=False, # Disable dragging to make point click easier
        clickmode='event+select',
        legend=dict(
            orientation="h",
            yanchor="bottom",
            y=1.02,
            xanchor="right",
            x=1
        )
    )

    # 1. Render Plot
    selection = st.plotly_chart(
        fig, 
        use_container_width=True, 
        on_select="rerun", 
        selection_mode="points", 
        key=f"{key_prefix}_plot"
    )

    # 2. Logic to accumulate points (Exact copy of ui_gating.py logic)
    if selection and selection.get("selection") and selection["selection"].get("points"):
        pts = selection["selection"]["points"]
        count = len(pts)
        
        if count > 0:
            # Extract new points
            new_x = [p["x"] for p in pts]
            new_y = [p["y"] for p in pts]
            new_candidates = list(zip(new_x, new_y))
            
            # Get current pending points
            current_pending = st.session_state[pending_key]
            updated_pending = list(current_pending)
            
            has_changes = False
            for cand in new_candidates:
                # Check if close to existing
                is_close = False
                for ex in current_pending:
                    # Match tolerance from ui_gating.py
                    if np.isclose(cand[0], ex[0], atol=1e-5) and np.isclose(cand[1], ex[1], atol=1e-5):
                        is_close = True
                        break
                if not is_close:
                    updated_pending.append(cand)
                    has_changes = True
            
            if has_changes:
                # Sort if >= 3 to make polygon (Convex/Angular sort)
                final_pending = updated_pending
                if len(updated_pending) >= 3:
                    try:
                        arr = np.array(updated_pending)
                        cx, cy = np.mean(arr[:,0]), np.mean(arr[:,1])
                        angles = np.arctan2(arr[:,1] - cy, arr[:,0] - cx)
                        indices = np.argsort(angles)
                        final_pending = [updated_pending[i] for i in indices]
                    except: pass
                
                st.session_state[pending_key] = final_pending
                st.rerun()

    # 3. Actions / Confirmation
    if st.session_state[pending_key]:
        st.info(f"Selected {len(st.session_state[pending_key])} points (Polygon).")
        col1, col2 = st.columns(2)
        with col1:
             if st.button("Clear Points", key=f"{key_prefix}_clear"):
                st.session_state[pending_key] = []
                st.rerun()
    
    # Return vertices if they exist
    if len(st.session_state[pending_key]) >= 3:
        return st.session_state[pending_key]
    return None

def render_training_gui():
    st.title("Glow Training Data Generator")

    # --- Step 0: Setup / Folder Selection ---
    if "source_dir" not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.source_dir = "training/source_fcs"
    
    col_src, col_btn = st.columns([4, 1])
    with col_src:
        source_input = st.text_input("Source FCS Folder", value=st.session_state.source_dir)
        st.session_state.source_dir = source_input
    
    # --- Step 1: Scan & Annotate ---
    if "fcs_df" not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.fcs_df = pd.DataFrame(columns=["Filename", "FullPath", "CellType"])
    
    if st.button("Scan Folder"):
        if os.path.isdir(st.session_state.source_dir):
            files = []
            for root, _, filenames in os.walk(st.session_state.source_dir):
                for f in filenames:
                    if f.lower().endswith(".fcs"):
                        files.append(os.path.join(root, f))
            
            files = sorted(files)
            data = []
            for f in files:
                fname = os.path.basename(f)
                # Try to infer cell type from parent folder 
                parent = os.path.basename(os.path.dirname(f))
                
                # Heuristic: If folder seems to be a known cell line, use it. 
                # Otherwise default to 'hek' as requested, avoiding random folder names as types.
                if parent.lower() in ['snu', 'colo', 'hug1n', 'hug1']:
                    ctype = parent
                else:
                    ctype = "hek"
                
                data.append({"Filename": fname, "FullPath": f, "CellType": ctype})
            
            st.session_state.fcs_df = pd.DataFrame(data)
            st.session_state.step = "annotation"
        else:
            st.error("Invalid Directory")

    if st.session_state.get("step") == "annotation":
        st.subheader("1. Annotate Cell Types")
        st.info("Assign a Cell Type to each file. Files with the same Cell Type will be gated together.")
        
        edited_df = st.data_editor(
            st.session_state.fcs_df[["Filename", "CellType"]],
            disabled=["Filename"],
            num_rows="fixed",
            use_container_width=True,
            height=400
        )
        
        # Update session state with edits
        st.session_state.fcs_df.update(edited_df)
        
        if st.button("Confirm Annotations & Proceed"):
            # Clean up empty or Unknown
            final_df = st.session_state.fcs_df.copy()
            final_df = final_df[final_df["CellType"].str.strip() != ""]
            st.session_state.fcs_df = final_df
            st.session_state.unique_types = sorted(final_df["CellType"].unique().tolist())
            st.session_state.current_type_idx = 0
            st.session_state.step = "gating"
            st.rerun()

    # --- Step 2: Aggregated Gating ---
    if st.session_state.get("step") == "gating":
        unique_types = st.session_state.unique_types
        idx = st.session_state.current_type_idx
        
        if idx >= len(unique_types):
            st.success("All Cell Types Processed! 🎉")
            if st.button("Start Over"):
                st.session_state.clear()
                st.rerun()
            return

        current_type = unique_types[idx]
        
        # Sidebar Progress
        st.sidebar.title("Progress")
        st.sidebar.progress((idx) / len(unique_types))
        st.sidebar.write(f"Processing: **{current_type}**")
        st.sidebar.write(f"Queue: {unique_types[idx:]}")

        st.header(f"Step 2: Gating for '{current_type}'")
        
        # Get files for this type
        type_files = st.session_state.fcs_df[st.session_state.fcs_df["CellType"] == current_type]["FullPath"].tolist()
        st.write(f"Found {len(type_files)} files for {current_type}.")
        
        # Load Aggregate Data (session state cache)
        # We need a key that changes when type changes
        agg_key = f"agg_data_{current_type}"
        
        if agg_key not in st.session_state:
            with st.spinner(f"Loading representative data for {current_type}..."):
                # Load max 2000 events per file to keep it snappy, up to 100k total
                dfs = []
                # Limit input files if too many? No, we need representation.
                # Just sample small amount.
                sample_size = 2000 
                
                for fpath in type_files:
                    try:
                        tube = cytoflow.Tube(file=fpath)
                        # We can't easily partial load with cytoflow without full read first usually
                        # unless we use low level io. 
                        # Optimization: Read full, sample, discard.
                        import_op = cytoflow.ImportOp(tubes=[tube])
                        exp = import_op.apply()
                        
                        d = exp.data
                        if len(d) > sample_size:
                            d = d.sample(n=sample_size, random_state=42)
                        dfs.append(d)
                    except:
                        pass
                
                if dfs:
                    combined_df = pd.concat(dfs, ignore_index=True)
                    # Create a dummy experiment wrapper for our UI functions
                    # We can use the last loaded experiment object structure just for metadata (channels)
                    agg_exp = exp.clone()
                    agg_exp.data = combined_df
                    st.session_state[agg_key] = agg_exp
                else:
                    st.error("Could not load any data.")
                    return

        agg_exp = st.session_state[agg_key]
        
        # --- Gating UI (Same as before but on aggregated data) ---
        
        # Channel Selection (assume consistent across type)
        cols = agg_exp.data.columns.tolist()
        fsc_a = next((c for c in cols if "FSC" in c and "-A" in c), cols[0])
        ssc_a = next((c for c in cols if "SSC" in c and "-A" in c), cols[1] if len(cols)>1 else cols[0])
        
        c1, c2 = st.columns(2)
        lx = c1.selectbox("X Channel (Live)", cols, index=cols.index(fsc_a) if fsc_a in cols else 0, key=f"lx_{current_type}")
        ly = c2.selectbox("Y Channel (Live)", cols, index=cols.index(ssc_a) if ssc_a in cols else 0, key=f"ly_{current_type}")
        
        live_verts = manual_gate_ui(agg_exp, lx, ly, f"Live_{current_type}", f"Define Live Gate for ALL {current_type} data.")
        
        if live_verts and len(live_verts) >= 3:
            st.success("Live Gate Defined.")
            
            # Filter aggregate for singlets
            gater = GatingManager()
            # We assume gater can work on this dummy experiment
            # But GatingManager might rely on standard flow. Let's replicate manual filter.
            # Actually apply_manual_gate creates a copy.
            
            # Apply to AGGREGATE for visualization
            live_agg = gater.apply_manual_gate(agg_exp, "TempLive", lx, ly, live_verts)
            if "TempLive" in live_agg.data.columns:
                 live_filtered_data = live_agg.data[live_agg.data["TempLive"]].copy()
            else:
                 live_filtered_data = live_agg.data
            
            live_filtered_exp = agg_exp.clone()
            live_filtered_exp.data = live_filtered_data

            st.divider()
            st.subheader("Singlet Gating")
            
            fsc_h = next((c for c in cols if "FSC" in c and "-H" in c), cols[0])
            c3, c4 = st.columns(2)
            sx = c3.selectbox("X Channel (Singlet)", cols, index=cols.index(fsc_a) if fsc_a in cols else 0, key=f"sx_{current_type}")
            sy = c4.selectbox("Y Channel (Singlet)", cols, index=cols.index(fsc_h) if fsc_h in cols else 0, key=f"sy_{current_type}")

            singlet_verts = manual_gate_ui(live_filtered_exp, sx, sy, f"Singlets_{current_type}", f"Define Singlet Gate for {current_type}.")

            if singlet_verts and len(singlet_verts) >= 3:
                st.success("Singlet Gate Defined.")
                
                st.divider()
                if st.button(f"🚀 Process & Save All '{current_type}' Files", type="primary"):
                    progress_bar = st.progress(0.0)
                    status_text = st.empty()
                    
                    # Create Output Dir
                    type_out_dir = os.path.join(OUTPUT_DIR, current_type)
                    os.makedirs(type_out_dir, exist_ok=True)
                    
                    gater = GatingManager() # Re-init
                    
                    for i, fpath in enumerate(type_files):
                        fname = os.path.basename(fpath)
                        status_text.text(f"Processing {i+1}/{len(type_files)}: {fname}")
                        
                        try:
                            # Load FULL file
                            tube = cytoflow.Tube(file=fpath)
                            import_op = cytoflow.ImportOp(tubes=[tube])
                            full_exp = import_op.apply()
                            
                            df_out = full_exp.data.copy()
                            
                            # Apply Gates
                            exp_L = gater.apply_manual_gate(full_exp, "Live_Flag", lx, ly, live_verts)
                            df_out['cells'] = exp_L.data['Live_Flag'].astype(bool)
                            
                            exp_S = gater.apply_manual_gate(full_exp, "Singlet_Flag", sx, sy, singlet_verts)
                            
                            # Ensure singlets are subset of cells (Requirement: no cells=FALSE and singlets=TRUE)
                            raw_singlets = exp_S.data['Singlet_Flag'].astype(bool)
                            df_out['singlets'] = raw_singlets & df_out['cells']
                            
                            # Save
                            # Format: {folder_name}_{fcs_file_name_no_ext}.csv.gz (spaces to underscores)
                            parent_folder = os.path.basename(os.path.dirname(fpath))
                            # Sanitize naming
                            safe_parent = parent_folder.replace(" ", "_")
                            
                            # Remove extension using splitext
                            fname_no_ext = os.path.splitext(fname)[0]
                            safe_fname = fname_no_ext.replace(" ", "_")
                            
                            out_name = f"{safe_parent}_{safe_fname}.csv.gz"
                            out_path = os.path.join(type_out_dir, out_name)
                            df_out.to_csv(out_path, index=False, compression='gzip')
                            
                        except Exception as e:
                            st.error(f"Failed to process {fname}: {e}")
                        
                        progress_bar.progress((i + 1) / len(type_files))
                    
                    st.success(f"Completed {current_type}!")
                    
                    # Advance State
                    st.session_state.current_type_idx += 1
                    # Clear memory
                    del st.session_state[agg_key] 
                    st.rerun()

    # Clear lingering states from previous runs to avoid confusion if needed



if __name__ == "__main__":
    render_training_gui()

==================================================
FILE: tests/test_histogram_logic.py
==================================================
import unittest
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import plotly.graph_objects as go
import sys
import os

# Adjust path to import glow
sys.path.append(os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..')))

from glow.analysis.positive import get_channel_data_for_samples, calculate_positive_stats
from glow.analysis.stats import get_summary_statistics

class MockExperiment:
    def __init__(self, data):
        self.data = data
        # Simulate that channels property might exist but we shouldn't use it in stats if it crashes
        # But here we just mock data.

    @property
    def conditions(self):
        # Simulate the crash typical of Cytoflow when metadata is out of sync
        raise KeyError("Metadata missing 'cells'")

class TestHistogramIntergration(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        # Create a dataframe with mixed sample types (some might read as int, some string?)
        # Standard flow data often has 'Sample' as a string column
        # Samples: S1 (normal), S2 (high dynamic range), S3 (negative/zero only)
        self.df = pd.DataFrame({
            'Sample': ['S1', 'S1', 'S2', 'S2', 'S3', 'S3', 'S4'],
            'GFP-A': [10.0, 100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0, -50.0, 0.0, np.nan],
            'cells': [True] * 7,
            'singlets': [True] * 7
        })
        self.experiment = MockExperiment(self.df)

    def test_end_to_end_data_flow(self):
        """Simulate the flow from Stats generation -> UI Selection -> Data Retrieval -> Plotting"""

        # 1. UI: Get Summary Stats to populate sample list
        stats_df = get_summary_statistics(self.experiment)
        sample_list = stats_df['Sample'].unique().tolist()

        print(f"\nUI Sample List: {sample_list}")

        # Verify samples are found
        self.assertIn('S1', sample_list)
        self.assertIn('S3', sample_list)

        # 2. UI: User selects 'S2' (High value) and 'S3' (Negative/Zero) and S4 (NaN)
        selected_samples = ['S2', 'S3', 'S4']
        curr_channel = 'GFP-A'

        # 3. UI: Fetch Data
        data_map = get_channel_data_for_samples(self.experiment, curr_channel, selected_samples)

        # Verify Data Map
        self.assertIn('S2', data_map)
        self.assertIn('S3', data_map)
        self.assertIn('S4', data_map)

        # Check values consistency
        s4_vals = data_map['S4']
        print(f"Data S4: {s4_vals}")

        # 4. UI Logic: Calculate Range for Slider
        all_values = []
        for s in data_map:
            # We must handle NaNs here if the UI does filtering
            # But the CURRENT UI code effectively trusts np.min/max

            # The UI flattens list then calls min/max (which propagates NaNs)
            all_values.extend(data_map[s])

        # If we have NaNs, np.min returns NaN
        # We need to simulate the FIX I'm about to make.
        # Clean NaNs
        all_values = [x for x in all_values if not np.isnan(x)]

        raw_min = float(np.min(all_values))
        raw_max = float(np.max(all_values))

        print(f"Raw Range: {raw_min} to {raw_max}")
        self.assertEqual(raw_min, -50.0)
        self.assertEqual(raw_max, 10000.0)


        # 5. UI Logic: Log Scale Filtering
        use_log = True
        pos_vals = [v for v in all_values if v > 0]

        # Check what happens if we force log
        if not pos_vals:
            use_log = False

        valid_min = float(np.min(pos_vals)) if pos_vals else raw_min
        valid_max = float(np.max(pos_vals)) if pos_vals else raw_max

        print(f"Log Range: {valid_min} to {valid_max}")
        self.assertEqual(valid_min, 1000.0)

        # 6. Plotly Object Creation (Smoke Test)
        fig = go.Figure()
        for s in selected_samples:
            vals = data_map[s]
            # UI Code: if use_log: vals = vals[vals > 0]
            if use_log:
                vals = vals[vals > 0]

            if len(vals) > 0:
                fig.add_trace(go.Histogram(x=vals, name=s))

        # If S3 was all negative/zero, it should not be in the plot if log is True
        # checking trace names
        trace_names = [t.name for t in fig.data]
        print(f"Traces in Figure: {trace_names}")

        self.assertIn('S2', trace_names)
        self.assertNotIn('S3', trace_names) # S3 (-50, 0) filtered out by log logic

        # 7. Verify Layout Range logic
        layout_range = None
        if use_log:
            if valid_min > 0:
                layout_range = [np.log10(valid_min), np.log10(valid_max)]

        print(f"Layout Range: {layout_range}")
        self.assertAlmostEqual(layout_range[0], 3.0) # log10(1000)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(layout_range[1], 4.0) # log10(10000)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()


==================================================
FILE: tests/test_compensation_high_baseline.py
==================================================
import unittest
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import logging
from glow.compensation.manager import CompensationManager

# Configure logging to see output during tests if needed
logging.basicConfig(level=logging.DEBUG)

class TestCompensationHighBaseline(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        self.manager = CompensationManager()
        self.channels = ['FITC-A', 'PE-A']
        self.baseline_val = 10000.0
        
        # 1. Simulate Data with High Baseline
        # Unstained: Gaussian around 10,000
        self.df_unstained = pd.DataFrame({
            'FITC-A': np.random.normal(self.baseline_val, 100, 1000),
            'PE-A': np.random.normal(self.baseline_val, 100, 1000),
            'Sample': 'Unstained'
        })
        
        # FITC Single Stain:
        # FITC High (True signal ~100,000 + Baseline)
        # PE Spillover = 20% of (FITC_Observed - Baseline)
        # Observed PE = Baseline + 0.2 * (True_FITC)
        
        # Let's construct it carefully:
        # True FITC Signal = Random(100,000)
        true_fitc = np.random.normal(100000, 5000, 1000)
        
        obs_fitc = true_fitc + self.baseline_val
        # PE sees 20% of the True FITC signal + Baseline
        obs_pe = (true_fitc * 0.2) + self.baseline_val
        
        self.df_fitc = pd.DataFrame({
            'FITC-A': obs_fitc,
            'PE-A': obs_pe,
            'Sample': 'FITC_Control'
        })
        
        # PE Single Stain: (Assume no spill to FITC for simplicity, or 0% spill)
        true_pe = np.random.normal(100000, 5000, 1000)
        obs_pe_2 = true_pe + self.baseline_val
        obs_fitc_2 = self.baseline_val # No spill
        
        self.df_pe = pd.DataFrame({
            'FITC-A': obs_fitc_2,
            'PE-A': obs_pe_2,
            'Sample': 'PE_Control'
        })
        
        # Combine into Experiment-like structure
        self.full_df = pd.concat([self.df_unstained, self.df_fitc, self.df_pe], ignore_index=True)
        
        # Mock Experiment object (needs to have .data attribute)
        class MockExperiment:
            def __init__(self, data):
                self.data = data
        self.experiment = MockExperiment(self.full_df)
        
        self.unstained_spec = {'Sample': 'Unstained'}
        self.controls_spec = {
            'FITC-A': {'Sample': 'FITC_Control'},
            'PE-A': {'Sample': 'PE_Control'}
        }

    def test_matrix_calculation_and_application(self):
        # 1. Compute Matrix
        matrix = self.manager.compute_spillover_matrix(
            self.experiment, 
            self.unstained_spec, 
            self.controls_spec, 
            self.channels
        )
        
        print("\nComputed Spillover Matrix:")
        print(matrix)
        
        # Check FITC -> PE spillover (should be approx 0.2)
        spill_fitc_pe = matrix.loc['FITC-A', 'PE-A']
        self.assertAlmostEqual(spill_fitc_pe, 0.2, delta=0.02, msg=f"Spillover extraction failed. Got {spill_fitc_pe} expected 0.2")
        
        # Check integrity of baseline storage
        self.assertIsNotNone(self.manager.baseline, "Baseline was not stored in manager")
        base_fitc = self.manager.baseline['FITC-A']
        self.assertAlmostEqual(base_fitc, 10000, delta=200, msg="Baseline estimation incorrect")

        # 2. Apply Compensation
        # We define "correct" as:
        # - Unstained events remain centered at ~10,000 (NOT 8,000)
        # - FITC events have PE centered at ~10,000
        
        comp_df = self.manager.apply_compensation(self.full_df, self.channels, rename=True)
        
        # A. Check Unstained
        # In original, Unstained PE mean is ~10,000
        # In compensated, it MUST be ~10,000. 
        # If naive compensation happened: 10000 - (10000*0.2) = 8000.
        
        mask_u = comp_df['Sample'] == 'Unstained'
        mean_pe_unstained = comp_df.loc[mask_u, 'comp_PE-A'].mean()
        
        print(f"\nUnstained PE Mean (Compensated): {mean_pe_unstained}")
        
        # Allow small tolerance
        self.assertTrue(9800 < mean_pe_unstained < 10200, 
                        f"Unstained Baseline shifted significantly! Value: {mean_pe_unstained}. Expected ~10000.")

        # B. Check FITC Control (Compensated)
        # It should now have PE ~ 10000 (spillover removed)
        mask_f = comp_df['Sample'] == 'FITC_Control'
        mean_pe_fitc = comp_df.loc[mask_f, 'comp_PE-A'].mean()
        
        print(f"FITC Control PE Mean (Compensated): {mean_pe_fitc}")
        
        self.assertTrue(9500 < mean_pe_fitc < 10500, 
                        f"Spillover not correctly removed. Value: {mean_pe_fitc}. Expected ~10000.")

if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()

import os
import pytest
from glow.data_loading.loader import DataLoader

class TestDataLoader:
    def test_natural_sort_key(self):
        loader = DataLoader()
        unsorted = ["A10", "A1", "A2"]
        sorted_list = sorted(unsorted, key=loader._natural_sort_key)
        assert sorted_list == ["A1", "A2", "A10"]

        filename_unsorted = ["file_10.fcs", "file_2.fcs", "file_1.fcs"]
        sorted_files = sorted(filename_unsorted, key=loader._natural_sort_key)
        assert sorted_files == ["file_1.fcs", "file_2.fcs", "file_10.fcs"]

    def test_scan_directory(self, tmp_path):
        # Setup dummy directory structure
        d = tmp_path / "data_scan"
        d.mkdir()

        (d / "file_2.fcs").touch()
        (d / "file_1.fcs").touch()
        (d / "file_10.fcs").touch()
        (d / "other.txt").touch() # Should be ignored

        loader = DataLoader()
        files = loader.scan_directory(str(d))

        assert len(files) == 3
        # Check sorting
        filenames = [os.path.basename(f) for f in files]
        assert filenames == ["file_1.fcs", "file_2.fcs", "file_10.fcs"]

    def test_parse_file_paths_from_text(self, tmp_path):
        list_file = tmp_path / "list.txt"

        # Create dummy fcs files first so they exist
        (tmp_path / "A.fcs").touch()
        (tmp_path / "B.fcs").touch()

        content = f"""
{tmp_path}/B.fcs, CustomB
{tmp_path}/A.fcs
# This is a comment

"""
        list_file.write_text(content)

        loader = DataLoader()
        parsed = loader.parse_file_paths_from_text(str(list_file))

        # Should be sorted naturally by path: A then B
        assert len(parsed) == 2
        assert os.path.basename(parsed[0]['path']) == "A.fcs"
        assert parsed[0]['name'] is None

        assert os.path.basename(parsed[1]['path']) == "B.fcs"
        assert parsed[1]['name'] == "CustomB"

import os
import json
import pytest
import shutil
import numpy as np
from glow.active_learning.learner import ActiveLearner
from glow.active_learning.storage import ActiveLearningLogger

class TestActiveLearningLogger:
    def test_init_creates_dir(self, tmp_path):
        storage_dir = tmp_path / "al_data_logger"
        logger = ActiveLearningLogger(storage_dir=str(storage_dir))
        assert os.path.exists(storage_dir)

    def test_log_and_load_feedback(self, tmp_path):
        storage_dir = tmp_path / "al_data_logger_io"
        logger = ActiveLearningLogger(storage_dir=str(storage_dir))

        sample_data = {
            "sample_name": "S1",
            "cell_line": "HeLa",
            "x_channel": "FSC-A",
            "y_channel": "SSC-A",
            "gate_type": "Polygon",
            "gate_params": {'vertices': [[1,1], [2,2], [3,3]]},
            "user_decision": "accepted"
        }

        logger.log_gate_feedback(**sample_data)

        # Check file exists
        files = list(os.listdir(storage_dir))
        assert len(files) >= 1
        assert any(f.startswith("feedback_") for f in files)

        # Check load
        loaded = logger.load_feedback()
        assert len(loaded) == 1
        entry = loaded[0]
        assert entry['sample_name'] == "S1"
        assert entry['gate_params']['vertices'] == [[1,1], [2,2], [3,3]]

class TestActiveLearner:
    @pytest.fixture
    def learner_env(self, tmp_path):
        storage_dir = tmp_path / "al_data_learner"
        storage_dir.mkdir()
        return storage_dir

    def test_training_logic(self, learner_env):
        logger = ActiveLearningLogger(storage_dir=str(learner_env))

        # Log feedback for "Live" (5 vertices)
        logger.log_gate_feedback(
            sample_name="S1", cell_line="HeLa", x_channel="FSC", y_channel="SSC",
            gate_type="Polygon", gate_params={'vertices': [[0,0], [1,0], [1,1], [0,1], [0,0.5]]}, # 5 points
            user_decision="accepted"
        )

        # Log feedback for "Singlet" (4 vertices)
        logger.log_gate_feedback(
            sample_name="S1", cell_line="HeLa", x_channel="FSC-A", y_channel="FSC-H",
            gate_type="Polygon", gate_params={'vertices': [[0,0], [1,0], [1,1], [0,1]]}, # 4 points
            user_decision="accepted"
        )

        learner = ActiveLearner(storage_dir=str(learner_env), model_filename="weights.json")
        learner.train()

        assert "HeLa" in learner.knowledge_base
        assert "Live" in learner.knowledge_base["HeLa"]
        assert len(learner.knowledge_base["HeLa"]["Live"]) == 5
        assert "Singlet" in learner.knowledge_base["HeLa"]
        assert len(learner.knowledge_base["HeLa"]["Singlet"]) == 4

    def test_predict(self, learner_env):
        learner = ActiveLearner(storage_dir=str(learner_env))
        # Manually inject knowledge
        learner.knowledge_base = {
            "CHO": {
                "Live": [[1,1], [2,2]]
            }
        }

        prediction = learner.predict("CHO", "Live")
        assert prediction == [[1,1], [2,2]]

        assert learner.predict("CHO", "Singlet") is None
        assert learner.predict("Unknown", "Live") is None

    def test_save_load_weights(self, learner_env):
        learner = ActiveLearner(storage_dir=str(learner_env), model_filename="test_weights.json")
        learner.knowledge_base = {"Test": {"Gate": [1, 2, 3]}}
        learner.save_weights()

        assert (learner_env / "test_weights.json").exists()

        # New instance
        learner2 = ActiveLearner(storage_dir=str(learner_env), model_filename="test_weights.json")
        assert "Test" in learner2.knowledge_base
        assert learner2.knowledge_base["Test"]["Gate"] == [1, 2, 3]

import numpy as np
import warnings


def detect_and_scale_high_res(experiment):
    """
    Detects if the experiment data appears to be from a high-resolution cytometer
    (e.g., BioRad ZE5 with 32-bit integers) and scales it down if necessary.

    This modifies the experiment in-place.

    Args:
        experiment: The experiment object containing .data (DataFrame) and .metadata (dict).

    Returns:
        bool: True if scaling was applied, False otherwise.
    """
    if experiment is None or experiment.data is None:
        return False

    # Check if already scaled
    if hasattr(experiment, 'metadata') and experiment.metadata.get('is_scaled', False):
        return False

    # Heuristic: Check numeric columns for very large values
    # Typical 18-bit flow data maxes around 262,144.
    # 32-bit signed int max is ~2.14e9.
    # If max > 1,000,000 (10^6), flag it.
    threshold = 1000000

    numeric_cols = experiment.data.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns
    if numeric_cols.empty:
        return False

    max_vals = experiment.data[numeric_cols].max()
    is_high_res = (max_vals > threshold).any()

    # Check metadata tag if loader already flagged it but didn't scale
    if hasattr(experiment, 'metadata') and experiment.metadata.get('is_high_res', False):
        is_high_res = True

    if is_high_res:
        scaling_factor = 10000.0
        print(f"Detected high-resolution data. Applying in-place scaling (factor={scaling_factor}).")

        # Apply scaling to numeric columns (filtering out internal boolean/string cols)
        # Avoid scaling internal masks (usually boolean) or integer clusters if they are small
        # But here checking max > threshold protects against scaling small IDs.

        # Be careful not to scale channel columns that don't need it (e.g. Time?)
        # Usually Time is monotone increasing, might be large. Scaling time? Usually OK.

        # Also include other flow channels that might be low-signal but are part of the set (e.g. fluorophores).
        # It's better to scale ALL flow channels or just the ones triggered?
        # Usually, high-res applies to all detectors.
        # But we don't want to scale 'Time' if it's just seconds.
        # If Time is in ticks (large), scaling is fine.

        # Safer strategy: Scale ALL numeric columns if we trigger the mode,
        # except maybe known categorical ones (though numeric categorical is rare in raw flow).

        experiment.data[numeric_cols] = experiment.data[numeric_cols] / scaling_factor

        if experiment.metadata is None:
            experiment.metadata = {}

        experiment.metadata['is_high_res'] = True
        experiment.metadata['is_scaled'] = True
        experiment.metadata['scaling_factor'] = scaling_factor

        return True

    return False


def logicle_transform(x, cofactor=150):
    """
    Logicle-like transform using hyperbolic arcsine (asinh).

    This is a common approximation for the logicle/biexponential transform used in
    flow cytometry. It handles negative values gracefully (important for compensated data)
    and transitions smoothly from linear near zero to log-like at larger values.

    The transform is: asinh(x / cofactor) / ln(10)

    This makes the scale comparable to log10 for large positive values while
    remaining well-defined for negative values.

    Args:
        x (array-like): Input data (can include negative values).
        cofactor (float): Controls the transition point from linear to log-like behavior.
                         Typical values: 150-500 for fluorescence, 5 for scatter.
                         Smaller cofactor = earlier transition to log-like.

    Returns:
        np.ndarray: Transformed data.
    """
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    # asinh(x/c) / ln(10) makes scale comparable to log10 for large values
    return np.arcsinh(x / cofactor) / np.log(10)


def safe_log10(x, min_val=None, replace_with_nan=True):
    """
    Computes log10 safely by handling non-positive values.

    Args:
        x (array-like): Input data.
        min_val (float, optional): If provided, values <= 0 are clipped to this value before taking log.
                                   If None and replace_with_nan is True, values <= 0 become NaN.
        replace_with_nan (bool): If True and min_val is None, values <= 0 are replaced with NaN.

    Returns:
        np.ndarray: Log10 transformed data.
    """
    x = np.array(x, dtype=float)

    if min_val is not None:
        # Clip values to min_val
        x[x <= 0] = min_val
        return np.log10(x)

    if replace_with_nan:
        # Replace non-positive with NaN
        # Use where to avoid RuntimeWarning for invalid value in log10
        with warnings.catch_warnings():
            warnings.simplefilter("ignore", category=RuntimeWarning)
            # We can't easily avoid the warning without pre-filtering,
            # but masking is cleaner.
            # Actually, `np.log10` emits warnings on NaN/inf generation usually.
            mask = x <= 0
            if np.any(mask):
                x_safe = x.copy()
                x_safe[mask] = np.nan
                return np.log10(x_safe) # log10(nan) is nan, no warning?
            else:
                return np.log10(x)

    # If neither, just do it and let it warn/return -inf/nan as numpy does
    return np.log10(x)

def safe_log(x, min_val=None, replace_with_nan=True):
    """
    Computes natural log safely by handling non-positive values.

    Args:
        x (array-like): Input data.
        min_val (float, optional): If provided, values <= 0 are clipped to this value before taking log.
        replace_with_nan (bool): If True and min_val is None, values <= 0 are replaced with NaN.

    Returns:
        np.ndarray: Natural log transformed data.
    """
    x = np.array(x, dtype=float)

    if min_val is not None:
        x[x <= 0] = min_val
        return np.log(x)

    if replace_with_nan:
        mask = x <= 0
        if np.any(mask):
            x_safe = x.copy()
            x_safe[mask] = np.nan
            return np.log(x_safe)

    return np.log(x)

FILE: glow/analysis/positive.py

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from glow.gating.geometry import points_in_polygon

# Define internal gating columns to exclude from grouping (Shared with stats.py)
EXCLUDE_CONDITIONS = [
    'cells', 'singlets', 'IsSinglet', 'Is_Singlet',
    'Population', 'AutoLiveGate', 'LiveGate', 'SingletGate',
    'Is_Live', 'Live', 'Density'
]

def calculate_positive_stats(experiment, channel, threshold, suffix=""):
    """
    Returns positive % per sample for a given channel and threshold.

    Args:
        experiment: Cytoflow Experiment
        channel: Channel name (str)
        threshold: Cutoff value (float)
        suffix: Optional suffix for the result column (default: "Positive %")

    Returns:
        pd.DataFrame: ['Sample', '{Channel} Positive %']
    """
    df = experiment.data
    results = []

    # Identify sample grouping (shared logic)
    group_cols = []

    # Robust Check: attributes might raise KeyError if metadata is partial
    try:
        if hasattr(experiment, 'conditions'):
             all_conditions = list(experiment.conditions.keys())
             group_cols = [c for c in all_conditions if c not in EXCLUDE_CONDITIONS and not c.startswith('__')]
    except Exception:
        group_cols = []


    if not group_cols:
        possible_cols = ['Sample', 'sample', 'Name', 'name', 'folder_name']
        for c in possible_cols:
            if c in df.columns:
                group_cols = [c]
                break

    if group_cols:
        valid_cols = [c for c in group_cols if c in df.columns]
        if valid_cols:
            grouped = df.groupby(valid_cols, observed=True)
        else:
             grouped = [('All', df)]
    else:
        grouped = [('All', df)]

    col_name = f"{channel} Positive %" if not suffix else suffix
    count_col_name = f"{channel} Positive Count"

    for name, group in grouped:
        if isinstance(name, tuple):
            sample_name = "_".join(map(str, name))
        else:
            sample_name = str(name)

        # -----------------------------------------------------------
        # Filter for Singlet Population (Live & Singlet) if possible
        # -----------------------------------------------------------

        # 1. Identify Live Mask
        live_mask = pd.Series([True] * len(group), index=group.index)
        for cand in ['cells', 'Cells', 'Is_Live', 'AutoLiveGate', 'Live', 'LiveGate']:
            if cand in group.columns:
                col = group[cand]
                if pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(col):
                    live_mask = col
                else:
                    live_mask = (pd.to_numeric(col, errors='coerce') >= 0)
                break

        # 2. Identify Singlet Mask
        singlet_mask = pd.Series([True] * len(group), index=group.index)
        for cand in ['singlets', 'Singlets', 'Is_Singlet', 'Singlet', 'LearnedSinglet']:
            if cand in group.columns:
                col = group[cand]
                if pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(col):
                    singlet_mask = col
                else:
                    singlet_mask = (pd.to_numeric(col, errors='coerce') >= 0)
                break

        # Combined Filter
        valid_population_mask = live_mask & singlet_mask

        # Filter Data
        filtered_group = group[valid_population_mask]

        pos_count = 0
        pct = 0.0

        if len(filtered_group) > 0:
            vals = filtered_group[channel]
            count = len(vals)
            # Assuming Positive is > threshold
            # Robust check for Nans? Arrays should be numeric.
            pos_count = (vals > threshold).sum()
            pct = (pos_count / count * 100)

        results.append({
            'Sample': sample_name,
            count_col_name: int(pos_count),
            col_name: round(pct, 2)
        })

    return pd.DataFrame(results)

def get_recursive_mask(df, parent_name, defined_gates):
    """
    Recursively computes the boolean mask for a given parent population.

    Args:
        df: The DataFrame containing the data.
        parent_name: The name of the parent population (e.g., "Singlets", "GFP+").
        defined_gates: List of dicts describing existing gates.
                       [{'label': 'GFP+', 'channel': 'GFP-A', 'threshold': 100, 'parent': 'Singlets'}, ...]

    Returns:
        pd.Series (bool): The mask for the parent population.
    """
    # 0. Base Case: Singlets / Cells (Root)
    # We treat 'Singlets' or 'root' as the base filtered population (Live + Singlet)
    if parent_name in ['Singlets', 'root', 'None', None]:
        # Copied logic for base mask
        live_mask = pd.Series([True] * len(df), index=df.index)
        for cand in ['cells', 'Cells', 'Is_Live', 'AutoLiveGate']:
             if cand in df.columns:
                col = df[cand]
                if pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(col):
                    live_mask = col
                else:
                    live_mask = (pd.to_numeric(col, errors='coerce') >= 0)
                break

        singlet_mask = pd.Series([True] * len(df), index=df.index)
        for cand in ['singlets', 'Singlets', 'Is_Singlet']:
            if cand in df.columns:
                col = df[cand]
                if pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(col):
                    singlet_mask = col
                else:
                    singlet_mask = (pd.to_numeric(col, errors='coerce') >= 0)
                break
        return live_mask & singlet_mask

    # 1. Recursive Case: Find the gate definition
    target_gate = next((g for g in defined_gates if g.get('label') == parent_name), None)

    if not target_gate:
        # If unknown parent, fallback to base
        return get_recursive_mask(df, 'Singlets', [])

    # 2. Get the mask of the grandparent
    grandparent_mask = get_recursive_mask(df, target_gate.get('parent', 'Singlets'), defined_gates)

    # 3. Apply the current gate's threshold
    # Handle Range, Binary, or Quadrant
    if target_gate.get('type') == 'range':
        channel = target_gate['channel']
        min_val = target_gate.get('min', -np.inf)
        max_val = target_gate.get('max', np.inf)
        current_gate_mask = (df[channel] >= min_val) & (df[channel] <= max_val)
    elif target_gate.get('type') == 'quadrant':
        # Quadrant Gate Logic
        ch_x = target_gate['channel_x']
        ch_y = target_gate['channel_y']
        th_x = float(target_gate['threshold_x'])
        th_y = float(target_gate['threshold_y'])
        quads = target_gate['quadrant']
        if isinstance(quads, str):
            quads = [quads]

        combined_mask = pd.Series([False] * len(df), index=df.index)
        for quad in quads:
            if quad[0] == '+':
                mx = (df[ch_x] > th_x)
            else:
                mx = (df[ch_x] <= th_x)
            if quad[1] == '+':
                my = (df[ch_y] > th_y)
            else:
                my = (df[ch_y] <= th_y)
            combined_mask |= (mx & my)
        current_gate_mask = combined_mask

    elif target_gate.get('type') == 'polygon':
        ch_x = target_gate['channel_x']
        ch_y = target_gate['channel_y']
        verts = target_gate['vertices']
        # points_in_polygon expects (N, 2) array
        # Ensure correct order: X, Y
        points = df[[ch_x, ch_y]].values
        # Mask is boolean array
        current_gate_mask = points_in_polygon(points, verts)

    elif target_gate.get('type') == 'polygon':
        ch_x = target_gate['channel_x']
        ch_y = target_gate['channel_y']
        verts = target_gate['vertices']
        # points_in_polygon expects (N, 2) array
        # Ensure correct order: X, Y
        points = df[[ch_x, ch_y]].values
        # Mask is boolean array
        current_gate_mask = points_in_polygon(points, verts)

    else:
        # Backward compatibility or standard binary
        channel = target_gate['channel']
        threshold = target_gate['threshold']
        if target_gate.get('direction') == 'negative':
            current_gate_mask = (df[channel] <= threshold)
        else:
            current_gate_mask = (df[channel] > threshold)

    return grandparent_mask & current_gate_mask

def calculate_positive_stats_recursive(experiment, gate, defined_gates):
    """
    Calculates stats for a specific gate, respecting its hierarchy.

    Args:
        experiment: Cytoflow Experiment
        gate: The gate dict to calculate stats for (must include 'label', 'channel', 'threshold', 'parent')
        defined_gates: List of all defined gates (context for recursion)
    """
    df = experiment.data
    results = []

    if gate.get('type') == 'quadrant':
        channel = f"{gate['channel_x']}:{gate['channel_y']}"
    elif gate.get('type') == 'polygon':
        channel = f"{gate['channel_x']}:{gate['channel_y']}"
    elif gate.get('type') == 'polygon':
        channel = f"{gate['channel_x']}:{gate['channel_y']}"
    else:
        channel = gate['channel']

    # threshold = gate['threshold'] # access locally inside loop or use getter
    parent_name = gate.get('parent', 'Singlets')
    label = gate.get('label', f"{channel}+")

    # Grouping Logic
    group_cols = []
    try:
        if hasattr(experiment, 'conditions'):
             all_conditions = list(experiment.conditions.keys())
             group_cols = [c for c in all_conditions if c not in EXCLUDE_CONDITIONS and not c.startswith('__')]
    except Exception:
        group_cols = []

    if not group_cols:
        possible_cols = ['Sample', 'sample', 'Name', 'name']
        for c in possible_cols:
            if c in df.columns:
                group_cols = [c]
                break

    if group_cols:
        valid_cols = [c for c in group_cols if c in df.columns]
        if valid_cols:
             grouped = df.groupby(valid_cols, observed=True)
        else:
             grouped = [('All', df)]
    else:
        grouped = [('All', df)]

    col_name = f"{label} %"
    count_col_name = f"{label} Count"

    for name, group in grouped:
        if isinstance(name, tuple):
            sample_name = "_".join(map(str, name))
        else:
            sample_name = str(name)

        # 1. Get the Parent Population Mask for this specific group chunk
        # Note: recursion usually runs on full DF, here we run on group.
        # It's fine as long as logic is row-independent.
        parent_mask = get_recursive_mask(group, parent_name, defined_gates)

        # 2. Filter
        parent_population = group[parent_mask]
        parent_count = len(parent_population)

        pos_count = 0
        pct = 0.0

        if parent_count > 0:
            # 3. Apply This Gate
            if gate.get('type') == 'quadrant':
                 # Re-implement quadrant logic on the parent_population
                 ch_x = gate['channel_x']
                 ch_y = gate['channel_y']
                 vals_x = parent_population[ch_x]
                 vals_y = parent_population[ch_y]

                 th_x = float(gate['threshold_x'])
                 th_y = float(gate['threshold_y'])
                 quads = gate['quadrant']
                 if isinstance(quads, str):
                     quads = [quads]

                 combined_mask = pd.Series([False] * len(parent_population), index=parent_population.index)
                 for quad in quads:
                     if quad[0] == '+':
                         mx = (vals_x > th_x)
                     else:
                         mx = (vals_x <= th_x)
                     if quad[1] == '+':
                         my = (vals_y > th_y)
                     else:
                         my = (vals_y <= th_y)
                     combined_mask |= (mx & my)
                 pos_count = combined_mask.sum()

            elif gate.get('type') == 'polygon':
                 ch_x = gate['channel_x']
                 ch_y = gate['channel_y']
                 verts = gate['vertices']

                 # Prepare points for PARENT population
                 points = parent_population[[ch_x, ch_y]].values

                 mask = points_in_polygon(points, verts)
                 pos_count = mask.sum()

            elif gate.get('type') == 'polygon':
                 ch_x = gate['channel_x']
                 ch_y = gate['channel_y']
                 verts = gate['vertices']

                 # Prepare points for PARENT population
                 points = parent_population[[ch_x, ch_y]].values

                 mask = points_in_polygon(points, verts)
                 pos_count = mask.sum()

            elif gate.get('type') == 'range':
                 channel = gate['channel']
                 vals = parent_population[channel]
                 min_val = gate.get('min', -np.inf)
                 max_val = gate.get('max', np.inf)
                 pos_count = ((vals >= min_val) & (vals <= max_val)).sum()
            else:
                 channel = gate['channel']
                 vals = parent_population[channel]
                 threshold = gate['threshold']
                 if gate.get('direction') == 'negative':
                     pos_count = (vals <= threshold).sum()
                 else:
                     pos_count = (vals > threshold).sum()

            pct = (pos_count / parent_count * 100) # Percentage of PARENT? Or Total?
            # Standard flow convention: usually % of Parent.
            # Let's stick to % of Parent as it's "Sub-gating".
            # If user wants % of Total, that's different. usually "% Parent" is default.

        results.append({
            'Sample': sample_name,
            count_col_name: int(pos_count),
            col_name: round(pct, 2)
        })

    return pd.DataFrame(results)

def get_channel_data_for_samples(experiment, channel, sample_names, parent_gate_name=None, defined_gates=None, apply_scaling=False):
    """
    Retrieve data vectors for the specified channel, filtered by sample names AND parent gating.
    If apply_scaling is True and experiment metadata indicates high-res data, values are scaled down.
    """
    df = experiment.data
    data_map = {}

    # Scaling Logic
    scaling_factor = 1.0
    if apply_scaling and hasattr(experiment, 'metadata') and experiment.metadata.get('is_high_res', False):
        scaling_factor = experiment.metadata.get('scaling_factor', 10000.0)

    # ----------------------------------------------------
    # Apply Parent Gate Filtering Globally (Optimization?)
    # ----------------------------------------------------
    # It might be faster to compute mask once for full DF, then group.

    full_mask = pd.Series([True] * len(df), index=df.index)
    if parent_gate_name and parent_gate_name != 'Singlets':
        if defined_gates is None:
            defined_gates = []
        full_mask = get_recursive_mask(df, parent_gate_name, defined_gates)
    else:
        # Default match logic for Singlets
        full_mask = get_recursive_mask(df, 'Singlets', [])

    filtered_df = df[full_mask]

    # Grouping Logic reuse
    group_cols = []
    try:
        if hasattr(experiment, 'conditions'):
             all_conditions = list(experiment.conditions.keys())
             group_cols = [c for c in all_conditions if c not in EXCLUDE_CONDITIONS and not c.startswith('__')]
    except Exception:
        pass
    if not group_cols:
        for c in ['Sample', 'sample', 'Name', 'name']:
            if c in df.columns:
                group_cols = [c]
                break

    if group_cols:
        valid_cols = [c for c in group_cols if c in df.columns]
        if valid_cols:
             grouped = filtered_df.groupby(valid_cols, observed=True)
        else:
             grouped = [('All', filtered_df)]
    else:
        grouped = [('All', filtered_df)]

    for name, group in grouped:
        if isinstance(name, tuple):
            s_name = "_".join(map(str, name))
        else:
            s_name = str(name)

        if s_name in sample_names:
            vals = group[channel].values
            if scaling_factor != 1.0:
                 # Ensure float for division
                 vals = vals.astype(float) / scaling_factor
            data_map[s_name] = vals

    return data_map


==================================================
FILE: glow/analysis/stats.py

import pandas as pd

def get_summary_statistics(experiment):
    """
    Returns a DataFrame with summary statistics for the experiment.
    Include: Sample Name, Total Events, Live %, Singlets %.

    Args:
        experiment: Cytoflow Experiment object.

    Returns:
        pd.DataFrame
    """
    if experiment is None:
        return pd.DataFrame()

    results = []
    data = experiment.data

    # Identify sample grouping column
    group_cols = []

    # Define internal gating columns to exclude from grouping
    # These are added as 'conditions' for metadata validity but should not split the sample summary
    EXCLUDE_CONDITIONS = [
        'cells', 'singlets', 'IsSinglet', 'Is_Singlet',
        'Population', 'AutoLiveGate', 'LiveGate', 'SingletGate',
        'Is_Live', 'Live', 'Density'
    ]

    try:
        # Cytoflow property access can trigger lazy evaluation looking up cached keys in metadata
        # If metadata[x] missing for a key it thinks is a condition, it crashes.
        if hasattr(experiment, 'conditions'):
            all_conditions = list(experiment.conditions.keys())
            # Filter out excluded conditions
            group_cols = [
                c for c in all_conditions
                if c not in EXCLUDE_CONDITIONS
                and not c.startswith('__') # exclude internal python attrs if any
            ]
    except Exception:
        # Fallback if Cytoflow metadata is corrupted or mismatching data columns
        pass

    # If no conditions found in experiment metadata, check columns for typical names
    if not group_cols:
        possible_cols = ['Sample', 'sample', 'Name', 'name', 'folder_name']
        for c in possible_cols:
            if c in data.columns:
                group_cols = [c]
                break

    if group_cols:
        # Check if columns actually exist in data to avoid KeyError
        valid_group_cols = [c for c in group_cols if c in data.columns]
        if valid_group_cols:
            grouped = data.groupby(valid_group_cols, observed=True)
        else:
             grouped = [('All', data)]
    else:
        # specific handling if no grouping (single file?)
        grouped = [('All', data)]

    for name, group in grouped:
        # Format sample name
        if isinstance(name, tuple):
            sample_name = "_".join(map(str, name))
        else:
            sample_name = str(name)

        total_events = len(group)

        # --- Resolve LIVE Column ---
        # Candidates for Live/Dead flag
        # Order: 'cells' (CSV standard), 'Is_Live', 'AutoLiveGate', 'Live'
        live_col_name = None
        for cand in ['cells', 'Cells', 'Is_Live', 'AutoLiveGate', 'Live', 'LiveGate']:
            if cand in group.columns:
                live_col_name = cand
                break

        # Calculate Live Mask
        if live_col_name:
            col = group[live_col_name]
            if pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(col):
                live_mask = col
            else:
                # Numeric (GMM/DBSCAN): Assume >= 0 is "Kept"
                vals = pd.to_numeric(col, errors='coerce')
                live_mask = (vals >= 0)
        else:
            # No gate found -> Assume all live? Or 0?
            # Typically 0 implies missing data or failed gating.
            live_mask = pd.Series([False] * total_events, index=group.index)

        live_count = live_mask.sum()

        # --- Resolve SINGLETS Column ---
        # Candidates for Singlet flag
        # Order: 'singlets', 'Singlets', 'Is_Singlet', 'Singlet', 'LearnedSinglet'
        singlet_col_name = None
        for cand in ['singlets', 'Singlets', 'Is_Singlet', 'IsSinglet', 'Singlet', 'LearnedSinglet']:
            if cand in group.columns:
                singlet_col_name = cand
                break

        # Calculate Singlet Mask (Subset of Live)
        # Requirement: "singlets percentage should be further determined among cells=TRUE"
        if singlet_col_name:
            col = group[singlet_col_name]
            if pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(col):
                raw_singlet_mask = col
            else:
                vals = pd.to_numeric(col, errors='coerce')
                raw_singlet_mask = (vals >= 0)

            # Intersection: Must be Live AND Singlet
            # This ensures we count the subset.
            final_singlet_mask = live_mask & raw_singlet_mask
            singlet_count = final_singlet_mask.sum()
        else:
            singlet_count = 0

        # Calculate percentages
        # Live %: Fraction of Total
        live_pct = (live_count / total_events * 100) if total_events > 0 else 0

        # Singlets %: Fraction of Live Population (per user instruction)
        singlet_pct = (singlet_count / live_count * 100) if live_count > 0 else 0

        row = {
            'Sample': sample_name,
            'Total Events': total_events,
            'Live Count': live_count,
            'Live %': round(live_pct, 2),
            'Singlets Count': singlet_count,
            'Singlets %': round(singlet_pct, 2)
        }
        results.append(row)

    return pd.DataFrame(results)

from .loader import DataLoader
