import cytoflow
import os
import shutil
import pandas as pd
import re
import zipfile
import pyarrow.parquet as pq
import numpy as np

class ParquetBatchLoader:
    def __init__(self, file_path, batch_size=100000, columns=None, row_group_indices=None):
        self.file_path = file_path
        self.batch_size = batch_size
        self.columns = columns
        self.parquet_file = pq.ParquetFile(file_path)
        self.num_row_groups = self.parquet_file.num_row_groups
        self.total_rows = self.parquet_file.metadata.num_rows

        if row_group_indices is not None:
            self.row_group_indices = row_group_indices
        else:
            self.row_group_indices = range(self.num_row_groups)

    def __iter__(self):
        # Iterate over row groups.
        # Note: A single row group might be larger or smaller than batch_size.
        # For strict memory control, we could read row groups and yield chunks.
        # But iteratively reading row groups is the standard zero-copy(ish) way.
        for i in self.row_group_indices:
            table = self.parquet_file.read_row_group(i, columns=self.columns)
            yield table

class DataLoader:
    def __init__(self):
        """
        Initialize the DataLoader.
        It can hold references to loaded files.
        """
        self.loaded_files_metadata = []

    @staticmethod
    def sanitize_key(name):
        import re
        import numpy as np
        # Replace invalid chars with underscore
        clean = re.sub(r'[^0-9a-zA-Z_]', '_', str(name))
        # Cannot start with number
        if clean and clean[0].isdigit():
            clean = "_" + clean
        # Ensure not empty
        if not clean:
            clean = "condition_" + str(np.random.randint(1000))
        return clean

    def _natural_sort_key(self, text):
        return [int(c) if c.isdigit() else c.lower() for c in re.split('([0-9]+)', str(text))]

    def save_uploaded_files(self, uploaded_files, target_dir="data/uploaded_raw", folder_name=None):
        if folder_name:
            folder_name = "".join(x for x in folder_name if (x.isalnum() or x in "._-"))
            target_dir = os.path.join(target_dir, folder_name)

        if not os.path.exists(target_dir):
            os.makedirs(target_dir, exist_ok=True)

        saved_paths = []
        for uploaded_file in uploaded_files:
            file_path = os.path.join(target_dir, uploaded_file.name)
            with open(file_path, "wb") as f:
                f.write(uploaded_file.getbuffer())
            saved_paths.append(os.path.abspath(file_path))

        saved_paths.sort(key=self._natural_sort_key)
        return saved_paths

    def save_uploaded_folder_zip(self, uploaded_zip, target_dir="data/uploaded_raw_zip"):
        if os.path.exists(target_dir):
            shutil.rmtree(target_dir) # Clean up previous uploads
        os.makedirs(target_dir, exist_ok=True)

        try:
            with zipfile.ZipFile(uploaded_zip, 'r') as zip_ref:
                zip_ref.extractall(target_dir)
        except zipfile.BadZipFile:
            print("Error: Invalid zip file")
            return []

        saved_paths = []
        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(target_dir):
            for file in files:
                if file.lower().endswith('.fcs'):
                    saved_paths.append(os.path.abspath(os.path.join(root, file)))

        saved_paths.sort(key=self._natural_sort_key)
        return saved_paths

    def scan_directory(self, directory_path):
        paths = []
        if not os.path.isdir(directory_path):
            print(f"Directory not found: {directory_path}")
            return paths

        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(directory_path):
            for file in files:
                if file.lower().endswith('.fcs'):
                    full_path = os.path.join(root, file)
                    paths.append(full_path)

        paths.sort(key=self._natural_sort_key)
        return paths

    @staticmethod
    def clear_upload_cache(target_dir="data/uploaded_raw"):
        """
        Clears the upload cache directory to free up space.
        """
        if os.path.exists(target_dir):
            try:
                shutil.rmtree(target_dir)
                os.makedirs(target_dir, exist_ok=True)
                print(f"Cache cleared: {target_dir}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Failed to clear cache {target_dir}: {e}")

    def parse_file_paths_from_text(self, file_path_list_file):
        paths = []
        if not os.path.exists(file_path_list_file):
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"File list not found: {file_path_list_file}")

        with open(file_path_list_file, 'r') as f:
            for line in f:
                line = line.strip()
                if line and not line.startswith("#"):
                    parts = line.split(',')
                    path = parts[0].strip()
                    custom_name = parts[1].strip() if len(parts) > 1 else None

                    if os.path.exists(path):
                        paths.append({'path': path, 'name': custom_name})
                    else:
                        print(f"Warning: File not found at {path}")

        paths.sort(key=lambda x: self._natural_sort_key(x['path']))

        return paths

    def generate_preview_metadata(self, paths, rename_strategy='folder_name'):
        preview_metadata = []
        for item in paths:
            if isinstance(item, dict):
                path = item['path']
                custom_name = item.get('name')
            else:
                path = str(item)
                custom_name = None

            if not os.path.exists(path):
                continue

            dirname = os.path.basename(os.path.dirname(path))
            filename = os.path.basename(path)

            if not filename.lower().endswith('.fcs'):
                continue

            if custom_name:
                new_name = custom_name
                if not new_name.lower().endswith('.fcs'):
                    new_name += '.fcs'
            elif rename_strategy == 'folder_name':
                name_prefix = dirname.replace(" ", "_")
                new_name = f"{name_prefix}_{filename}"
            else:
                new_name = filename

            file_meta = {
                'original_path': path,
                'display_name': new_name,
                'condition': dirname
            }
            preview_metadata.append(file_meta)
        return preview_metadata

    def process_files(self, metadata_list, output_dir=None):
        self.loaded_files_metadata = []

        if output_dir and not os.path.exists(output_dir):
            os.makedirs(output_dir)

        internal_keys = ['original_path', 'current_path', 'path', 'display_name', 'condition']

        for meta in metadata_list:
            original = meta['original_path']
            new_name = meta['display_name']

            final_path = original

            if output_dir:
                dest_path = os.path.join(output_dir, new_name)
                try:
                    shutil.copy2(original, dest_path)
                    final_path = dest_path
                except Exception as e:
                    print(f"Error copying {original}: {e}")

            # Sanitize metadata keys immediately upon loading/processing
            sanitized_meta = {}
            for k, v in meta.items():
                if k in internal_keys:
                    sanitized_meta[k] = v
                else:
                    clean_k = self.sanitize_key(k)
                    sanitized_meta[clean_k] = v

            sanitized_meta['current_path'] = final_path
            self.loaded_files_metadata.append(sanitized_meta)

        return self.loaded_files_metadata

    def prepare_fcs_files(self, paths, rename_strategy='folder_name', output_dir=None):
        meta = self.generate_preview_metadata(paths, rename_strategy)
        return self.process_files(meta, output_dir)

    def _detect_and_tag_high_res(self, experiment):
        """
        Detects if the experiment data appears to be from a high-resolution cytometer
        (e.g., BioRad ZE5 with 32-bit integers) and tags metadata accordingly.
        """
        if experiment is None or experiment.data is None:
            return experiment
        
        # Heuristic: Check numeric columns for very large values
        # Typical 18-bit flow data maxes around 262,144.
        # 32-bit signed int max is ~2.14e9.
        # If max > 1,000,000 (10^6), flag it.
        threshold = 1000000 
        
        numeric_cols = experiment.data.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns
        if numeric_cols.empty:
            return experiment

        max_vals = experiment.data[numeric_cols].max()
        if (max_vals > threshold).any():
            print("Detected high-resolution data. Tagging metadata.")
            new_meta = experiment.metadata.copy() if experiment.metadata else {}
            new_meta['is_high_res'] = True
            new_meta['scaling_factor'] = 10000.0
            experiment.metadata = new_meta
            
        return experiment

    def load_as_cytoflow_experiment(self, metadata_list=None):
        if metadata_list is None:
            metadata_list = self.loaded_files_metadata

        if not metadata_list:
            print("No files to load.")
            return None

        internal_keys = ['original_path', 'current_path', 'path', 'display_name', 'condition']

        all_condition_keys = set()
        all_condition_keys.add('Sample')
        all_condition_keys.add('Condition')

        # Collect all unique keys (sanitized)
        for meta in metadata_list:
             for key in meta.keys():
                 if key not in internal_keys:
                     clean_key = self.sanitize_key(key)
                     all_condition_keys.add(clean_key)

        print(f"DEBUG: All Condition Keys: {all_condition_keys}")

        import_conditions_schema = {}
        for key in all_condition_keys:
            import_conditions_schema[key] = "category"

        tubes = []
        for meta in metadata_list:
            tube_conditions = {}
            # Always ensure Sample and Condition are present
            tube_conditions['Sample'] = str(meta.get('display_name', 'Unknown'))

            if 'condition' in meta and meta['condition']:
                tube_conditions['Condition'] = str(meta['condition'])
            else:
                 tube_conditions['Condition'] = "Unspecified"

            # Populate other conditions
            for orig_key, val in meta.items():
                if orig_key in internal_keys:
                    continue

                clean_key = self.sanitize_key(orig_key)
                if clean_key in ['Sample', 'Condition']:
                    continue

                if val is not None and str(val).strip() != "":
                    tube_conditions[clean_key] = str(val)

            # Fill missing keys with NA
            for key in import_conditions_schema.keys():
                if key not in tube_conditions:
                    tube_conditions[key] = "NA"

            try:
                tube = cytoflow.Tube(file=meta['current_path'], conditions=tube_conditions)
                tubes.append(tube)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Failed to create Tube for {meta.get('current_path')}: {e}")

        # Modified Loading Logic: Handle disjoint channels by unioning per-tube Experiments
        if tubes:
            # Attempt standard import first (Optimized)
            try:
                import_op = cytoflow.ImportOp(tubes=tubes, conditions=import_conditions_schema)
                experiment = import_op.apply()
                return self._detect_and_tag_high_res(experiment)
            except Exception as e:
                # Check for channel mismatch error or generic CytoflowOpError
                msg = str(e)
                if "doesn't have the same channels" in msg or "CytoflowOpError" in str(type(e)):
                    print(f"Standard import failed ({msg}). Attempting manual channel union...")

                    # Manual Union Strategy
                    loaded_dfs = []
                    combined_metadata = {}

                    for tube in tubes:
                        try:
                            # Import single tube
                            op = cytoflow.ImportOp(tubes=[tube], conditions=import_conditions_schema)
                            ex = op.apply()
                            loaded_dfs.append(ex.data)

                            # Merge Metadata
                            # Cytoflow requires metadata for all columns (channels & conditions)
                            # We accumulate unique metadata entries.
                            for k, v in ex.metadata.items():
                                if k not in combined_metadata:
                                    # Copy to avoid reference issues
                                    combined_metadata[k] = v.copy() if isinstance(v, dict) else v
                                else:
                                    # If it exists, merge for conditions
                                    existing = combined_metadata[k]
                                    if isinstance(existing, dict) and existing.get('type') == 'condition':
                                        current_vals = set(existing.get('values', []))
                                        new_vals = v.get('values', [])
                                        # Update values list with new seen values
                                        updated_vals = list(current_vals.union(new_vals))
                                        updated_vals.sort()
                                        existing['values'] = updated_vals
                                    # For channels, we assume metadata (voltage etc) is acceptable from the first source

                        except Exception as e_inner:
                            print(f"Error loading individual tube {tube.file}: {e_inner}")

                    if not loaded_dfs:
                        return None

                    # Concatenate all (Columns will be unioned, missing values -> NaN)
                    combined_df = pd.concat(loaded_dfs, axis=0, ignore_index=True, sort=False)

                    # Fill missing numeric channels with 0.0
                    numeric_cols = combined_df.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns
                    combined_df[numeric_cols] = combined_df[numeric_cols].fillna(0.0)

                    # Create unified experiment manually
                    try:
                        unified_experiment = cytoflow.Experiment()
                        unified_experiment.data = combined_df
                        unified_experiment.metadata = combined_metadata
                        return self._detect_and_tag_high_res(unified_experiment)
                    except Exception as e_final:
                         print(f"Failed to create unified experiment: {e_final}")
                         return None
                else:
                    # Propagate other errors
                    raise e
        else:
            return None


==================================================
FILE: glow/data_loading/metadata_parser.py

import pandas as pd

def parse_metadata_file(uploaded_file, header_row_idx, data_start_idx, global_exp_name_coords=None, df_raw=None):
    """
    Parses a CSV or Excel file into a DataFrame.

    Args:
        uploaded_file: The file object (from st.file_uploader or open()). Used if df_raw is None.
        header_row_idx: Index of the row containing headers.
        data_start_idx: Index of the row where data starts.
        global_exp_name_coords: Optional tuple (row, col) to extract experiment name.
        df_raw: Optional pre-loaded DataFrame (raw with no header).

    Returns:
        pd.DataFrame: The parsed metadata.
    """
    # 1. Read Raw
    if df_raw is None:
        if hasattr(uploaded_file, 'name') and uploaded_file.name.lower().endswith('.csv'):
            df_raw = pd.read_csv(uploaded_file, header=None)
        else:
            # Assumes Excel if not CSV, or let pandas handle it
            df_raw = pd.read_excel(uploaded_file, header=None)


    # 2. Extract Global Experiment Name if requested
    global_exp_name = None
    if global_exp_name_coords:
        g_row, g_col = global_exp_name_coords
        try:
            val = df_raw.iloc[g_row, g_col]
            if not pd.isna(val) and str(val).strip():
                global_exp_name = str(val)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # 3. Headers
    if header_row_idx >= len(df_raw):
         raise ValueError("Header row index out of bounds")

    header_series = df_raw.iloc[header_row_idx]
    headers = []
    for i, val in enumerate(header_series):
        if pd.isna(val) or str(val).strip() == "":
            headers.append(f"Untitled_{i}")
        else:
            headers.append(str(val))

    # Duplicate headers handling
    seen = {}
    final_headers = []
    for h in headers:
        if h in seen:
            seen[h] += 1
            final_headers.append(f"{h}_{seen[h]}")
        else:
            seen[h] = 0
            final_headers.append(h)

    # 4. Data Slice
    df_parsed = df_raw.iloc[data_start_idx:].copy()

    # 5. Assign Headers
    if len(df_parsed.columns) == len(final_headers):
        df_parsed.columns = final_headers

    df_parsed.reset_index(drop=True, inplace=True)

    # 6. Apply Global Experiment Name
    if global_exp_name:
        # Determine if we overwrite or fillna.
        # For simplicity, if we extracted it globally, we probably want to apply it globally.
        df_parsed["Experiment_Name"] = global_exp_name

    return df_parsed

==================================================
FILE: glow/compensation/manager.py

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import logging

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class CompensationManager:
    def __init__(self):
        self.spillover_matrix = None
        self.channels = []
        self.baseline = None # Store baseline (autofluorescence/offset) for correction

    def compute_spillover_matrix(self, experiment, unstained_spec, controls_spec, channels):
        """
        Compute pairwise spillover using standard Median Ratio method.
        Robustly determines Positive and Negative populations for slope calculation.
        """
        # Initialize identity matrix
        matrix = pd.DataFrame(np.eye(len(channels)), index=channels, columns=channels)

        # Retrieve Unstained Reference (if available) as a fallback negative
        unstained_medians = None
        if unstained_spec:
            try:
                unstained_data = self._get_data_subset(experiment, unstained_spec)
                if unstained_data is not None and len(unstained_data) > 100:
                    unstained_medians = unstained_data[channels].median()
            except Exception:
                logger.warning("Could not calculate background from unstained control.")

        # Baseline is NOT stored or used for correction in standard mode 
        # to strictly follow standard compensation math (Raw * InvMatrix).
        self.baseline = None 

        for from_channel in channels:
            if from_channel not in controls_spec:
                continue

            # Get Control Data
            spec = controls_spec[from_channel]
            tube_data = self._get_data_subset(experiment, spec)

            if tube_data is None or len(tube_data) < 100:
                logger.warning(f"Control for {from_channel} missing/small. Skipping.")
                continue

            # Work on a copy
            tube_data = tube_data.copy()

            # METHOD: Standard Median Ratio
            # Slope = (Pos_Signal - Neg_Signal) / (Pos_Primary - Neg_Primary)
            
            # 1. Determine Negative Reference
            # Priority: Internal Negative (bottom 10%) > Unstained Control
            q_lo = tube_data[from_channel].quantile(0.10)
            neg_subset = tube_data[tube_data[from_channel] <= q_lo]
            
            neg_medians = None
            if len(neg_subset) > 50:
                # Use internal negative if sufficient events exist
                neg_medians = neg_subset[channels].median()
            elif unstained_medians is not None:
                # Fallback to unstained if tube is "all positive" (e.g. bright beads)
                neg_medians = unstained_medians
            else:
                 # Last resort: minimums
                 neg_medians = tube_data[channels].min()

            # 2. Determine Positive Population
            # Top 2% (robust max)
            q_hi = tube_data[from_channel].quantile(0.98)
            pos_subset = tube_data[tube_data[from_channel] >= q_hi]

            if len(pos_subset) < 10:
                logger.warning(f"Not enough positive events in control {from_channel}")
                continue

            pos_medians = pos_subset[channels].median()

            # 3. Calculate Slopes
            # Delta = Pos - Neg
            delta_primary = pos_medians[from_channel] - neg_medians[from_channel]

            if delta_primary <= 1.0:
                 # Primary signal too weak or invalid
                 continue

            for to_channel in channels:
                if from_channel == to_channel:
                    matrix.loc[from_channel, to_channel] = 1.0
                    continue

                delta_secondary = pos_medians[to_channel] - neg_medians[to_channel]
                
                # Standard compensation calculates the ratio of the fluorescence in the detector channel
                # to the fluorescence in the primary channel.
                # If delta_secondary is negative, it implies the positive population is dimmer than 
                # the negative/unstained in this channel, which is physically impossible for spillover 
                # (unless noise). We clamp to 0.
                if delta_secondary < 0:
                    delta_secondary = 0

                slope = delta_secondary / delta_primary
                matrix.loc[from_channel, to_channel] = slope

        self.spillover_matrix = matrix
        self.channels = channels
        return matrix

    def get_compensation_matrix(self):
        """
        Invert the spillover matrix to get the compensation matrix.
        """
        if self.spillover_matrix is None:
            return None
        try:
            # Linear Algebra inverse
            comp_matrix = pd.DataFrame(
                np.linalg.inv(self.spillover_matrix.values),
                index=self.spillover_matrix.index,
                columns=self.spillover_matrix.columns
            )
            return comp_matrix
        except np.linalg.LinAlgError:
            logger.error("Singular matrix - cannot compensate.")
            return None

    def apply_compensation(self, df, channels, rename=True):
        """
        Apply compensation to dataframe using valid standard matrix multiplication.
        Assumes data is already properly scaled.
        """
        comp_matrix = self.get_compensation_matrix()
        if comp_matrix is None:
            return df

        # Only extract selected channels
        raw_data = df[channels].values
        
        # Standard Compensation: Data * Inverse(Spillover)
        # Any negative values resulting from Matrix Inverse applied to data with offsets
        # are mathematically correct in the context of compensation algebra.
        # Visualization should handle them (e.g. Biexponential/Logicle).
        compensated_data = raw_data.dot(comp_matrix.values)

        comp_df = df.copy()
        for i, ch in enumerate(channels):
            # Use 'comp_' prefix by default (snake_case)
            # Renaming to 'Comp-' is handled by the UI Analysis tab
            new_col = f"comp_{ch}" if rename else ch
            comp_df[new_col] = compensated_data[:, i]

        return comp_df

    def _get_data_subset(self, experiment, spec):
        """
        Helper to retrieve data for a specific sample/control.
        """
        if 'Sample' not in spec:
            return None

        sample_name = spec['Sample']
        if experiment is None or experiment.data is None:
            return None

        # Filter dataframe by Sample column
        if 'Sample' in experiment.data.columns:
            return experiment.data[experiment.data['Sample'] == sample_name]

        return None

        """
        Extract dataframe subset matching the spec.
        Spec example: {'Composite Name': 'Tube_001.fcs'}
        """
        # Assumption: Glow experiment data is a pandas DF in experiment.data
        # And metadata columns identify the samples.
        # Usually metadata keys like 'name' or 'filename' are columns in the big dataframe
        # OR they are handled via MultiIndex.
        # Glow's current loader merges everything.

        df = experiment.data
        mask = pd.Series(True, index=df.index)

        for key, value in spec.items():
            if key in df.columns:
                mask = mask & (df[key] == value)
            elif key in experiment.metadata:
                # If it's experiment-wide metadata, it might not differentiate rows unless split
                pass

        subset = df[mask]
        if subset.empty:
            # Try partial matching on string columns if exact match failed?
            # For now, simplistic.
            return None
        return subset

==================================================
FILE: glow/gating/boundary.py
==================================================
import numpy as np
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
from scipy.spatial import ConvexHull

def compute_density_boundary(points, grid_size=50, percentile=95, smoothing=2.0):
    """
    Computes a boundary around the high-density region of the points.
    Returns the Convex Hull of the density contour polygon to ensure a simple single polygon result.

    Args:
        points: np.ndarray (N, 2)
        grid_size: Resolution of the density grid.
        percentile: The cumulative mass to include (e.g., 95%).
        smoothing: Sigma for gaussian filter smoothing.

    Returns:
        (x, y): Coordinates of the boundary polygon (convex hull of the main contour).
    """
    if len(points) < 10:
        return None, None

    x = points[:, 0]
    y = points[:, 1]

    # Define grid limits with some padding
    x_min, x_max = x.min(), x.max()
    y_min, y_max = y.min(), y.max()
    pad_x = (x_max - x_min) * 0.1
    pad_y = (y_max - y_min) * 0.1

    x_edges = np.linspace(x_min - pad_x, x_max + pad_x, grid_size)
    y_edges = np.linspace(y_min - pad_y, y_max + pad_y, grid_size)

    # 2D Histogram
    H, xedges, yedges = np.histogram2d(x, y, bins=(x_edges, y_edges))
    H = H.T

    # Smooth
    H_smooth = gaussian_filter(H, sigma=smoothing)

    # Find Threshold
    sorted_pixels = np.sort(H_smooth.ravel())[::-1]
    target_mass = H_smooth.sum() * (percentile / 100.0)

    current_mass = 0
    threshold = 0
    for val in sorted_pixels:
        current_mass += val
        threshold = val
        if current_mass >= target_mass:
            break

    # Generate Contour
    xc = (xedges[:-1] + xedges[1:]) / 2
    yc = (yedges[:-1] + yedges[1:]) / 2

    from matplotlib.figure import Figure
    fig = Figure()
    ax = fig.add_subplot(111)
    cs = ax.contour(xc, yc, H_smooth, levels=[threshold])

    # Extract paths
    if not cs.allsegs:
        return None, None

    segs = cs.allsegs[0]
    if not segs:
        return None, None

    # Find longest segment by number of points
    longest_seg = max(segs, key=lambda s: len(s))

    # --- SIMPLIFICATION: Convex Hull ---
    try:
        # Compute Convex Hull of the contour points to get a simple polygon
        hull = ConvexHull(longest_seg)
        hull_indices = hull.vertices

        # ConvexHull returns indices in CCW order usually
        hull_points = longest_seg[hull_indices]

        # Close the loop
        hull_points = np.vstack((hull_points, hull_points[0]))

        return hull_points[:, 0], hull_points[:, 1]

    except Exception:
        # Fallback if hull fails (e.g. collinear points or too few points)
        return longest_seg[:, 0], longest_seg[:, 1]

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import cytoflow
from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import List, Optional, Tuple, cast
import warnings
from glow.utils import safe_log10

# Suppress Cytoflow PnR vs PnB warning (common in commercial FCS)
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", message="The data range .* doesn't match the data bits")


try:
    from sklearn.ensemble import IsolationForest
    SKLEARN_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    SKLEARN_AVAILABLE = False

# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. Compensation Management
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

class CompensationManager:
    """
    Manages compensation operations, applying spillover matrices or other
    adjustments to the flow cytometry data.
    """
    def apply_compensation(self, experiment, spillover_matrix_file=None):
        """
        Applies compensation.
        """
        if spillover_matrix_file:
            print(f"Applying compensation matrix from {spillover_matrix_file}...")
            # Note: Cytoflow favors control-based compensation.
            # This is a placeholder for where manual matrix application would happen.
            pass
        return experiment

# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. Gating Strategies Hierarchy
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

class GatingStrategy(ABC):
    """
    Abstract base class for all gating strategies.
    Defines the contract for applying a gate to an experiment.
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str]):
        """
        Args:
            name: The name of the gate/condition to be created.
            channels: The list of channels involved in the gating.
        """
        self.name = name
        self.channels = channels

    def validate_dimensions(self, required_dim: int):
        """Helper to validate channel count."""
        if len(self.channels) != required_dim:
            raise ValueError(f"Strategy {self.__class__.__name__} requires {required_dim} channels, got {len(self.channels)}")

    @abstractmethod
    def apply(self, experiment):
        """
        Applies the gate to the experiment.
        """
        pass

# --- Level 2: Broad Categories ---

class OutlierDetector:
    """
    Handles outlier detection and removal to provide clean data for gating estimation.
    """
    def __init__(self, method: str = 'isolation_forest', outlier_low: float = 0.01, outlier_high: float = 0.99):
        self.method = method
        self.outlier_low = outlier_low
        self.outlier_high = outlier_high
        self.use_isolation_forest = (method == 'isolation_forest') and SKLEARN_AVAILABLE

    def get_mask(self, experiment, channels: List[str]):
        """
        Returns a boolean mask (Series) where True = Keep (Inlier), False = Remove (Outlier).
        """
        data = experiment.data
        x, y = channels[0], channels[1] if len(channels) > 1 else channels[0]

        mask = None
        if self.use_isolation_forest and len(data) > 20:
            # Isolation Forest Strategy
            contam_est = 1.0 - (self.outlier_high - self.outlier_low)
            contam_est = max(0.001, min(0.4, contam_est))

            X_iso = data.loc[:, [x, y]].values

            if len(X_iso) > 20000:
                idx = np.random.choice(len(X_iso), 20000, replace=False)
                X_train = X_iso[idx]
            else:
                X_train = X_iso

            clf = IsolationForest(contamination=contam_est, n_jobs=-1, random_state=42)
            clf.fit(X_train)

            preds = clf.predict(X_iso)
            mask = (preds == 1) # 1 = inlier
        else:
            # Quantile Box Strategy
            x_low, x_high = data[x].quantile([self.outlier_low, self.outlier_high])
            if len(channels) > 1:
                y_low, y_high = data[y].quantile([self.outlier_low, self.outlier_high])
                mask = (data[x] >= x_low) & (data[x] <= x_high) &                        (data[y] >= y_low) & (data[y] <= y_high)
            else:
                mask = (data[x] >= x_low) & (data[x] <= x_high)

        # Ensure Series format
        if not isinstance(mask, (pd.Series, pd.DataFrame)):
            mask = pd.Series(mask, index=data.index)

        return mask

    def get_clean_subset(self, experiment, channels: List[str]):
        data = experiment.data
        mask = self.get_mask(experiment, channels)

        clean_data = data[mask]

        # Check if we removed everything
        if len(clean_data) < 10:
             print("Warning: Outlier removal resulted in too few events. Using full dataset.")
             return experiment

        try:
            # Create subset experiment for estimation
            est_data_reset = clean_data.reset_index(drop=True)
            est_data_reset['Sample'] = 'Subset'
            # Create fresh Experiment
            est_exp = cytoflow.Experiment(data=est_data_reset, metadata={'Sample': 'category'})
            return est_exp
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"OutlierDetector Warning: {e}. Falling back to full experiment.")
            return experiment


def remove_non_finite(experiment, channels):
    data = experiment.data
    # Check for NaNs/Infs in relevant channels
    mask = np.ones(len(data), dtype=bool)
    for col in channels:
        if col in data.columns:
            # Handle numeric types only
             if data[col].dtype.kind in 'fi':
                 mask &= np.isfinite(data[col])

    if mask.all():
        return experiment

    clean_data = data[mask].copy()

    # Reconstruct Experiment
    try:
        if hasattr(experiment, 'clone'):
            new_exp = experiment.clone()
            new_exp.data = clean_data
            return new_exp
    except:
        pass

    # For Cytoflow (fallback)
    try:
        import cytoflow
        # Attempt to preserve metadata if possible, or minimally reconstruction
        # Use existing metadata property if available
        meta = getattr(experiment, 'metadata', None)
        if meta is None:
             # Infer minimal metadata
             meta = {c: 'channel' for c in channels}

        return cytoflow.Experiment(data=clean_data, metadata=meta)
    except:
        # Last resort: if it behaves like an object with .data
        import copy
        new_exp = copy.copy(experiment)
        new_exp.data = clean_data
        return new_exp

class AutomaticGating(GatingStrategy):
    """
    Base class for automated, data-driven gating strategies.
    Examples: GMM, K-Means, Density-based.
    Supports optional OutlierDetector to clean data before estimation.
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str], outlier_detector: Optional[OutlierDetector] = None):
        super().__init__(name, channels)
        self.outlier_detector = outlier_detector

    def get_estimation_data(self, experiment):
        """
        Returns the dataset to be used for estimating the gate parameters.
        If an outlier detector is configured, it returns the cleaned subset.
        """
        # Step 1: Remove Non-Finite Values (NaN/Inf)
        clean_exp = remove_non_finite(experiment, self.channels)

        # Step 2: Apply Outlier Detection
        if self.outlier_detector:
            return self.outlier_detector.get_clean_subset(clean_exp, self.channels)
        
        return clean_exp

class ManualGating(GatingStrategy):
    """
    Base class for manual, user-defined gating strategies.
    Examples: Polygon, Rectangle, Range/Threshold.
    """
    pass

# --- Level 3: Specific Implementations of Automatic Gating ---

class GMMGating(AutomaticGating):
    """
    Gaussian Mixture Model gating.
    Can be used for clustering, live/dead discrimination, or singlet finding.
    Dimension: N (usually 1 or 2).
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str], num_components=2, return_model=False, outlier_detector: Optional[OutlierDetector] = None):
        super().__init__(name, channels, outlier_detector)
        self.num_components = num_components
        self.return_model = return_model

    def apply(self, experiment):
        gmm = cytoflow.GaussianMixtureOp(name=self.name,
                                         channels=self.channels,
                                         num_components=self.num_components)

        # Estimate on (potentially cleaned) data
        estimation_data = self.get_estimation_data(experiment)
        gmm.estimate(estimation_data)

        res = gmm.apply(experiment)

        if self.return_model:
            return res, gmm
        return res

class DensityGating(AutomaticGating):
    """
    Density-based gating (kernel density estimation).
    Selects the high-density region of the plot.
    Dimension: 2.
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str], keep: float = 0.9, sigma: float = 1.0, outlier_detector: Optional[OutlierDetector] = None):
        super().__init__(name, channels, outlier_detector)
        self.validate_dimensions(2)
        self.keep = keep
        self.sigma = sigma

    def apply(self, experiment):
        try:
            density_op = cytoflow.DensityGateOp(name=self.name,
                                                xchannel=self.channels[0],
                                                ychannel=self.channels[1],
                                                keep=self.keep,
                                                sigma=self.sigma)

            estimation_data = self.get_estimation_data(experiment)
            
            # Sanity check
            if len(estimation_data.data) < 10:
                raise ValueError("Not enough events for density estimation after cleaning.")

            density_op.estimate(estimation_data)

            return density_op.apply(experiment)
        except Exception as e:
            # Fallback for "autodetected range" or other plotting/KDE errors
            msg = str(e)
            if "autodetected range" in msg or "finite" in msg or "Singular matrix" in msg:
                print(f"Density Gating failed ({msg}). Falling back to robust quantile gating...")
                
                # Fallback: Use OutlierDetector or simple Quantiles to create a boolean gate
                # This approximates "finding the main population"
                if self.outlier_detector:
                    mask = self.outlier_detector.get_mask(experiment, self.channels)
                else:
                    # Default robust quantile
                    od = OutlierDetector(method='quantile', outlier_low=0.05, outlier_high=0.95)
                    mask = od.get_mask(experiment, self.channels)
                
                # Apply mask to create boolean channel
                res = experiment.clone()
                # Ensure mask is boolean series compatible with data index
                res.data[self.name] = mask.astype(bool)
                # Add metadata for the new channel
                if hasattr(res, 'metadata'):
                    res.metadata[self.name] = {'type': 'condition', 'class': 'boolean'}
                
                return res
            raise e



class DBSCANGating(AutomaticGating):
    """
    DBSCAN-based gating.
    Identifies clusters based on density and proximity.
    Dimension: N (usually 2).
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str], eps: float = 0.05, min_samples: int = 50, outlier_detector: Optional[OutlierDetector] = None):
        super().__init__(name, channels, outlier_detector)
        self.eps = eps
        self.min_samples = min_samples

    def apply(self, experiment):
        from sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN
        from sklearn.neighbors import KNeighborsClassifier
        from sklearn.preprocessing import MinMaxScaler

        MAX_SAMPLES = 20000

        # Get estimation data (potentially cleaned)
        estimation_data = self.get_estimation_data(experiment)

        # Get full data for prediction later
        full_data = experiment.data.loc[:, self.channels].values

        # Determine if we need to downsample for the clustering step
        X = cast(pd.DataFrame, estimation_data.data).loc[:, self.channels].values
        n_samples = len(X)

        scaler = MinMaxScaler()

        if n_samples > MAX_SAMPLES:
            # Downsample for DBSCAN
            indices = np.random.choice(n_samples, MAX_SAMPLES, replace=False)
            X_subset = X[indices]
            X_scaled_subset = scaler.fit_transform(X_subset) # Fit scaler on subset

            # Scale min_samples relative to downsampling
            ratio = MAX_SAMPLES / n_samples
            adjusted_min_samples = max(3, int(self.min_samples * ratio))

            # Run DBSCAN on subset
            db = DBSCAN(eps=self.eps, min_samples=adjusted_min_samples).fit(X_scaled_subset)
            labels_subset = db.labels_

            # Train KNN classifier to propagate labels
            knn = KNeighborsClassifier(n_neighbors=5)
            knn.fit(X_scaled_subset, labels_subset)

            # Predict for ALL data (scaled with the same scaler)
            # Use chunks to avoid memory spike during prediction if massive
            X_full_scaled = scaler.transform(full_data)
            labels_full = knn.predict(X_full_scaled)

            # Note: KNN will predict a label for everything, even outliers from DBSCAN (-1).
            # This is generally acceptable for "gating" as we want a boundary,
            # and -1 points (outliers) will form a "noise" class region.

        else:
            # Run on full estimation data
            X_scaled = scaler.fit_transform(X)
            db = DBSCAN(eps=self.eps, min_samples=self.min_samples).fit(X_scaled)
            labels_est = db.labels_

            # If estimation_data IS experiment data, we are done
            # But if estimation_data is a subset (outlier removal), we need to propagate or map back
            # For simplicity, if we have a mismatch, we use KNN as well

            if len(X) != len(full_data):
                knn = KNeighborsClassifier(n_neighbors=1)
                knn.fit(X_scaled, labels_est)
                X_full_scaled = scaler.transform(full_data)
                labels_full = knn.predict(X_full_scaled)
            else:
                labels_full = labels_est

        # Clone experiment
        res = experiment.clone()
        res.data[self.name] = labels_full

        return res

# --- Level 3: Specific Implementations of Manual Gating ---

class PolygonGating(ManualGating):
    """
    Manual Polygon Gate.
    Dimension: 2.
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str], vertices: List[Tuple[float, float]]):
        super().__init__(name, channels)
        self.validate_dimensions(2)
        self.vertices = vertices

    def apply(self, experiment):
        poly_gate = cytoflow.PolygonOp(name=self.name,
                                       xchannel=self.channels[0],
                                       ychannel=self.channels[1],
                                       vertices=self.vertices)
        return poly_gate.apply(experiment)

class RangeGating(ManualGating):
    """
    Manual Range/Threshold Gate (1D).
    Dimension: 1.
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channel: str, low: Optional[float] = None, high: Optional[float] = None):
        super().__init__(name, [channel])
        self.low = low
        self.high = high

    def apply(self, experiment):
        range_op = cytoflow.RangeOp(name=self.name,
                                    channel=self.channels[0],
                                    low=self.low,
                                    high=self.high)
        return range_op.apply(experiment)

class MLPGating(AutomaticGating):
    """
    MLP-based gating using a pre-trained neural network.
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str], model_path: str = "data/gating_model_full.pkl", outlier_detector: Optional[OutlierDetector] = None, cell_type: str = "colo"):
        super().__init__(name, channels, outlier_detector)
        self.model_path = model_path
        self.cell_type = cell_type

    def apply(self, experiment):
        from glow.active_learning.models import GatingClassifier
        import os

        if not os.path.exists(self.model_path):
             # Fallback or Error?
             # Check if we can find it in the generic path
             self.model_path = "data/gating_model_full.pkl"
             if not os.path.exists(self.model_path):
                raise FileNotFoundError(f"MLP Model not found at {self.model_path}")

        clf = GatingClassifier.load(self.model_path)

        data = experiment.data

        # Prepare Features: Model expects 2 columns, usually Log transformed.
        xs = data[self.channels[0]].values
        ys = data[self.channels[1]].values

        # Heuristic Log Transform if data seems linear (max > 10)
        # The model 'gating_model_full.pkl' was trained on Log data.
        if np.max(xs) > 10:
            xs = safe_log10(xs + 1, min_val=1e-9)
        if np.max(ys) > 10:
            ys = safe_log10(ys + 1, min_val=1e-9)

        X = np.column_stack([xs, ys])

        # Prepare Metadata for Cell-Type Aware Inference
        # 1. Dataset ID: We treat the current experiment as a single batch (ID=0)
        #    so the DensityCenteringTransformer calculates the mode of THIS specific data.
        dataset_ids = np.zeros(len(X), dtype=int)

        # 2. Cell Type: Broadcast user selection
        cell_types = np.array([self.cell_type] * len(X))

        # Predict
        preds = clf.predict(X, dataset_ids=dataset_ids, cell_types=cell_types)

        # Result
        res = experiment.clone()

        # Map 0/1 to meaningful labels if desired, or keep as is.
        # User UI expects 'AutoLiveGate' to split population.
        # 1 = Live, 0 = Debris usually.
        # We'll cast to string or int to match expectations of categorical gate
        res.data[self.name] = preds

        return res

class MLPSingletGating(AutomaticGating):
    """
    MLP-based singlet gating using a pre-trained neural network.
    """
    def __init__(self, name: str, channels: List[str], model_path: str = "data/singlet_model_full.pkl", outlier_detector: Optional[OutlierDetector] = None, cell_type: str = "colo"):
        super().__init__(name, channels, outlier_detector)
        self.model_path = model_path
        self.cell_type = cell_type

    def apply(self, experiment):
        from glow.active_learning.models import SingletClassifier
        import os

        if not os.path.exists(self.model_path):
             self.model_path = "data/singlet_model_full.pkl"
             if not os.path.exists(self.model_path):
                raise FileNotFoundError(f"Singlet Model not found at {self.model_path}")

        clf = SingletClassifier.load(self.model_path)

        data = experiment.data

        # Prepare Features: Model expects Log_FSC_A, Log_FSC_H
        xs = data[self.channels[0]].values  # Area
        ys = data[self.channels[1]].values  # Height

        # Heuristic Log Transform if data seems linear
        if np.max(xs) > 10:
            xs = safe_log10(xs + 1, min_val=1e-9)
        if np.max(ys) > 10:
            ys = safe_log10(ys + 1, min_val=1e-9)

        X = np.column_stack([xs, ys])

        dataset_ids = np.zeros(len(X), dtype=int)
        cell_types = np.array([self.cell_type] * len(X))

        # Predict: 1 = Singlet, 0 = Doublet
        preds = clf.predict(X, dataset_ids=dataset_ids, cell_types=cell_types)

        res = experiment.clone()
        res.data[self.name] = preds == 1 # Boolean mask for ease of use
        return res

from typing import List
from .strategies import (
    CompensationManager,
    GatingStrategy,
    GMMGating,
    DensityGating,
    DBSCANGating,
    MLPGating,
    MLPSingletGating,
    PolygonGating,
    OutlierDetector
)

# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Gating Workflow
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

class GatingWorkflow:
    """
    Manages a Standard Gating Hierarchy.
    Typical Flow: Live/Dead -> Singlets -> Downstream
    """
    def __init__(self):
        self.steps: List[GatingStrategy] = []

    def add_step(self, strategy: GatingStrategy):
        self.steps.append(strategy)

    def run(self, experiment):
        """
        Runs specific steps in order.
        Note: Cytoflow operations often just add conditions to the object.
        So we pass the experiment through the chain.
        """
        current_exp = experiment
        for strategy in self.steps:
            # Handle potential tuple returns from strategies that return models
            result = strategy.apply(current_exp)
            if isinstance(result, tuple):
                current_exp = result[0]
                # If we need to store the model, we should handle it here
            else:
                current_exp = result
        return current_exp

# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Gating Manager (Facade/Compatibility Layer)
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

class GatingManager:
    """
    Central facade for gating operations.
    Maintains backward compatibility with existing pipeline code while using
    the new hierarchy.
    """
    def __init__(self):
        self.compensation_manager = CompensationManager()
        # The workflow instance could be used for building complex pipelines
        self.workflow = GatingWorkflow()

    def apply_compensation(self, experiment, spillover_matrix_file=None):
        return self.compensation_manager.apply_compensation(experiment, spillover_matrix_file)

    def _resolve_channel(self, experiment, channel_name: str, variants: List[str] = None) -> str:
        """
        Helper to resolve channel names flexibly (case-insensitive, variants).
        """
        if channel_name in experiment.channels:
            return channel_name

        lower_map = {c.lower(): c for c in experiment.channels}
        
        if channel_name.lower() in lower_map:
            return lower_map[channel_name.lower()]

        if variants:
            for v in variants:
                if v in experiment.channels:
                    return v
                if v.lower() in lower_map:
                    return lower_map[v.lower()]
        
        # Fallback: Try to find something that looks like the requested channel
        # e.g. "FSC-A" -> look for "FSC" and "A" or "Area"
        # This is a bit of a heuristic for common flow cytometry naming
        base = channel_name.upper()
        candidates = []
        if "FSC" in base or "FORWARD" in base:
            if "A" in base or "AREA" in base:
                candidates = ["FSC-Area", "FS-Area", "Forward Scatter-Area", "FSC-A", "FS-A"]
            elif "H" in base or "HEIGHT" in base:
                candidates = ["FSC-Height", "FS-Height", "Forward Scatter-Height", "FSC-H", "FS-H"]
            elif "W" in base or "WIDTH" in base:
                candidates = ["FSC-Width", "FS-Width", "Forward Scatter-Width", "FSC-W", "FS-W"]
        elif "SSC" in base or "SIDE" in base:
             if "A" in base or "AREA" in base:
                candidates = ["SSC-Area", "SS-Area", "Side Scatter-Area", "SSC-A", "SS-A"]
             elif "H" in base or "HEIGHT" in base:
                candidates = ["SSC-Height", "SS-Height", "Side Scatter-Height", "SSC-H", "SS-H"]
             elif "W" in base or "WIDTH" in base:
                candidates = ["SSC-Width", "SS-Width", "Side Scatter-Width", "SSC-W", "SS-W"]

        for c in candidates:
            if c in experiment.channels:
                return c
            if c.lower() in lower_map:
                return lower_map[c.lower()]

        return channel_name

    def remove_duplicates(self, experiment, fsc_h_channel="FSC-H", fsc_a_channel="FSC-A", linearity_threshold=0.1, method="gmm", cell_type="colo"):
        """
        Removes doublets/duplicates.
        """
        # Resolve channels
        fsc_h_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, fsc_h_channel, ["FSC-Height", "FS-H", "FS-Height"])
        fsc_a_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, fsc_a_channel, ["FSC-Area", "FS-A", "FS-Area"])

        if method == "gmm":
            strategy = GMMGating(name="_temp_singlet_mask_auto",
                                 channels=[fsc_a_channel, fsc_h_channel],
                                 num_components=1)
        elif method == "mlp":
            strategy = MLPSingletGating(name="_temp_singlet_mask_auto",
                                        channels=[fsc_a_channel, fsc_h_channel],
                                        cell_type=cell_type)
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown singlet gating method: {method}")

        return strategy.apply(experiment)

    def auto_gate_live_cells(self, experiment, x_channel="FSC-A", y_channel="SSC-A", method="density", num_components=2, keep=0.9, sigma=2.0, outlier_low=0.01, outlier_high=0.99, return_model=False, use_outlier_removal=False, eps=0.05, min_samples=50, cell_type="colo"):
        """
        Uses AutoGatingStrategy (GMM, Density).
        method: 'gmm', 'density', or 'smart' (legacy alias for density + outliers).
        """
        # Resolve channels
        x_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, x_channel, ["FSC-Area", "FS-A", "FS-Area"])
        y_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, y_channel, ["SSC-Area", "SS-A", "SS-Area"])

        outlier_detector = None

        # Handle 'smart' alias or explicit request
        if method == 'smart':
            method = 'density'
            use_outlier_removal = True

        if use_outlier_removal:
            outlier_detector = OutlierDetector(method='isolation_forest',
                                               outlier_low=outlier_low,
                                               outlier_high=outlier_high)

        if method == 'dbscan':
            strategy = DBSCANGating(name="_temp_live_labels",
                                    channels=[x_channel, y_channel],
                                    eps=eps,
                                    min_samples=min_samples,
                                    outlier_detector=outlier_detector)
        elif method == 'gmm':
            strategy = GMMGating(name="_temp_live_labels",
                                 channels=[x_channel, y_channel],
                                 num_components=num_components,
                                 return_model=return_model,
                                 outlier_detector=outlier_detector)
        elif method == 'density':
            strategy = DensityGating(name="_temp_live_labels",
                                     channels=[x_channel, y_channel],
                                     keep=keep,
                                     sigma=sigma,
                                     outlier_detector=outlier_detector)
        elif method == 'mlp':
            strategy = MLPGating(name="_temp_live_labels",
                                 channels=[x_channel, y_channel],
                                 outlier_detector=outlier_detector,
                                 cell_type=cell_type)
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown gating method: {method}")

        return strategy.apply(experiment)

    def apply_manual_gate(self, experiment, name, x_channel, y_channel, vertices):
        """
        Uses ManualGatingStrategy (Polygon).
        """
        x_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, x_channel)
        y_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, y_channel)

        strategy = PolygonGating(name=name,
                                 channels=[x_channel, y_channel],
                                 vertices=vertices)
        return strategy.apply(experiment)

    def apply_density_gate(self, experiment, name, x_channel, y_channel, keep=0.9, sigma=2.0):
        """
        Uses AutoGatingStrategy (Density implementation).
        """
        x_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, x_channel)
        y_channel = self._resolve_channel(experiment, y_channel)

        strategy = DensityGating(name=name,
                                 channels=[x_channel, y_channel],
                                 keep=keep,
                                 sigma=sigma)
        return strategy.apply(experiment)

    def filter_by_condition(self, experiment, condition_name, value):
        """
        Filters the experiment.
        """
        pass

from matplotlib.path import Path

def is_point_in_polygon(point, polygon):
    """
    Check if a point is in a polygon.
    Args:
        point: (x, y) tuple or list
        polygon: list of (x, y) tuples/lists
    Returns:
        bool
    """
    p = Path(polygon)
    # contains_point returns 0 or 1, cast to bool
    return bool(p.contains_point(point))

def points_in_polygon(points, polygon):
    """
    Vectorized version.
    Args:
        points: (N, 2) array
        polygon: list of vertices
    Returns:
        boolean array of shape (N,)
    """
    p = Path(polygon)
    return p.contains_points(points)

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
import numpy as np
import matplotlib.colors as mc
import colorsys
from .utils import get_point_density
import plotly.express as px

def calc_log_limits(data_series):
    """
    Calculates log axis limits:
    Min: Closest lower 10^x (minimal 10^0)
    Max: 10^5 higher than min
    """
    # Filter valid positive values
    valid = data_series[data_series > 0]
    if len(valid) == 0:
        return None

    min_val = valid.min()
    # floor log10
    base_exponent = np.floor(np.log10(min_val))

    # "minimal 10^0"
    final_exponent = max(0, base_exponent)

    low = 10**final_exponent
    high = 10**(final_exponent + 5)

    return low, high

def plot_2d_density_with_running_means(experiment, x_channel, y_channel, facet_col=None, hue_col=None, xscale='log', yscale='log', batch_size=1000, show_dots=True, show_dots_summary=None, show_grid=False, merged_figsize=(5, 5)):
    """
    Plots a row of 2D plots (one per facet).
    Overlays running means:
    - Main lines for the current facet (bold).
    - Ghost lines for other facets (faint, for comparison).
    Adapted from user provided 'annotate_2d_density_with_running_mean'.
    """
    # Heuristic for legend scaling based on summary figure size (fixed 5x5 currently)
    # If we want it to be "dynamic", we assume 5x5 is the "standard" size.
    # Legend is separate.
    legend_font_size = 14
    legend_marker_scale = 1.5

    if show_dots_summary is None:
        show_dots_summary = show_dots

    df = experiment.data

    # Calc custom limits if log
    x_lims = calc_log_limits(df[x_channel]) if xscale == 'log' else None
    y_lims = calc_log_limits(df[y_channel]) if yscale == 'log' else None

    # User requested: no matter what is chosen for color and facet, each experiment name should has its own curve.
    # We use "Experiment Name" as the internal grouping variable for trend lines.
    exp_col = None
    for c in ["Experiment Name", "Experiment_Name", "Sample", "sample"]:
        if c in df.columns:
            exp_col = c
            break

    # 1. Determine Facets (Axes)
    if facet_col and facet_col in df.columns:
        facets = sorted(df[facet_col].unique())
    else:
        facets = ["All Data"]

    n_facets = len(facets)

    max_cols = 5
    n_cols = min(n_facets, max_cols)
    n_rows = (n_facets + n_cols - 1) // n_cols

    # Use constrained_layout or tight_layout
    # Defaut to 300px (3 inches) for individual plots
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(n_rows, n_cols, figsize=(3 * n_cols, 3 * n_rows), constrained_layout=True, sharex=True, sharey=True)

    if n_rows * n_cols > 1:
        axs = axs.flatten()
    else:
        axs = [axs]

    # 2. Determine Hues (Lines within each facet)
    # The 'Hues' here determine the COLOR of the lines.
    # If hue_col is provided, we color by that.
    # Otherwise, if we group by experiment, we might want unique colors for experiments or a single color.
    # To satisfy the request: each experiment gets a curve.
    # We will iterate over unique experiments within each facet.
    if hue_col and hue_col in df.columns:
        unique_colors = sorted(df[hue_col].unique())
    else:
        unique_colors = [None]

    # Color Palette per Color Category: Distinct Hues, Excluding Greens
    n_needed = len(unique_colors) if len(unique_colors) > 0 else 1

    # Generate a large pool to pick from (HUSL for perceptual uniformity)
    n_pool = max(50, n_needed * 5)
    pool_palette = sns.color_palette("husl", n_pool)

    valid_colors = []
    for c in pool_palette:
        h, b, s = colorsys.rgb_to_hls(*c)
        valid_colors.append(c)

    # Select indices maximizing distance
    safe_palette = []
    if len(valid_colors) > 0:
        if len(valid_colors) >= n_needed:
             # Use endpoint=False to avoid picking the first and last colors which are often similar (Red vs Red-Purple)
             # given that the valid pool wraps around in hue space (Start is Red, End is Red-wrap)
             indices = np.linspace(0, len(valid_colors), n_needed, endpoint=False, dtype=int)
             selection = [valid_colors[i] for i in indices]
        else:
             # Cycle if needed
             selection = valid_colors * (n_needed // len(valid_colors) + 1)
             selection = selection[:n_needed]
    else:
        selection = sns.color_palette("bright", n_needed)

    # Apply brightness/saturation tweaks
    for c in selection:
         h, b, s = colorsys.rgb_to_hls(*c)
         # Slightly brighten and ensure high saturation as requested
         new_l = min(0.85, max(0.5, b * 1.1))
         # Increase saturation slightly but cap at 1.0
         new_s = min(1.0, max(0.6, s * 1.2))
         safe_palette.append(colorsys.hls_to_rgb(h, new_l, new_s))

    color_map = {h: safe_palette[i] for i, h in enumerate(unique_colors)}

    # Detect Replicate Column
    rep_col = None
    for c in df.columns:
        if c.lower() == 'replicate':
            rep_col = c
            break

    # 3. Pre-calculate Facet Traces (For Separate Plots - Split by Replicates)
    facet_traces = []

    for facet in facets:
        if facet == "All Data":
            facet_df = df
        else:
            facet_df = df[df[facet_col] == facet]

        if len(facet_df) == 0:
            continue

        # Group by Experiment Name (the requested individual curve)
        if exp_col:
            groups = facet_df.groupby(exp_col, observed=False)
        else:
            groups = [(None, facet_df)]

        for group_name, exp_subset in groups:
            # Handle Replicates: Split exp_subset into replicates if available
            replicate_subgroups = []
            if rep_col:
                # Group by replicate
                for rep_val, rep_df in exp_subset.groupby(rep_col, observed=False):
                    replicate_subgroups.append((rep_val, rep_df))
            else:
                # Treat as single group
                replicate_subgroups.append(("All", exp_subset))

            # Determine base color and label based on hue_col
            if hue_col and hue_col in df.columns:
                h_val = exp_subset[hue_col].iloc[0]
                base_color = color_map.get(h_val, 'black')
                base_label = f"{h_val} ({group_name})" if group_name is not None else str(h_val)
            else:
                h_val = None
                base_color = 'black'
                base_label = str(group_name) if group_name is not None else "Mean" # Use group name if no hue

            # Iterate through replicates to create traces
            n_reps = len(replicate_subgroups)
            for i, (rep_name, subset_df) in enumerate(replicate_subgroups):

                if len(subset_df) < (batch_size // 5):
                    continue

                # Calculate Color Variation (Lightness Shift)
                final_color = base_color
                label = base_label

                if n_reps > 1:
                    label = f"{base_label} (Rep {rep_name})"
                    try:
                        c_rgb = mc.to_rgb(base_color)
                        c_hls = colorsys.rgb_to_hls(*c_rgb)
                        # Spread lightness centered around original L
                        # Shift range +/- 0.15 depending on number of reps
                        if n_reps > 1:
                            # Map i to range [-1, 1]
                            rel_pos = (i / (n_reps - 1)) * 2 - 1  if n_reps > 1 else 0
                            # Shift lightness: e.g. -0.15 to +0.15
                            l_shift = rel_pos * 0.15
                            new_l = max(0.2, min(0.9, c_hls[1] + l_shift))
                            final_color = colorsys.hls_to_rgb(c_hls[0], new_l, c_hls[2])
                    except Exception:
                        pass # Keep base color if conversion fails

                # Calculate Rolling Mean
                subset_df = subset_df.sort_values(by=x_channel)

                eff_batch = batch_size
                if len(subset_df) < eff_batch:
                    eff_batch = max(5, int(len(subset_df) * 0.1))

                roller = subset_df.rolling(window=eff_batch, min_periods=eff_batch//2, center=True)
                roll_x = roller[x_channel].mean()
                roll_y = roller[y_channel].mean()

                facet_traces.append({
                    'facet': facet,
                    'group': group_name,
                    'x': roll_x,
                    'y': roll_y,
                    'color': final_color,
                    'label': label,
                    'hue_val': h_val
                })

    # 4. Pre-calculate Summary Traces (For Merged Plot - Pooled Replicates)
    summary_traces = []

    summary_group_col = hue_col if hue_col else exp_col
    # If no hue and no experiment column found? Rare, but default to whole DF
    if not summary_group_col:
        sum_groups = [None]
    else:
        # Sort to ensure consistent color mapping
        sum_groups = sorted(df[summary_group_col].unique())

    for g_val in sum_groups:
        if g_val is None:
            g_df = df
        else:
            g_df = df[df[summary_group_col] == g_val]

        if len(g_df) < (batch_size // 5):
            continue

        if hue_col:
            base_color = color_map.get(g_val, 'black')
        else:
            # When grouping by experiment (no Hue), assign distinct colors cyclically from safe palette
            if safe_palette and sum_groups:
                 base_color = safe_palette[sum_groups.index(g_val) % len(safe_palette)]
            else:
                 base_color = 'black'

        # Calculate Rolling Mean on POOLED data (all replicates)
        g_df_sorted = g_df.sort_values(by=x_channel)

        eff_batch = batch_size
        if len(g_df_sorted) < eff_batch:
            eff_batch = max(5, int(len(g_df_sorted) * 0.1))

        roller = g_df_sorted.rolling(window=eff_batch, min_periods=eff_batch//2, center=True)
        roll_x = roller[x_channel].mean()
        roll_y = roller[y_channel].mean()

        summary_traces.append({
            'x': roll_x,
            'y': roll_y,
            'color': base_color,
            'label': str(g_val)
        })


    # 4. Plotting Loop
    for i, ax in enumerate(axs):
        if i >= n_facets:
            ax.set_visible(False)
            continue

        current_facet = facets[i]
        ax.set_title(str(current_facet))

        # A. Background Scatter (Density or Grey)
        if show_dots:
            if current_facet == "All Data":
                bg_data = df
            else:
                bg_data = df[df[facet_col] == current_facet]

            # Sample for performance/visuals if NOT calculating full density?
            # Or sample anyway because density on 1M points is slow to render.
            n_points = len(bg_data)
            if n_points > 10000:
                 # If coloring by density, ideally we estimate density on FULL data, then sample points to plot.
                 # But for speed, sample first.
                 bg_data = bg_data.sample(10000, random_state=42)

            x_vals = bg_data[x_channel].values
            y_vals = bg_data[y_channel].values

            # Density Color Calculation
            z, mask = get_point_density(x_vals, y_vals, log_x=(xscale=='log'), log_y=(yscale=='log'))
            x_vals = x_vals[mask]
            y_vals = y_vals[mask]
            # z is already filtered to match mask by get_point_density, so we don't apply mask to it again.
            # z = z[mask] <- This was the bug

            # Sort by density so high density dots are on top
            sort_idx = np.argsort(z)

            ax.scatter(x_vals[sort_idx], y_vals[sort_idx], c=z[sort_idx], cmap='viridis_r', s=3, alpha=0.6, rasterized=True, zorder=0)

        if show_grid:
            ax.grid(True, which='both', linestyle='--', linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.5)
        else:
            ax.grid(False)
            ax.set_facecolor('white')

        # B. Plot Traces (Facet specific)
        for trace in facet_traces:
            # Check if this trace belongs to current facet
            is_current = (trace['facet'] == current_facet)

            if is_current:
                # Main Line: Bold, opaque
                ax.plot(trace['x'], trace['y'], color=trace['color'], linewidth=3, alpha=1.0, label=trace['label'], zorder=10)

        # Axis scales/labels
        if xscale == 'log':
            ax.set_xscale('log')
            if x_lims:
                ax.set_xlim(x_lims)
        if yscale == 'log':
            ax.set_yscale('log')
            if y_lims:
                ax.set_ylim(y_lims)

        # Only show xlabel on bottom-most plot in column (handle ragged grid)
        if i + n_cols >= n_facets:
            ax.set_xlabel(x_channel)
            ax.tick_params(labelbottom=True)

        # Only show ylabel on left-most plot in row
        if i % n_cols == 0:
            ax.set_ylabel(y_channel)


    # 5. Create Separate Summary Figure
    # Default to 500px (5 inches) for merged plot
    fig_summary, ax_sum = plt.subplots(figsize=merged_figsize)
    ax_sum.set_title("Merged")

    if show_dots_summary:
         # For summary, maybe just sample randomly from everything?
        if len(df) > 5000:
            sum_bg = df.sample(5000, random_state=42)
        else:
            sum_bg = df

        # Density on pooled data? Or just grey?
        # "merged is with itself" -> usually trends.
        # But if we want scatter, maybe just grey is safer to avoid confusion with density of specific groups.
        # Or calculate global density. Let's do global density.
        sx = sum_bg[x_channel].values
        sy = sum_bg[y_channel].values
        sz, smask = get_point_density(sx, sy, log_x=(xscale=='log'), log_y=(yscale=='log'))
        sx = sx[smask]
        sy = sy[smask]
        sz = sz[smask]
        s_idx = np.argsort(sz)

        ax_sum.scatter(sx[s_idx], sy[s_idx], c=sz[s_idx], cmap='viridis_r', s=2, alpha=0.5, rasterized=True, zorder=0)

    if show_grid:
        ax_sum.grid(True, which='both', linestyle='--', linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.5)
    else:
        ax_sum.grid(False)
        ax_sum.set_facecolor('white')

    for trace in summary_traces:
        # Plot pooled/summary traces on summary
        # For the legend, we still want to group by Hue if labels are messy
        ax_sum.plot(trace['x'], trace['y'], color=trace['color'], linewidth=3.5, alpha=1.0, label=trace['label'], zorder=10)


    # De-duplicate legend to show unique Hues or group names
    handles, labels = ax_sum.get_legend_handles_labels()

    legend_args = None
    legend_kwargs = {}

    if hue_col:
        # Map back to hue label only for a cleaner legend
        clean_labels = []
        clean_handles = []
        seen = set()
        for h, lab in zip(handles, labels):
             # Extract the hue part from "Hue (Experiment)"
             base_l = lab.split(" (")[0]
             if base_l not in seen:
                  clean_labels.append(base_l)
                  clean_handles.append(h)
                  seen.add(base_l)
        if clean_handles:
             legend_args = (clean_handles, clean_labels)
             legend_kwargs = {'title': hue_col, 'loc': 'center left', 'fontsize': legend_font_size, 'markerscale': legend_marker_scale}
    else:
        by_label = dict(zip(labels, handles))
        if by_label:
            legend_args = (by_label.values(), by_label.keys())
            legend_kwargs = {'loc': 'center left', 'fontsize': legend_font_size, 'markerscale': legend_marker_scale}

    # Separate Legend Figure
    fig_legend = None
    if legend_args:
        # Create a separate figure for the legend
        # Scale figure size based on font/marker scale?
        # Default font size is ~10pt. If user sets 5, box should shrink.
        scale_factor = (legend_font_size / 10.0) * legend_marker_scale

        n_items = len(legend_args[0])
        fig_h = max(1.0, n_items * 0.2 * scale_factor)
        fig_w = 1.5 * scale_factor

        fig_legend = plt.figure(figsize=(fig_w, fig_h))
        fig_legend.patch.set_alpha(0.0)

        # Enforce fontsize
        legend_kwargs['fontsize'] = legend_font_size
        if 'title_fontsize' not in legend_kwargs:
             legend_kwargs['title_fontsize'] = legend_font_size

        # Use fig.legend to place it centered or filling the figure
        # We put it at center of figure
        fig_legend.legend(*legend_args, **legend_kwargs)

    if xscale == 'log':
        ax_sum.set_xscale('log')
        if x_lims:
            ax_sum.set_xlim(x_lims)
    if yscale == 'log':
        ax_sum.set_yscale('log')
        if y_lims:
            ax_sum.set_ylim(y_lims)
    ax_sum.set_xlabel(x_channel)
    ax_sum.set_ylabel(y_channel)

    # Force 1:1 Aspect Ratio on the Axes Box
    ax_sum.set_box_aspect(1)

    return fig, fig_summary, fig_legend

def plot_2d_scatter(experiment, x_channel, y_channel, hue=None, facet=None, xscale='log', yscale='log', show_trend=False, show_dots=True, show_dots_summary=None, show_grid=False, title=None, figsize=(4, 4), merged_figsize=(5, 5)):
    """
    Wrapper decision maker for 2D plots.
    """
    # If show_trend is requested, use the fancy specialized plot
    if show_trend:
        # We Map 'facet' arg to the 'facet_col' and 'hue' to 'hue_col'
        return plot_2d_density_with_running_means(
            experiment, x_channel, y_channel,
            facet_col=facet,
            hue_col=hue,
            xscale=xscale,
            yscale=yscale,
            batch_size=1000,
            show_dots=show_dots,
            show_dots_summary=show_dots_summary,
            show_grid=show_grid,
            merged_figsize=merged_figsize
        )

    # Otherwise standard scatter
    df = experiment.data
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=figsize)

    # Log scale
    log_x = (xscale == 'log')
    log_y = (yscale == 'log')

    sns.scatterplot(
        data=df, x=x_channel, y=y_channel, hue=hue,
        s=10, alpha=0.6, ax=ax
    )

    if log_x:
        ax.set_xscale('log')
        x_lims = calc_log_limits(df[x_channel])
        if x_lims:
            ax.set_xlim(x_lims)
    if log_y:
        ax.set_yscale('log')
        y_lims = calc_log_limits(df[y_channel])
        if y_lims:
            ax.set_ylim(y_lims)

    if title:
        ax.set_title(title)

    return fig


def plot_2d_scatter_plotly(experiment, x_channel, y_channel, hue=None, facet=None, scale='linear', show_grid=False, title=None):
    """
    Plotly version of 2D Scatter.
    """
    df = experiment.data.copy()

    # Ensure numerical consistency if Scale is log
    log_x = (scale == 'log')
    log_y = (scale == 'log')

    # Plotly handles log scaling in update_layout

    # Construct title
    full_title = title if title else f"{x_channel} vs {y_channel}"

    # Basic Scatter
    # We use plotly express for easy facet/hue handling

    # Subsample if too large for interactivity? Plotly WebGL can handle ~100k reasonably well.
    # Let's cap at 50k for safety for now or let it ride.
    if len(df) > 50000:
        df = df.sample(50000, random_state=42)
        full_title += " (Subsampled 50k)"

    fig = px.scatter(
        df,
        x=x_channel,
        y=y_channel,
        color=hue if hue in df.columns else None,
        facet_col=facet if facet in df.columns else None,
        facet_col_wrap=3 if facet else 0,
        title=full_title,
        opacity=0.6,
        render_mode='webgl' # Faster rendering
    )

    # Update Axes
    fig.update_xaxes(showgrid=show_grid)
    fig.update_yaxes(showgrid=show_grid)

    if log_x:
        fig.update_xaxes(type="log")
    if log_y:
        fig.update_yaxes(type="log")

    # Layout Tweaks
    fig.update_layout(
        template="plotly_white",
        margin=dict(l=40, r=40, t=60, b=40),
        legend=dict(
            orientation="h",
            yanchor="bottom",
            y=1.02,
            xanchor="right",
            x=1
        )
    )

    return fig
