import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from .utils import adjust_pvalues, get_significance_label

def plot_means_with_significance(experiment, channel, group_by, comparisons=[], statistic='mean',
                               hue_order=None, show_distribution=True, replicate_col=None,
                               log_scale=False, annotation_cols=[], correction_method='t-test',
                               use_bars=False, font_size=12, bracket_spacing=0.15,
                               gates=None, gate_channel=None, gate_threshold=None,
                               error_bar='sem'):
    """
    Violin/Strip plot with mean lines, replicate dots, and significance brackets.
    correction_method: 't-test', 'bonferroni', 'fdr'
    gates: List of dicts [{'channel': str, 'threshold': (low, high) or float}]
    error_bar: 'sem', 'sd', or None
    """
    df = experiment.data

    # 1. Prepare Data (Gating)
    plot_df = df.copy()

    # Handle multiple gates
    if gates:
        print(f"DEBUG: Applying {len(gates)} gates to Bar Plot")
        for i, gate in enumerate(gates):
            ch = gate.get('channel')
            th = gate.get('threshold')
            direction = gate.get('direction', 'positive') # Default to positive

            if ch in plot_df.columns and th is not None:
                before = len(plot_df)
                if isinstance(th, (tuple, list)) and len(th) == 2:
                    plot_df = plot_df[(plot_df[ch] >= th[0]) & (plot_df[ch] <= th[1])]
                else:
                    if direction == 'negative' or direction == 'Negative (<)':
                        plot_df = plot_df[plot_df[ch] < th]
                    else:
                        plot_df = plot_df[plot_df[ch] > th]
                print(f"DEBUG: Gate {i} ({ch}) reduced points from {before} to {len(plot_df)}")

    # Backward compatibility for single gate
    if gate_channel and gate_threshold is not None:
        if isinstance(gate_threshold, (tuple, list)) and len(gate_threshold) == 2:
            plot_df = plot_df[(plot_df[gate_channel] >= gate_threshold[0]) & (plot_df[gate_channel] <= gate_threshold[1])]
        else:
            plot_df = plot_df[plot_df[gate_channel] > gate_threshold]

    # Combined Filter Check
    if len(plot_df) == 0:
        fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(6, 4))
        ax.text(0.5, 0.5, "No events remaining\nafter applying gates",
                ha='center', va='center', color='red', fontsize=14)
        ax.set_axis_off()
        return fig

    if group_by not in df.columns:
        fig, ax = plt.subplots()
        ax.text(0.5, 0.5, "Invalid Group Column", ha='center')
        return fig

    unique_groups = hue_order if hue_order else sorted(df[group_by].unique())
    # Filter groups to only those present in data
    unique_groups = [g for g in unique_groups if g in df[group_by].values]

    # Adjust width to be consistent across modes
    width_per_group = 1.2
    
    # Calculate desired width but clamp to 15 inches (approx 1500px) to fit in screen
    calc_width = max(3, len(unique_groups)*width_per_group)
    final_width = min(15, calc_width)
    
    # Consistent height for both modes
    final_height = 4.5
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(final_width, final_height))

    # If log_scale and data is large, we should log transform the data for plotting
    # so that the KDE (violin shape) is calculated on log-space (Gaussian-like)
    # rather than linear space (skewed).
    # We will then label the Y-axis with 10^x formats.

    transformed_channel = channel
    if log_scale:
        transformed_channel = f"log_{channel}"
        # Filter positive values
        plot_df = plot_df[plot_df[channel] > 0].copy()
        plot_df[transformed_channel] = np.log10(plot_df[channel])
        # We will plot using this transformed column

    # Ensure replicate col is categorical if used for hue
    if replicate_col and replicate_col in plot_df.columns:
        plot_df[replicate_col] = plot_df[replicate_col].astype(str)

    # 2. Distribution Plot (Violin or Strip)
    # Using 'jitter' to spread points
    if show_distribution:
        # Step-like histogram violin (mimic) or smooth violin?
        # User requested "look more like this" (image has stepped look usually, or at least hollow)
        # We use violinplot with 'white' face and 'gray' edge.
        # bw_adjust=1 (default) gives standard smoothing.
        # width=0.8 to separate groups

        # Use hue=replicate_col to split violins by replicate
        # dodge=False to overlap them at the same position
        # Use a Greys palette for replicates so they are distinguishable
        # Use a nice palette for replicates if applicable (e.g. pastel)
        # Avoid Greys which was for the hollow style
        n_reps = len(plot_df[replicate_col].unique()) if replicate_col else 1
        # User requests Hollow Violins with Grey outlines (no fill colors)
        # We use a Greys palette for the outlines
        n_reps = len(plot_df[replicate_col].unique()) if replicate_col else 1
        # Select grey variants.
        violin_palette = sns.color_palette("Greys", n_colors=n_reps + 2)[1:-1]

        v = sns.violinplot(
            data=plot_df, x=group_by, y=transformed_channel if log_scale else channel,
            hue=replicate_col if replicate_col else None,
            order=unique_groups,
            dodge=False, # Overlap violins
            ax=ax,
            palette=violin_palette if replicate_col else None,
            inner=None, linewidth=1.5, saturation=1, cut=0, width=0.8,
            bw_adjust=0.8, legend=False
        )

        # Set facecolor to none to make it hollow
        import matplotlib.collections
        for collection in ax.collections:
            if isinstance(collection, matplotlib.collections.PolyCollection):
                original_face = collection.get_facecolor()
                collection.set_edgecolor(original_face)
                collection.set_facecolor('none')
                collection.set_alpha(1.0) # Opaque lines


    # 3. Replicate Dots & Mean Lines
    # Map groups to x-coordinates
    group_map = {g: i for i, g in enumerate(unique_groups)}
    palette = sns.color_palette("bright", len(unique_groups))

    # 3. Replicate Dots & Mean Lines
    # Map groups to x-coordinates
    group_map = {g: i for i, g in enumerate(unique_groups)}
    palette = sns.color_palette("bright", len(unique_groups))

    # We maintain two types of means:
    # 1. rep_means_linear: List of linear means of each replicate (for stats & FC)
    # 2. plot_mean_val: The value to plot the green line at (Log space if log_scale)

    group_stats_info = {} # Store linear replicate means for each group

    # Process each group to calculate stats
    for i, grp in enumerate(unique_groups):
        sub = plot_df[plot_df[group_by] == grp]
        if len(sub) == 0:
            group_stats_info[grp] = {'linear_reps': np.array([]), 'linear_grand_mean': 0}
            continue

        linear_reps = np.array([])
        linear_grand_mean = 0

        # Calculate Linear Replicate Means (Always Linear for Stats/FC)
        if replicate_col and replicate_col in sub.columns:
            # Group by replicate -> Calculate Linear Mean of each
            linear_rep_means = sub.groupby(replicate_col)[channel].mean()
            # Remove NaNs
            linear_reps = linear_rep_means.dropna().values
            if len(linear_reps) > 0:
                 linear_grand_mean = np.mean(linear_reps)
            else:
                 # No replicates valid? fallback to all events linear mean
                 linear_grand_mean = sub[channel].mean()
                 linear_reps = sub[channel].values # Treat events as replicates for T-test?? No, this breaks N.
                 # If no replicates, we have 1 replicate = mean
                 linear_reps = np.array([linear_grand_mean])
        else:
             # No replicate column
             linear_grand_mean = sub[channel].mean()
             linear_reps = np.array([linear_grand_mean]) # 1 sample

        group_stats_info[grp] = {'linear_reps': linear_reps, 'linear_grand_mean': linear_grand_mean}

        x_idx = group_map[grp]

        # 3.2 Drawing Dots (Replicates)
        # We need to render the dots at their correct Y position
        # If log_scale is True, we plot Log10(LinearMeanOfReplicate)
        # If log_scale is False, we plot LinearMeanOfReplicate

        if replicate_col and replicate_col in sub.columns:
             # We need a dataframe for stripplot
             # Re-access groupby to keep replicate IDs
             lrm = sub.groupby(replicate_col)[channel].mean()
             dot_data = []
             for rid, val in lrm.items():
                 y_pos = np.log10(val) if log_scale and val > 0 else val
                 dot_data.append({group_by: grp, replicate_col: str(rid), 'y_val': y_pos})

             if dot_data:
                 d_df = pd.DataFrame(dot_data)
                 # Force conversion to list to satisfy type checker for ax.scatter
                 y_vals = d_df['y_val'].values.astype(float)
                 n_dots = len(y_vals)

                 # Calculate symmetric x-offsets around x_idx
                 if n_dots > 1:
                     # spread=0.1 means dots will be within +/- 0.05 of the center
                     spread = 0.1
                     x_offsets = np.linspace(-spread/2, spread/2, n_dots)
                     x_positions = x_idx + x_offsets
                 else:
                     x_positions = [x_idx]

                 ax.scatter(x_positions, y_vals.tolist(), color='black', s=25, edgecolor='white', linewidth=0.5, zorder=25)

        # 3.3 Drawing Mean Line (or Bar)
        # We plot the specific Y value corresponding to the "Mean of Means"
        # If log_scale, plot log10(linear_grand_mean)
        plot_mean_val = np.log10(linear_grand_mean) if log_scale and linear_grand_mean > 0 else linear_grand_mean

        # Calculate Error for Error Bars
        err_val = 0
        if error_bar and len(linear_reps) > 1:
            if error_bar.lower() == 'sd':
                err_val = np.std(linear_reps)
            elif error_bar.lower() == 'sem':
                err_val = np.std(linear_reps) / np.sqrt(len(linear_reps))

        # Determine Error Bar Y-offsets (asymmetric if log scale)
        yerr = None
        if err_val > 0:
            if log_scale:
                # Calculate bounds in linear, then transform to log offsets
                # We use max(1e-10, ...) to avoid log(<=0)
                lower_linear = max(1e-10, linear_grand_mean - err_val)
                upper_linear = linear_grand_mean + err_val

                lower_log = np.log10(lower_linear)
                upper_log = np.log10(upper_linear)

                # yerr for errorbar needs (lower_offset, upper_offset)
                yerr = [[plot_mean_val - lower_log], [upper_log - plot_mean_val]]
            else:
                yerr = err_val

        # Determine Bar/Line Color based on Fluorophore
        # Blue/Violet: TagBFP, Pacific Blue, etc. (#64B5F6 - Soft Blue)
        # Red/Orange: mCherry, PE, APC, etc. (#E57373 - Soft Red)
        # Green (Default): GFP, YFP, etc. (#74C476 - Muted Green)
        
        c_upper = channel.upper()
        if any(x in c_upper for x in ['BFP', 'TAGBFP', 'PACIFIC', 'DAPI', 'BV421', 'V450', 'SB436']):
             main_color = '#64B5F6'
        elif any(x in c_upper for x in ['MCHERRY', 'MSCARLET', 'RFP', 'DSRED', 'PE', 'APC', 'AF647', 'CY5', 'TDTO']):
             main_color = '#E57373'
        else:
             main_color = '#74C476'

        if use_bars:
            # Draw Bar
            # If show_distribution is True, maybe make bar more transparent or narrower
            bar_alpha = 0.5 if show_distribution else 0.9
            ax.bar(x_idx, plot_mean_val, width=0.6, color=main_color, edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5, zorder=0, alpha=bar_alpha) # zorder=0 to be behind dots

            if yerr is not None:
                ax.errorbar(x_idx, plot_mean_val, yerr=yerr, fmt='none', ecolor='black', capsize=10, elinewidth=2.0, zorder=20)
        else:
            # Standard Line
            ax.hlines(y=plot_mean_val, xmin=x_idx-0.3, xmax=x_idx+0.3, colors=main_color, linewidth=2, zorder=15, alpha=0.8)


    # 4. Significance Brackets
    if comparisons:
        # Determine strict Y-limit based on what is visible
        if show_distribution:
            # Full distribution visible, use data max
            if log_scale:
                 max_data = plot_df[transformed_channel].max()
            else:
                 max_data = plot_df[channel].max()
        else:
            # Only dots/means visible. Find max of means.
            # We can use the max of 'group_stats_info' values (plot_mean_val or y_pos of dots)
            all_ymax = []
            for grp, info in group_stats_info.items():
                # Add mean line position
                val = info['linear_grand_mean']
                y_val = np.log10(val) if log_scale and val > 0 else val
                all_ymax.append(y_val)

                # Add error bar top if applicable (only if bars are enabled)
                if use_bars and error_bar and len(info['linear_reps']) > 1:
                    reps = info['linear_reps']
                    if error_bar.lower() == 'sd':
                        e = np.std(reps)
                    else: # sem
                        e = np.std(reps) / np.sqrt(len(reps))

                    top = val + e
                    y_top = np.log10(top) if log_scale and top > 0 else top
                    all_ymax.append(y_top)

                # Add dots positions
                for r in info['linear_reps']:
                    y_r = np.log10(r) if log_scale and r > 0 else r
                    all_ymax.append(y_r)

            if all_ymax:
                max_data = max(all_ymax)
            else:
                max_data = 0

        current_y = max_data

        # 1. Collect all raw p-values first for adjustment
        comparison_results = []
        for i, comp_tuple in enumerate(comparisons):
            if len(comp_tuple) == 2:
                g1, g2 = comp_tuple
                custom_label = None
            elif len(comp_tuple) == 3:
                 g1, g2, custom_label = comp_tuple
            else:
                 continue

            if g1 not in group_stats_info or g2 not in group_stats_info:
                continue
            if g1 == g2:
                continue

            # Use stored Linear Replicate Means for Statistics
            info1 = group_stats_info[g1]
            info2 = group_stats_info[g2]

            d1 = info1['linear_reps']
            d2 = info2['linear_reps']

            # Calculate Fold Change of Grand Means
            m1 = info1['linear_grand_mean']
            m2 = info2['linear_grand_mean']
            fc = m2 / m1 if m1 != 0 else 0

            # Use custom t-test on Replicate Means
            # If len(d1) < 2, ttest returns NaN or error usually
            p = 1.0
            if len(d1) > 1 and len(d2) > 1:
                try:
                    from scipy import stats
                    _, p = stats.ttest_ind(d1, d2, equal_var=False)
                except Exception:
                    p = 1.0

            comparison_results.append({
                'g1': g1, 'g2': g2, 'p': p, 'fc': fc, 'custom': custom_label,
                'x1': group_map[g1], 'x2': group_map[g2]
            })

        # 2. Adjust p-values
        raw_p = [r['p'] for r in comparison_results]
        adjusted_p = adjust_pvalues(raw_p, method=correction_method)

        # 3. Draw Brackets
        
        # Simplified Bracket Spacing Logic
        # bracket_spacing comes from slider (0.05 to 0.30, default 0.15)
        # We want this to represent a fraction of "Typical Plot Height".
        
        step_val = 0
        if log_scale:
            # In Log Scale, typical visual height is 4-5 decades.
            # 0.15 * 4 = 0.6 decades per step. This is quite responding.
            # If user wants smaller steps, they slide to 0.05 -> 0.2 decades.
            TYPICAL_LOG_HEIGHT = 5.0 
            step_val = TYPICAL_LOG_HEIGHT * bracket_spacing
        else:
            # In Linear Scale, height depends on data max.
            # We assume the plot goes from 0 to max_data.
            # If max_data is 1000, step is 150.
            ref_height = max_data if max_data > 0 else 1.0
            step_val = ref_height * bracket_spacing

        for i, res in enumerate(comparison_results):
            p_adj = adjusted_p[i]
            fc = res['fc']
            sig_label = get_significance_label(p_adj)

            # Use custom label if provided, else construct standard one
            label = res['custom']
            if not label or label == '*':
                 # User Request: Just Fold Change, append stars if significant. No 'ns' text.
                 suffix = sig_label if sig_label != "ns" else ""
                 label = f"{fc:.1f}x{suffix}"

            # Draw
            x1, x2 = res['x1'], res['x2']
            
            # Apply step
            # If Distribution is plotted, move the first bracket closer to data
            this_step = step_val
            if i == 0 and show_distribution:
                 this_step = step_val * 0.4
            
            current_y += this_step
            
            # Leg height is fraction of step
            h = step_val * 0.25

            ax.plot([x1, x1, x2, x2], [current_y, current_y+h, current_y+h, current_y], lw=1, c='black')
            ax.text((x1+x2)/2, current_y + h, label, ha='center', va='bottom', fontsize=font_size)

    # Final Axis Formatting
    ax.set_xlabel("")
    ax.spines['top'].set_visible(False)
    ax.spines['right'].set_visible(False)
    ax.tick_params(axis='both', labelsize=font_size)

    # Manual Box/Bar Plot requires explicit x-ticks setup since we plotted at integer indices
    if not show_distribution:
        ax.set_xticks(range(len(unique_groups)))
        ax.set_xticklabels([str(g) for g in unique_groups])

    # Label Formatting
    clean_channel = channel.replace("-A", "")
    y_label_text = f"{clean_channel} fluorescence intensity (A.U.)"

    if log_scale:
        from matplotlib.ticker import FuncFormatter, MaxNLocator
        
        # Determine Limits for Integers
        current_y_max = ax.get_ylim()[1]
        
        # User Request: If data is around 10^1, set max to 10^3.
        # This implies rounding up to nearest integer + 2 padding
        # But we must be careful not to hide data if brackets pushed it up
        
        # 1. Round up to nearest integer
        import math
        ceil_max = math.ceil(current_y_max)
        
        # 2. Add cushion (1 or 2 decades) ONLY if NOT showing distribution
        # If we are close to the integer boundary, add 1. 
        if not show_distribution:
            new_max = ceil_max + 1
            
            # Ensure we don't zoom in (cut off data)
            if new_max < current_y_max:
                 new_max += 1

            # User Request: Ensure Y-axis starts at 10^0 (0 in log space) for cleaner baseline
            # This is especially important when use_bars=False (dots only) to avoid zooming in too much on high values
            ax.set_ylim(bottom=0, top=new_max)

        def log_formatter_int(x, pos):
             return f"$10^{{{int(round(x))}}}$"

        ax.yaxis.set_major_formatter(FuncFormatter(log_formatter_int))
        ax.yaxis.set_major_locator(MaxNLocator(integer=True))

        # Add Log Scale Minor Ticks (2, 3.. 9 inside each decade)
        # Since we are on a linear log10 axis, these are at integer + log10(k)
        from matplotlib.ticker import FixedLocator
        current_ylim = ax.get_ylim()
        floor_min = int(math.floor(current_ylim[0]))
        ceil_max = int(math.ceil(current_ylim[1]))
        
        minor_locs = []
        for d in range(floor_min, ceil_max + 1):
            for k in range(2, 10):
                 val = d + np.log10(k)
                 if current_ylim[0] <= val <= current_ylim[1]:
                     minor_locs.append(val)
        
        if minor_locs:
             ax.yaxis.set_minor_locator(FixedLocator(minor_locs))
             ax.tick_params(which='minor', axis='y', length=3)

        ax.set_ylabel(y_label_text, fontsize=font_size)
    else:
        ax.set_ylabel(y_label_text, fontsize=font_size)

    # Update x-axis labels with annotations if requested
    if annotation_cols:
        # Prepare table data: Columns are Groups, Row 1 is Group Name, Subsequent rows are Annotations
        # Row labels
        row_labels = [group_by] + annotation_cols

        # Build cell text (list of lists, rows x cols)
        cell_text = []

        # 1. Group Name Row
        cell_text.append([str(g) for g in unique_groups])

        # 2. Annotation Rows
        for col in annotation_cols:
            row_vals = []
            for grp in unique_groups:
                subset = plot_df[plot_df[group_by] == grp]
                val = ""
                if not subset.empty:
                    # Representative value (first row)
                    val = str(subset.iloc[0][col])
                    # If mixed values exists, maybe indicate? For now assume 1:1 mapping
                row_vals.append(val)
            cell_text.append(row_vals)

        # Create Table
        # Turn off x tick labels
        ax.set_xticklabels([])
        ax.tick_params(axis='x', length=0)

        # Add table at bottom
        the_table = ax.table(cellText=cell_text, rowLabels=row_labels, loc='bottom', cellLoc='center', edges='open')
        the_table.auto_set_font_size(False)
        the_table.set_fontsize(font_size)
        the_table.scale(1, 1.5) # Add some padding height

        # Remove table borders (cells)
        for key, cell in the_table.get_celld().items():
            cell.set_linewidth(0)

    # Ensure no grid lines on the plot
    ax.grid(False)

    # Text Overlap Prevention
    # Use layout engine to spacing (only if compatible, otherwise tight_layout with padding)
    # Text Overlap Prevention
    # Use layout engine to spacing (only if compatible, otherwise tight_layout with padding)
    # fig.tight_layout() removed to avoid GridSpec warnings with Tables

    return fig


==================================================
FILE: glow/plotting/histogram.py

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import seaborn as sns
import numpy as np
from matplotlib import gridspec
from matplotlib.ticker import ScalarFormatter, NullFormatter, MaxNLocator, LogLocator, LogFormatterMathtext, FuncFormatter

def _gaussian_kernel1d(sigma: float, radius: int | None = None) -> np.ndarray:
    if sigma is None or sigma <= 0:
        return np.array([1.0])
    if radius is None:
        radius = int(np.ceil(3 * float(sigma)))
    x = np.arange(-radius, radius + 1)
    k = np.exp(-(x ** 2) / (2 * float(sigma) ** 2))
    k /= k.sum()
    return k


def _smooth_1d(y: np.ndarray, sigma: float) -> np.ndarray:
    k = _gaussian_kernel1d(sigma)
    if k.size == 1:
        return y
    return np.convolve(y, k, mode="same")


def _histogram_curve(values: np.ndarray, bins: int, log_scale: bool) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
    values = np.asarray(values)
    values = values[np.isfinite(values)]
    if log_scale:
        values = values[values > 0]
    if values.size == 0:
        return np.array([]), np.array([])

    if log_scale:
        valid_vals = values[values > 0]
        if len(valid_vals) == 0:
             return np.array([]), np.array([])

        # Robust min/max: Clamp min to 0.01 to avoid extreme scaling from artifacts/noise
        vmin = max(valid_vals.min(), 0.01)
        vmax = valid_vals.max()

        if vmin >= vmax:
             vmax = vmin * 10

        edges = np.logspace(np.log10(vmin), np.log10(vmax), int(bins) + 1)
    else:
        edges = np.linspace(values.min(), values.max(), int(bins) + 1)

    counts, edges = np.histogram(values, bins=edges)
    if log_scale:
        # For log-spaced edges, the geometric mean is the correct "visual center" on a log axis
        # centers = sqrt(edge[i] * edge[i+1])
        centers = np.sqrt(edges[:-1] * edges[1:])
    else:
        centers = 0.5 * (edges[:-1] + edges[1:])
    return centers, counts.astype(float)


def plot_1d_histogram(
    experiment,
    channel,
    hue=None,
    scale='log',
    bins=100,
    color=None,
    legend_cols=None,
    legend_font_size=10,
    title=None,
    smooth_sigma: float = 2.0,
    normalization: str = "total",
    show_yaxis: bool = False,
):
    """
    Generates a 1D Ridge Plot (stacked histograms) if hue is provided.
    Includes a statistics table matching the visual style of the reference.
    """
    df = experiment.data
    log_scale = (scale == 'log')

    norm = (normalization or "").lower().strip()
    if norm in {"none", "original", "raw"}:
        norm = "original"
    elif norm in {"modal", "mode"}:
        norm = "modal"
    elif norm in {"total", "count", "total_count", "area", "density"}:
        norm = "total"
    else:
        # Backward-safe default that matches KDE-style behavior
        norm = "total"

    # If no hue, simple histogram (Individual Plots)
    if not hue or hue not in df.columns:
        fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(8, 6))

        c = color if color is not None else 'gray'
        if log_scale:
            ax.set_xscale('log')

        x, y = _histogram_curve(df[channel].values, bins=bins, log_scale=log_scale)
        if x.size > 0:
            if norm == "total":
                s = y.sum()
                y = y / s if s > 0 else y
            elif norm == "modal":
                m = y.max()
                y = y / m if m > 0 else y

            y_s = _smooth_1d(y, smooth_sigma)
            # Use ax.fill() with explicit polygon vertices for robust rendering
            # This handles log scale and figure resizing better than fill_between
            poly_x = np.concatenate([[x[0]], x, [x[-1]]])
            poly_y = np.concatenate([[0], y_s, [0]])
            ax.fill(poly_x, poly_y, alpha=0.5, color=c, zorder=1)
            ax.plot(x, y_s, color=c, linewidth=2, zorder=2)
            ax.set_ylim(bottom=0)

        if not show_yaxis:
            ax.set_yticks([])
            ax.set_ylabel("")

        if title:
            ax.set_title(title)
        else:
            ax.set_title(f"Histogram of {channel}")
        return fig, None

    # Ridge Plot Logic
    groups = sorted(df[hue].unique())
    n_groups = len(groups)

    if n_groups == 0:
        return plt.figure(), None

    # Determine columns to return in stats data
    if legend_cols is None:
        legend_cols = [hue, "Mean"]

    # Calculate stats for the table
    # We will return this as a list of dicts suitable for pd.DataFrame construction
    stats_data = []

    for g in groups:
        sub = df[df[hue] == g]
        row_dict = {}

        for col_key in legend_cols:
            if col_key == "Mean":
                vals = sub[channel]
                if log_scale:
                     v = vals[vals > 0]
                     mean_val = np.exp(np.log(v).mean()) if len(v) > 0 else 0
                else:
                    mean_val = vals.mean()
                row_dict["Mean"] = f"{mean_val:.2f}"

            elif col_key == "Median":
                 median_val = sub[channel].median()
                 row_dict["Median"] = f"{median_val:.2f}"

            elif col_key == "Count":
                 row_dict["Count"] = str(len(sub))

            elif col_key == hue:
                row_dict[hue] = str(g)

            elif col_key in df.columns:
                # Try to get unique value
                u_vals = sub[col_key].unique()
                if len(u_vals) == 1:
                    row_dict[col_key] = str(u_vals[0])
                else:
                    row_dict[col_key] = "Mixed"
            else:
                row_dict[col_key] = "-"

        # Ensure Hue column is present for mapping/coloring in UI if needed, even if not in legend_cols
        if hue not in row_dict:
            row_dict[hue] = str(g)

        stats_data.append(row_dict)

    # Layout
    plt.rcParams.update({'font.size': 10})

    # If y-axis is shown, reduce overlap to prevent tick collisions
    h_space = -0.1 if not show_yaxis else 0.05

    # Responsive height
    fig = plt.figure(figsize=(8, max(4, n_groups * 0.8)))
    gs = gridspec.GridSpec(n_groups, 1, hspace=h_space)

    palette = sns.color_palette("bright", n_groups)

    # Determine x limits
    if log_scale:
        valid_data = df[df[channel] > 0][channel]
        if len(valid_data) > 0:
            x_min = 10**(np.floor(np.log10(valid_data.min())))

            # User Request: Minimal x axis value should be 10^0 = 1.0
            if x_min < 1.0:
                x_min = 1.0

            x_max = 10**(np.ceil(np.log10(valid_data.max())))
        else:
            x_min, x_max = 1.0, 1000 # Default fallback >= 1.0
    else:
        x_min = df[channel].min()
        x_max = df[channel].max()

    axes = []
    y_max_global = 0.0
    cached_curves: list[tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = []

    # Precompute curves so we can enforce consistent y limits across rows
    for group in groups:
        subset = df[df[hue] == group]
        x, y = _histogram_curve(subset[channel].values, bins=bins, log_scale=log_scale)
        if x.size == 0:
            cached_curves.append((x, y))
            continue

        if norm == "total":
            s = y.sum()
            y = y / s if s > 0 else y
        elif norm == "modal":
            m = y.max()
            y = y / m if m > 0 else y

        y = _smooth_1d(y, smooth_sigma)
        cached_curves.append((x, y))
        if y.size > 0:
            y_max_global = max(y_max_global, float(np.nanmax(y)))

    for i, group in enumerate(groups):
        ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[i])
        axes.append(ax)
        ax.set_zorder(n_groups - i)

        color = palette[i]

        x, y = cached_curves[i]
        if x.size > 0:
            # Fill with alpha, outline solid with same color
            ax.fill_between(x, y, color=color, alpha=0.6, zorder=2)
            # Thicker outline (3.0 -> 3.5 to be safe/bold)
            ax.plot(x, y, color=color, linewidth=3.5, zorder=3)

        # Aesthetics
        ax.set_ylabel("")
        if show_yaxis:
            ax.spines['left'].set_visible(True)
            ax.yaxis.set_major_locator(MaxNLocator(nbins=3, prune='upper'))
            ax.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ScalarFormatter(useOffset=False))
            ax.yaxis.get_offset_text().set_visible(False)
            ax.tick_params(axis='y', which='both', left=True, labelleft=True, labelsize=8)
        else:
            ax.set_yticks([])
            ax.yaxis.set_major_formatter(NullFormatter())
            ax.yaxis.set_minor_formatter(NullFormatter())
            ax.spines['left'].set_visible(False)

        ax.spines['top'].set_visible(False)
        ax.spines['right'].set_visible(False)
        ax.grid(False)
        ax.patch.set_alpha(0)

        # X Axis Limits
        ax.set_xlim(x_min, x_max)
        if y_max_global > 0:
            ax.set_ylim(0, y_max_global * 1.3)

        # Log Scale Configuration
        if log_scale:
            ax.set_xscale('log')
            # Explicitly set locators
            # Major ticks: Powers of 10
            ax.xaxis.set_major_locator(LogLocator(base=10.0, numticks=15))
            # Minor ticks: 2-9 multiples. Use list explicitly
            ax.xaxis.set_minor_locator(LogLocator(base=10.0, subs=[2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9], numticks=100))

            # Formatter: Explicit FuncFormatter for safe x10^z labeling
            def log_10_fmt(x, pos):
                # Avoid log(0)
                if x <= 0: return ""
                log_val = np.log10(x)
                if np.isclose(log_val, np.round(log_val)):
                    return f"$10^{{{int(np.round(log_val))}}}$"
                return ""

            ax.xaxis.set_major_formatter(FuncFormatter(log_10_fmt))
            # Force null formatter on minor ticks to ensure no labels appear
            ax.xaxis.set_minor_formatter(NullFormatter())

        # Subplot Specific Visibility
        if i < n_groups - 1:
            ax.set_xlabel("")
            # Hide everything for upper plots
            ax.tick_params(axis='x', which='both', bottom=False, labelbottom=False)
            ax.spines['bottom'].set_visible(False)
            # Faint guide line
            ax.axhline(0, color='gray', linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.3)
        else:
            # Bottom Plot: Show Axis and Ticks
            ax.set_xlabel(channel)

            # Major Ticks: Visible, Labeled
            ax.tick_params(axis='x', which='major', bottom=True, direction='out', length=6, width=1.2, colors='black', labelbottom=True, pad=5)
            # Minor Ticks: Visible, Unlabeled. Explicitly force labelbottom=False again
            ax.tick_params(axis='x', which='minor', bottom=True, direction='out', length=3, width=0.8, colors='black', labelbottom=False)

            ax.spines['bottom'].set_visible(True)
            ax.spines['bottom'].set_linewidth(1.0)
            ax.spines['bottom'].set_color('black')

            # Solid baseline
            ax.axhline(0, color='black', linewidth=1.0)

            # Solid baseline
            ax.axhline(0, color='black', linewidth=1.0)

    plt.tight_layout()
    return fig, stats_data


def plot_ridgeline_matplotlib(
    experiment,
    channel,
    hue=None,
    scale='log',
    bins=100,
    color=None,
    offset=0.5,
    smooth_sigma: float = 2.0,
    normalization: str = "total",
    show_yaxis: bool = False,
    legend_cols=None,
    show_replicates: bool = False,
    replicate_col: str | None = None,
):
    """
    Generates a Single-Axis Ridgeline Plot (Matplotlib) with adjustable offset.
    """
    df = experiment.data
    log_scale = (scale == 'log')

    norm = (normalization or "").lower().strip()
    if norm in {"none", "original", "raw"}:
        norm = "original"
    elif norm in {"modal", "mode"}:
        norm = "modal"
    elif norm in {"total", "count", "total_count", "area", "density"}:
        norm = "total"
    else:
        norm = "total"

    groups = sorted(df[hue].unique()) if hue and hue in df.columns else [None]
    n_groups = len(groups)

    # If no hue, treat as single group
    if not hue or hue not in df.columns:
        groups = [None]
        n_groups = 1

    # Determine columns to return in stats data
    if legend_cols is None:
        legend_cols = [hue, "Mean"] if hue else ["Mean"]

    # Calculate stats for the table
    stats_data = []

    for g in groups:
        if g is None:
            sub = df
        else:
            sub = df[df[hue] == g]

        row_dict = {}

        for col_key in legend_cols:
            if col_key == "Mean":
                vals = sub[channel]
                if log_scale:
                     v = vals[vals > 0]
                     mean_val = np.exp(np.log(v).mean()) if len(v) > 0 else 0
                else:
                    mean_val = vals.mean()
                row_dict["Mean"] = f"{mean_val:.2f}"

            elif col_key == "Median":
                 median_val = sub[channel].median()
                 row_dict["Median"] = f"{median_val:.2f}"

            elif col_key == "Count":
                 row_dict["Count"] = str(len(sub))

            elif col_key == hue:
                row_dict[hue] = str(g)

            elif col_key in df.columns:
                u_vals = sub[col_key].unique()
                if len(u_vals) == 1:
                    row_dict[col_key] = str(u_vals[0])
                else:
                    row_dict[col_key] = "Mixed"
            else:
                row_dict[col_key] = "-"

        if hue and hue not in row_dict:
            row_dict[hue] = str(g)

        stats_data.append(row_dict)

    # Layout - Single Axis
    plt.rcParams.update({'font.size': 10})

    # Height scales with number of groups AND offset to maintain consistent per-group visual height.
    # Total vertical span in units of "one histogram height" is approx 1 + (N-1)*offset.
    # We allocate more inches per unit height + overhead.
    effective_units = 1.0 + max(0, n_groups - 1) * offset

    # 2.0 inches base overhead (Axes labels, Tick labels, Title space)
    # 0.8 inches per effective histogram unit height
    # This ensures that if offset increases, total height increases proportionally.
    calc_height = 2.0 + (effective_units * 0.8)

    # Ensure min height of 3 inches
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(8, max(3, calc_height)))

    # Colors
    if n_groups > 1:
        palette = sns.color_palette("bright", n_groups)
    else:
        palette = [color if color else 'gray']

    # Determine x limits
    if log_scale:
        valid_data = df[df[channel] > 0][channel]
        if len(valid_data) > 0:
            x_min = 10**(np.floor(np.log10(valid_data.min())))

            # User Request: Minimal x axis value should be 10^0 = 1.0
            if x_min < 1.0:
                x_min = 1.0

            x_max = 10**(np.ceil(np.log10(valid_data.max())))
        else:
            x_min, x_max = 1.0, 1000 # Default fallback >= 1.0
    else:
        x_min = df[channel].min()
        x_max = df[channel].max()

    # Cached curves is now a list of lists of (x, y) tuples.
    # Structure: cached_curves[group_index] -> [ (x1,y1), (x2,y2), ... ]
    cached_curves = []
    y_max_global = 0.0

    # 1. Compute Curves and Global Max
    for group in groups:
        if group is not None:
             subset = df[df[hue] == group]
        else:
             subset = df # No hue

        group_curves = [] # List of (x,y)

        # Determine separate datasets to compute curves for (Replicates vs Aggregated)
        sub_datasets = []
        if show_replicates and replicate_col and replicate_col in subset.columns:
             # Find unique replicates
             reps = subset[replicate_col].unique()
             for r in reps:
                  sub_datasets.append(subset[subset[replicate_col] == r])
        else:
             sub_datasets.append(subset)

        for ds in sub_datasets:
            x, y = _histogram_curve(ds[channel].values, bins=bins, log_scale=log_scale)
            if x.size == 0:
                continue

            if norm == "total":
                s = y.sum()
                y = y / s if s > 0 else y
            elif norm == "modal":
                m = y.max()
                y = y / m if m > 0 else y

            y = _smooth_1d(y, smooth_sigma)
            if y.size > 0:
                 group_curves.append((x, y))
                 y_max_global = max(y_max_global, float(np.nanmax(y)))

        cached_curves.append(group_curves)

    if y_max_global <= 0:
        return fig, []

    # 2. Determine Offsets
    offset_step = y_max_global * offset

    # 3. Plot Loops (Single Axis)
    # Iterate Backwards (Top to Bottom) - Painter's Algorithm
    yticks = []
    yticklabels = []

    for i in range(n_groups):
        group = groups[i]
        curves_list = cached_curves[i] # This is a list of (x,y)
        c = palette[i] if n_groups > 1 else palette[0]

        # Base Y position
        # First sample at top, last at bottom
        base_y = (n_groups - 1 - i) * offset_step
        yticks.append(base_y)
        label_text = str(group) if group is not None else "All"
        yticklabels.append(label_text)

        # zorder: Lower Y (Front) -> Higher Z
        # We start z at high value for top plot.
        # Actually in Painter's algo with fill, we draw from back to front usually?
        # But here we are using fill_between, overlapping.
        # If we draw top one first (i=0, base_y=high), then draw next (i=1, base_y=lower),
        # the next one might overlap the top one's bottom.
        # Wait, usually we want the "Front" (Lower Y) to cover the "Back" (Higher Y).
        # So we should draw Higher Y first (Back) -> Lower Y last (Front).
        # i=0 is the Top (Higher Y). i=N is Bottom (Lower Y).
        # So we should draw loop i=0 first, then i=1.
        # BUT zorder overrides draw order.
        # We want Bottom one (i=N-1) to have HIGHER zorder than Top one (i=0) so it covers the "feet" of the top one?
        # Standard Ridgeline: The one in 'front' (lower on screen) covers the ones behind it.
        # So i (index) increases -> goes down screen -> should be on top.
        # So z should increase with i.

        # Current code was: z = (n_groups - i) * 2.
        # If i=0 (Top): z = N*2 (High Z).
        # If i=N (Bottom): z = 0 (Low Z).
        # This puts the TOP curve ON TOP of the bottom curve.
        # If the Top curve has a long tail going down, it will cover the peak of the curve below it.
        # Usually Ridgeline is the opposite: Bottom curves cover the tails of Top curves.
        # Let's fix this logic while we are here if it was wrong, or keep consistency.
        # "First sample on top" was requested.
        # Usually Ridgelines look like mountains. The closer mountain (lower) covers the further one (higher).
        # So Lower Y (Higher Index) should have Higher Z.
        z_group = (i + 1) * 2 # i=0 -> 2. i=N -> Higher.

        # Wait, the user specifically asked "Change order... first sample on top".
        # If I draw Bottom (Last Sample) ON TOP of Top (First Sample), then the Last Sample is "In Front".
        # Okay, I will stick to what seems to be the standard "Mountain" view z-ordering.
        # i increases -> Y decreases -> appearing "closer" -> Higher Z.

        # User explicitly requested: "make sure the histograms at the bottom are overlaid on top of histograms above it."
        # i=0 (Top, High Y). i=N-1 (Bottom, Low Y).
        # We want Bottom to cover Top. So Z should increase with i.
        z_group = i * 2 + 2


        # Plot each replicate
        # If multiple replicates, they share the z_group but maybe we tweak slightly or use alpha?
        # If we just overlay them, order doesn't matter much between replicates of same group.

        for k, (x, y) in enumerate(curves_list):
            if x.size > 0:
                y_shifted = y + base_y

                is_replicate_view = (show_replicates and len(curves_list) > 1)

                # If showing replicates, use lower alpha for fill so we can see density
                # User request: Lower opacity (including outline) to better visualize replicates
                fill_alpha = 0.25 if is_replicate_view else 0.6

                # User request: Reduce outline width by 1 for both (was 2.0 -> now 1.0)
                gw = 1.0

                # If replicate view, also lower outline alpha to match
                # Standard view outline is usually solid (alpha=1.0)
                outline_alpha = 0.6 if is_replicate_view else 1.0

                # Fill
                ax.fill_between(x, base_y, y_shifted, color=c, alpha=fill_alpha, zorder=z_group)
                # Outline
                ax.plot(x, y_shifted, color=c, linewidth=gw, alpha=outline_alpha, zorder=z_group+1)

        # Baseline
        ax.axhline(base_y, color='gray', linewidth=0.5, alpha=0.3, zorder=-1)

    # 4. Axis Configuration

    # Y Axis: Show Group Labels
    ax.set_yticks(yticks)
    ax.set_yticklabels(yticklabels)
    ax.tick_params(axis='y', left=False) # Hide ticks

    # Clear Spines
    ax.spines['left'].set_visible(False)
    ax.spines['right'].set_visible(False)
    ax.spines['top'].set_visible(False)
    ax.grid(False)

    # X Axis Setup
    ax.set_xlim(x_min, x_max)
    # Limit calculation: (N-1)*offset + 1.2*height
    top_limit = ((n_groups-1) * offset_step) + y_max_global * 1.2
    ax.set_ylim(0, top_limit if top_limit > 0 else 1)

    if log_scale:
        ax.set_xscale('log')
        ax.xaxis.set_major_locator(LogLocator(base=10.0, numticks=15))
        ax.xaxis.set_minor_locator(LogLocator(base=10.0, subs=[2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9], numticks=100))

        def log_10_fmt(x, pos):
            if x <= 0: return ""
            log_val = np.log10(x)
            if np.isclose(log_val, np.round(log_val)):
                return f"$10^{{{int(np.round(log_val))}}}$"
            return ""

        ax.xaxis.set_major_formatter(FuncFormatter(log_10_fmt))
        ax.xaxis.set_minor_formatter(NullFormatter())

    ax.set_xlabel(channel)
    ax.spines['bottom'].set_visible(True)
    ax.spines['bottom'].set_color('black')
    ax.spines['bottom'].set_linewidth(1.0)

    # Ticks style
    ax.tick_params(axis='x', which='major', bottom=True, direction='out', length=6, width=1.2, colors='black', labelbottom=True, pad=5)
    ax.tick_params(axis='x', which='minor', bottom=True, direction='out', length=3, width=0.8, colors='black', labelbottom=False)

    # Add vertical line at left boundary
    ax.axvline(x_min, color='black', linewidth=1.0, zorder=1000)

    plt.tight_layout()
    return fig, stats_data

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from scipy import stats
from typing import Any, cast
import importlib.util
from glow.utils import safe_log10
try:
    import statsmodels.stats.multitest as mt
except ImportError:
    mt = None

# Try to import cytoflow, but don't fail if not present
if importlib.util.find_spec("cytoflow") is not None:
    CYTOFLOW_AVAILABLE = True
else:
    CYTOFLOW_AVAILABLE = False

def get_channels(experiment):
    """Returns a list of available channels in the experiment."""
    # Priority: explicit property -> data columns -> empty
    try:
        if hasattr(experiment, 'channels'):
             return sorted(experiment.channels)
    except Exception:
        pass # Fallback if cytoflow property crashes

    # Fallback if channels attribute is missing but data exists
    if hasattr(experiment, 'data'):
        # Filter for numeric columns
        return [c for c in experiment.data.columns if pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(experiment.data[c])]

    return []

def get_conditions(experiment):
    """Returns a list of available conditions (metadata columns) in the experiment."""
    try:
        if hasattr(experiment, 'conditions'):
            if isinstance(experiment.conditions, dict):
                return sorted(experiment.conditions.keys())
            return sorted(experiment.conditions) # if list
    except Exception:
        pass # Fallback if cytoflow property crashes

    # Fallback
    if hasattr(experiment, 'data'):
         return sorted([c for c in experiment.data.columns if not pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(experiment.data[c])])

    return []

def filter_experiment(experiment, condition, values):
    """
    Filters experiment to keep only events where 'condition' is in 'values'.
    Returns a new experiment-like object (or filters the dataframe).
    """
    data = experiment.data
    if condition not in data.columns:
        return experiment

    subset_df = data[data[condition].isin(values)].copy()

    # Return a new object of the same type if possible, or a basic wrapper
    # Using the same class if possible
    cls = type(experiment)
    try:
        # Check if constructor accepts just data, or try to mimic
        # Pass metadata if available to preserve channel/condition definitions
        if hasattr(experiment, 'metadata'):
            return cls(subset_df, metadata=experiment.metadata)
        return cls(subset_df)
    except Exception:
        # Fallback to a simple container
        class SimpleExp:
            def __init__(self, d, orig=None):
                self.data = d
                self._orig = orig

            @property
            def channels(self):
                # Try to inherit from original experiment to ensure consistency
                if self._orig and hasattr(self._orig, 'channels'):
                    return [c for c in self._orig.channels if c in self.data.columns]

                # Robust fallback: Numeric but NOT Boolean (excludes flags like 'cells')
                # Also exclude common metadata names explicitly in case they are numeric (e.g. Replicates)
                excludes = ['cells', 'singlets', 'Is_Live', 'Is_Singlet', 'LiveGate', 'SingletGate',
                            'Replicate', 'replicate', 'Experiment_Name', 'Experiment Name', 'Time', 'time',
                            'Concentration', 'Timepoint', 'Group', 'group']

                return [c for c in self.data.columns if pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(self.data[c])
                        and not pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(self.data[c])
                        and c not in excludes]

            @property
            def conditions(self):
                # Try to inherit
                if self._orig and hasattr(self._orig, 'conditions'):
                    return self._orig.conditions

                return {c: "category" for c in self.data.columns if c not in self.channels}

            def clone(self):
                return SimpleExp(self.data.copy(), orig=self._orig)

        return SimpleExp(subset_df, orig=experiment)

def get_point_density(x, y, bins=100, log_x=False, log_y=False):
    """
    Calculates density for scatter plots using 2D histogram lookup.
    """
    # Filter NaNs
    mask = np.isfinite(x) & np.isfinite(y)
    # We use safe checking via safe_log10 implicitly if we wanted, but to keep logic similar:
    # If we use safe_log10, it returns NaNs for <= 0.
    # But we want to filter them out for histogram2d.

    # Original logic explicitly removed delicate values.
    # Let's keep the explicit filter as it's efficient for histogram2d which needs clean data.
    if log_x:
        mask &= (x > 0)
    if log_y:
        mask &= (y > 0)

    x = x[mask]
    y = y[mask]

    if len(x) == 0:
        return np.array([]), mask

    # Transform to log space for density calculation if needed
    # (Matches visual density on log-scaled plots)
    # Using safe_log10 adds an extra layer of safety even if filtered
    tx = safe_log10(x) if log_x else x
    ty = safe_log10(y) if log_y else y

    # Histogram
    hh, locx, locy = np.histogram2d(tx, ty, bins=bins)

    # Compute density for each point
    # Digitizing coordinates
    # np.searchsorted gives index to insert.
    # Bin i is [edges[i], edges[i+1]).
    # searchsorted returns i if x < edges[i].
    # We want bin index.

    ix = np.searchsorted(locx, tx) - 1
    iy = np.searchsorted(locy, ty) - 1

    # Clip indices
    ix = np.clip(ix, 0, bins-1)
    iy = np.clip(iy, 0, bins-1)

    # Lookup density
    z = hh[ix, iy]

    # Log normalize
    z = np.log1p(z)

    # Normalize 0..1
    if z.max() > z.min():
        z = (z - z.min()) / (z.max() - z.min())
    else:
        z = np.zeros_like(z)

    return z, mask

def calculate_gated_mean(df, gate_channel, value_channel, threshold, statistic='mean'):
    """
    Calculates the mean of value_channel for events where gate_channel > threshold.
    If threshold is a tuple/list of length 2, it is treated as (min, max).
    If threshold is single value, it is treated as lower bound (val > threshold).
    """
    if isinstance(threshold, (tuple, list)) and len(threshold) == 2:
        low, high = threshold
        gated_df = df[(df[gate_channel] >= low) & (df[gate_channel] <= high)]
    else:
        gated_df = df[df[gate_channel] > threshold]

    if len(gated_df) == 0:
        return np.nan

    vals = gated_df[value_channel]

    if statistic == 'mean':
        return vals.mean()
    elif statistic == 'median':
        return vals.median()
    elif statistic == 'geom_mean':
        # Geometric mean (ignore <= 0)
        pos_vals = vals[vals > 0]
        if len(pos_vals) == 0:
            return np.nan
        return np.exp(np.log(pos_vals).mean())

    return np.nan

def get_percentile_threshold(df, channel, percentile=99.9):
    """
    Returns the value at the given percentile for the channel.
    percentile should be 0-100.
    """
    if channel not in df.columns:
        return 0
    return np.percentile(df[channel].dropna(), percentile)

def get_percentile_of_value(df, channel, value):
    """
    Returns the percentile (0-100) of a given raw value in the distribution of the channel.
    """
    from scipy import stats
    if channel not in df.columns:
        return 0
    vals = df[channel].dropna()
    if len(vals) == 0:
        return 0
    # percentileofscore returns 0-100
    try:
        return stats.percentileofscore(vals, value)
    except Exception:
        return 0

def calculate_significance(data1, data2):
    """
    Calculates independent t-test between two arrays.
    Returns (p_value, star_label, fold_change).
    Fold change is mean(data2) / mean(data1).
    """
    d1 = np.array(data1)
    d2 = np.array(data2)
    # Remove NaNs
    d1 = d1[np.isfinite(d1)]
    d2 = d2[np.isfinite(d2)]

    if len(d1) < 2 or len(d2) < 2:
        return 1.0, "ns", 1.0

    # Calculate Fold Change (Mean based)
    m1 = np.mean(d1)
    m2 = np.mean(d2)
    fc = m2 / m1 if m1 != 0 else 0

    try:
        res = stats.ttest_ind(d1, d2, equal_var=False)
        p = float(cast(Any, res)[1])
        if p < 0.001:
            sig = "***"
        elif p < 0.01:
            sig = "**"
        elif p < 0.05:
            sig = "*"
        else:
            sig = "ns"
        return p, sig, fc
    except Exception:
        return 1.0, "ns", fc

def get_significance_label(p):
    if p < 0.001:
        return "***"
    elif p < 0.01:
        return "**"
    elif p < 0.05:
        return "*"
    else:
        return "ns"

def adjust_pvalues(p_values, method='t-test'):
    """
    Adjusts a list of p-values for multiple testing.
    Methods: 't-test' (none), 'bonferroni', 'fdr'
    Returns adjusted p-values.
    """
    if not p_values:
        return []

    if method in ['t-test', 'none'] or mt is None:
        return p_values

    method_map = {
        'bonferroni': 'bonferroni',
        'fdr': 'fdr_bh', # False Discovery Rate (Benjamini-Hochberg)
        'fdr_bh': 'fdr_bh'
    }

    m = method_map.get(method.lower(), 'fdr_bh')

    try:
        _, pvals_corrected, _, _ = mt.multipletests(p_values, method=m)
        return pvals_corrected
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error in p-value adjustment: {e}")
        return p_values

import argparse

# Ensure we can import modules from the current package context if run directly
# Logic: If this script is run, add parent dir to sys.path or use relative imports if run as module
# Ideally correct usage: python -m glow.pipeline.runner
from glow.data_loading.loader import DataLoader
from glow.gating.manager import GatingManager
from glow.active_learning.learner import ActiveLearner

def run_base_pipeline(file_list_path=None, input_dir=None, file_paths=None, rename_strategy='folder_name', output_dir='data/processed_fcs', cell_line=None):
    """
    Main entry point for the base pipeline logic.
    """
    loader = DataLoader()

    # 1. Load Paths
    final_paths = []
    if file_list_path:
        final_paths.extend(loader.parse_file_paths_from_text(file_list_path))
    if input_dir:
        final_paths.extend(loader.scan_directory(input_dir))
    if file_paths:
        final_paths.extend(file_paths)

    if not final_paths:
        print("Error: No valid file paths provided.")
        return None

    # 2. Rename & Prepare
    print("Preparing files (renaming and organizing)...")
    # Using 'prepare_fcs_files' as defined in data_loader.py (wrapper)
    metadata = loader.prepare_fcs_files(final_paths, rename_strategy=rename_strategy, output_dir=output_dir)

    # 3. Import to Cytoflow
    print("Loading files into Cytoflow Experiment...")
    experiment = loader.load_as_cytoflow_experiment(metadata)

    if experiment is None:
        print("Failed to load experiment.")
        return None

    # 4. Gating Strategy
    gater = GatingManager()
    learner = ActiveLearner()

    # Apply Learned Knowledge if available
    if cell_line:
        print(f"Checking for learned gates for cell line: {cell_line}...")

        # Singlet Gate (Learned)
        singlet_vertices = learner.get_learned_gate(cell_line, "Singlet")
        if singlet_vertices:
            print("Applying learned Singlet gate...")
            # Note: channel names here are hardcoded for now, but should ideally come from learned metadata too or user config
            experiment = gater.apply_manual_gate(experiment, "_temp_singlet_mask_learned", "FSC-A", "FSC-H", singlet_vertices)
        else:
             # Fallback to auto
             # print("Attempting to filter duplicates (Singlet finding)...")
             # experiment = gater.remove_duplicates(experiment)
             pass

        # Live Gate (Learned)
        live_vertices = learner.get_learned_gate(cell_line, "Live")
        if live_vertices:
            print("Applying learned Live gate...")
            experiment = gater.apply_manual_gate(experiment, "_temp_live_mask_learned", "FSC-A", "SSC-A", live_vertices)
            print("Learned Live gate applied.")
            # Skip Auto Gating if learned gate is applied?
            # Or maybe we return both and let user decide in UI.
            # For pipeline automation, we prefer the learned one.
            return experiment

    # B. Auto Live Gating (Fallback)
    print("Applying Automatic Live/Dead Gating (GMM)...")
    # Assuming FSC-A and SSC-A are present. This might fail if channels differ.
    try:
        experiment = gater.auto_gate_live_cells(experiment, x_channel="FSC-A", y_channel="SSC-A")
        print("Auto gating applied. Check '_temp_live_labels' condition.")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Warning: Auto gating failed (check channel names?): {e}")

    print("Pipeline execution finished.")
    return experiment

if __name__ == "__main__":
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Glow Base Pipeline")
    parser.add_argument("--file_list", help="Path to a text file containing list of FCS file paths")
    parser.add_argument("--input_dir", help="Directory to recursively search for FCS files")
    parser.add_argument("--output_dir", default="processed_fcs", help="Directory to save renamed files")
    parser.add_argument("--rename", default="folder_name", choices=["folder_name", "keep"], help="Renaming strategy")

    args = parser.parse_args()

    if args.file_list or args.input_dir:
        run_base_pipeline(file_list_path=args.file_list, input_dir=args.input_dir, output_dir=args.output_dir, rename_strategy=args.rename)
    else:
        print("Please provide --file_list or --input_dir to run via CLI.")

import streamlit as st
import pickle
import uuid

def add_plot_to_canvas(fig, title: str):
    """Store a plot in Streamlit session state for display in the Canvas tab.

    Stores the figure object directly to allow for dynamic resizing and updates.
    Returns True if added successfully.
    """

    if "canvas_plots" not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.canvas_plots = {}

    try:
        from matplotlib.figure import Figure

        # Check for Matplotlib Figure
        if isinstance(fig, Figure):
            # Create a deep copy to ensure the figure persists even if the original is closed
            try:
                # Force draw to ensure artists are initialized
                fig.canvas.draw()
                fig_copy = pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(fig))
            except Exception:
                # Fallback if pickling fails (e.g. complex backends), though less safe vs plt.close()
                fig_copy = fig
                # Tag as direct reference to prevent closing
                setattr(fig_copy, "_glow_is_direct_ref", True)

            # Store Matplotlib Figure
            # Use UUID as key to allow multiple plots with same title/content
            plot_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
            st.session_state.canvas_plots[plot_id] = {
                "type": "matplotlib",
                "title": title,
                "fig": fig_copy,
                "settings": {"width_cols": 1, "font_scale": 1.0, "dot_scale": 1.0}
            }
            return True

        import plotly.graph_objects as go
        # Check for Plotly Figure
        if isinstance(fig, go.Figure):
            # Store Plotly figure
            plot_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
            st.session_state.canvas_plots[plot_id] = {
                "type": "plotly",
                "title": title,
                "data": fig,
                "settings": {"width_cols": 1, "font_scale": 1.0, "dot_scale": 1.0}
            }
            return True
    except ImportError:
        pass
    except Exception as e:
        st.error(f"Failed to process plot: {e}")
        return False

    st.warning(f"Plot type {type(fig)} not supported for Canvas storage.")
    return False


def get_canvas_plots():
    return st.session_state.get("canvas_plots", {})


def add_asperatio_to_canvas(data, title: str):
    """Store asperatio data in Streamlit session state for display in the Canvas tab."""
    if "canvas_plots" not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.canvas_plots = {}

    plot_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
    st.session_state.canvas_plots[plot_id] = {
        "type": "asperatio",
        "title": title,
        "data": data,
        "settings": {"width_cols": 1, "font_scale": 1.0, "dot_scale": 1.0}
    }


def remove_plot_from_canvas(plot_id: str):
    if "canvas_plots" in st.session_state and plot_id in st.session_state.canvas_plots:
        del st.session_state.canvas_plots[plot_id]


def move_plot_in_canvas(plot_id: str, direction: str):
    """Move a plot up or down in the canvas order."""
    if "canvas_plots" not in st.session_state:
        return

    plots = st.session_state.canvas_plots
    keys = list(plots.keys())

    if plot_id not in keys:
        return

    idx = keys.index(plot_id)
    new_idx = idx - 1 if direction == "up" else idx + 1

    if 0 <= new_idx < len(keys):
        # Create a new dictionary with swapped retrieval order
        keys[idx], keys[new_idx] = keys[new_idx], keys[idx]
        st.session_state.canvas_plots = {k: plots[k] for k in keys}

import json
import os
import datetime
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

class ActiveLearningLogger:
    def __init__(self, storage_dir="data/active_learning_data"):
        self.storage_dir = storage_dir
        if not os.path.exists(self.storage_dir):
            os.makedirs(self.storage_dir)

    def log_gate_feedback(self, sample_name, cell_line, x_channel, y_channel, gate_type, gate_params, user_decision):
        """
        Logs user interaction for active learning.
        """
        entry = {
            "timestamp": datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "sample_name": sample_name,
            "cell_line": cell_line,
            "channels": [x_channel, y_channel],
            "gate_type": gate_type,
            "gate_params": self._serialize(gate_params),
            "user_decision": user_decision
        }

        filename = f"{self.storage_dir}/feedback_{datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%Y%m%d')}.jsonl"
        try:
            with open(filename, "a") as f:
                f.write(json.dumps(entry) + "\n")
        except (PermissionError, OSError) as e:
            print(f"Active Learning Logging Failed: {e}")

    def save_labeled_batch(self, X, y, source="user_labeling"):
        """
        Saves a batch of labeled data (features + labels) to a Parquet file.
        Useful for accumulating training data for the neural network.

        Args:
            X (np.ndarray): N x 2 array of features (Log_FSC, Log_SSC).
            y (np.ndarray): N array of labels (0/1).
            source (str): Metadata describing where this data came from.
        """
        timestamp = datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')
        filename = os.path.join(self.storage_dir, f"labeled_batch_{timestamp}.parquet")

        df = pd.DataFrame(X, columns=["Log_FSC", "Log_SSC"])
        df["Label"] = y
        df["Source"] = source
        df["Timestamp"] = datetime.datetime.now().isoformat()

        try:
            df.to_parquet(filename, index=False)
            print(f"Saved labeled batch to {filename} ({len(df)} samples)")
            return filename
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Failed to save labeled batch: {e}")
            return None

    def load_feedback(self):
        """
        Loads all feedback from the storage directory.
        Returns a list of dicts.
        """
        all_feedback = []
        if not os.path.exists(self.storage_dir):
            return all_feedback

        for filename in sorted(os.listdir(self.storage_dir)):
            if filename.startswith("feedback_") and filename.endswith(".jsonl"):
                full_path = os.path.join(self.storage_dir, filename)
                with open(full_path, 'r') as f:
                    for line in f:
                        if line.strip():
                            try:
                                all_feedback.append(json.loads(line))
                            except json.JSONDecodeError:
                                pass
        return all_feedback

    def load_all_labeled_data(self):
        """
        Loads and concatenates all saving labeled batches.
        Returns X, y arrays.
        """
        dfs = []
        if not os.path.exists(self.storage_dir):
             return None, None

        for filename in sorted(os.listdir(self.storage_dir)):
            if filename.startswith("labeled_batch_") and filename.endswith(".parquet"):
                try:
                    df = pd.read_parquet(os.path.join(self.storage_dir, filename))
                    dfs.append(df)
                except Exception as e:
                    print(f"Error reading {filename}: {e}")

        if not dfs:
            return None, None

        full_df = pd.concat(dfs, ignore_index=True)
        X = full_df[["Log_FSC", "Log_SSC"]].values
        y = full_df["Label"].values
        return X, y

    def _serialize(self, obj):
        """Helper to handle numpy types for JSON"""
        if isinstance(obj, np.ndarray):
            return obj.tolist()
        if isinstance(obj, np.integer):
            return int(obj)
        if isinstance(obj, np.floating):
            return float(obj)
        return obj
