import streamlit as st
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import plotly.graph_objects as go
import importlib
import glow.active_learning.learner
from glow.active_learning.learner import ActiveLearner

def get_active_learner(model_type='gating'):
    key = f"active_learner_{model_type}"

    # Ensure module is up to date if we are relying on recent changes
    # importlib.reload(glow.active_learning.learner)

    if key not in st.session_state:
        print(f"Initializing ActiveLearner for {model_type}")
        model_path = "data/gating_model_full.pkl" if model_type == 'gating' else "data/singlet_model_full.pkl"
        try:
             st.session_state[key] = ActiveLearner(model_path=model_path, model_type=model_type)
        except Exception as e:
             st.error(f"Failed to load ActiveLearner for {model_type}: {e}")
             return None

    # Auto-heal check (optional, can be removed if stable)
    learner = st.session_state[key]
    if not hasattr(learner, 'reset_model'):
        importlib.reload(glow.active_learning.learner)
        from glow.active_learning.learner import ActiveLearner as NewActiveLearner
        st.session_state[key] = NewActiveLearner(model_path=learner.model_path, model_type=learner.model_type)

    return st.session_state[key]

def render_reset_controls(model_type='gating'):
    """
    Renders a button to reset the model to its initial state.
    """
    st.divider()
    label = "Start Over (Reset Model)"
    if model_type == 'singlet':
        label = "Start Over (Reset Singlet Model)"

    if st.button(label, help="Reverts the model to the initial pre-trained state. All interactive learning will be lost.", key=f"reset_btn_{model_type}"):
        learner = get_active_learner(model_type)
        if learner:
            with st.spinner("Resetting model..."):
                success = learner.reset_model()

            if success:
                st.success("Model reset successfully!")
                # Clear UI state - slightly drastic but safe
                # We should only clear relevant state
                if model_type == 'gating':
                    if 'uncertain_indices' in st.session_state:
                        del st.session_state.uncertain_indices
                    if 'gated_experiment' in st.session_state:
                        del st.session_state.gated_experiment
                elif model_type == 'singlet':
                    if 'doublets_removed' in st.session_state:
                        del st.session_state.doublets_removed

                # Force refresh
                st.session_state.plot_id = st.session_state.get('plot_id', 0) + 1
                st.rerun()
            else:
                st.error("Could not reset model. Base model not found.")

def render_uncertainty_controls(experiment, x_col, y_col, model_type='gating'):
    """
    Renders the button to Release the Hounds (find uncertainties).
    """
    # if model_type != 'gating': return # Not implementing singlet uncertainty viz yet

    st.markdown("### 🧠 Active Learning")
    col1, col2 = st.columns(2)

    with col1:
        if st.button("🔍 Find Confusion", key=f"find_conf_{model_type}"):
            with st.spinner("Querying model..."):
                learner = get_active_learner(model_type)
                if not learner:
                    return

                df = experiment.data

                # Data Prep (Assume linear UI -> log Model)
                is_x_log = (df[x_col].min() < 0) or (df[x_col].max() < 10)
                vals_x = df[x_col].values
                vals_y = df[y_col].values

                if not is_x_log:
                    vals_x = np.log1p(vals_x)
                    vals_y = np.log1p(vals_y)

                if model_type == 'singlet':
                     query_df = pd.DataFrame({
                        "Log_FSC_A": vals_x,
                        "Log_FSC_H": vals_y
                    }, index=df.index)
                else:
                    query_df = pd.DataFrame({
                        "Log_FSC": vals_x,
                        "Log_SSC": vals_y
                    }, index=df.index)

                _, indices = learner.query_uncertainty(query_df, n_instances=5)
                st.session_state.uncertain_indices = indices
                st.rerun()

    with col2:
        if st.button("Clear Highlights", key=f"clear_highlights_{model_type}"):
            st.session_state.uncertain_indices = []
            st.rerun()

def get_uncertainty_trace(experiment, x_col, y_col):
    """
    Returns a Plotly trace for the uncertain points.
    """
    if 'uncertain_indices' not in st.session_state:
        return None

    indices = st.session_state.uncertain_indices
    # Robust check for empty list or empty numpy array
    if indices is None or len(indices) == 0:
        return None

    df = experiment.data

    try:
        # Filter indices that might be out of bounds if data changed
        valid_indices = [i for i in indices if i in df.index]

        # NOTE: indices returned by the learner are dataframe index labels (not positional)
        subset = df.loc[valid_indices]

        trace = go.Scatter(
            x=subset[x_col],
            y=subset[y_col],
            mode='markers',
            marker=dict(
                size=16,
                color='rgba(255, 0, 0, 0.08)',
                symbol='circle',
                line=dict(width=6, color='red')
            ),
            # Store original dataframe index so clicks on this trace can be mapped
            customdata=subset.index.astype(str),
            name="Uncertainty",
            hovertemplate="Uncertain point<br>Click to label<extra></extra>",
            hoverlabel=dict(bgcolor='red', font_size=14)
        )
        return trace
    except Exception:
        return None

def render_labeling_controls(experiment, x_col, y_col, selection, plot_df=None, model_type='gating'):
    """
    Renders buttons to label the selected points.
    """
    if not selection or not selection.get('selection') or not selection['selection'].get('points'):
        return

    pts = selection['selection']['points']
    count = len(pts)
    if count == 0:
        return

    st.divider()
    st.markdown(f"**📝 Label {count} Selected Points**")
    st.caption("Tip: you can select any cells (not just red-circled) using box select or Shift+Click, then label them.")
    strength = st.slider(
        "Learning strength",
        min_value=1,
        max_value=30,
        value=10,
        step=1,
        help="Higher values up-weight your labels so the boundary moves more visibly.",
        key=f"strength_{model_type}"
    )

    col1, col2 = st.columns(2)

    learner = get_active_learner(model_type)
    if not learner:
        st.error("Active Learning Model not initialized.")
        return

    # Labels depend on context
    lbl_pos = "✅ In Gate"
    lbl_neg = "🚫 Not In Gate"

    if model_type == 'singlet':
        lbl_pos = "✅ Singlet"
        lbl_neg = "🚫 Doublet"

    if col1.button(lbl_pos, key=f"lbl_pos_{model_type}"):
        with st.spinner("Updating model..."):
            _teach_batch(learner, experiment, x_col, y_col, pts, label=1, plot_df=plot_df, strength=strength, model_type=model_type)
    if col2.button(lbl_neg, key=f"lbl_neg_{model_type}"):
        with st.spinner("Updating model..."):
            _teach_batch(learner, experiment, x_col, y_col, pts, label=0, plot_df=plot_df, strength=strength, model_type=model_type)

def _teach_batch(learner, experiment, x_col, y_col, points, label, plot_df=None, strength=10, model_type='gating'):
    def _coerce_index_value(value):
        if value is None:
            return None
        if isinstance(value, (np.integer, int)):
            return int(value)
        if isinstance(value, str):
            s = value.strip()
            if s.isdigit():
                return int(s)
            return s
        return value

    def _extract_custom_index(point):
        for key in ('customdata', 'customData', 'custom_data'):
            if key not in point:
                continue
            raw = point.get(key)
            if raw is None:
                continue
            # px.scatter(custom_data=[...]) yields an array-like per point
            if isinstance(raw, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
                if len(raw) == 0:
                    continue
                return _coerce_index_value(raw[0])
            return _coerce_index_value(raw)
        return None

    # Prefer customdata (stable across traces), fallback to pointIndex/pointNumber.
    real_indices = []
    plot_indices = []

    for p in points:
        rid = _extract_custom_index(p)
        if rid is not None:
            real_indices.append(rid)
            continue

        idx = p.get('pointIndex')
        if idx is None:
            idx = p.get('point_index')
        if idx is None:
            idx = p.get('pointNumber')
        if idx is None:
            idx = p.get('point_number')

        if idx is not None:
            plot_indices.append(int(idx))

    # De-duplicate while preserving order (box select + trace overlays can double-report)
    if real_indices:
        real_indices = list(dict.fromkeys(real_indices))
    if plot_indices:
        plot_indices = list(dict.fromkeys(plot_indices))

    if not real_indices and not plot_indices:
        debug_keys = list(points[0].keys()) if points else "Empty"
        st.warning(
            f"No valid points selected. Debug info: Found {len(points)} raw points. Keys in first point: {debug_keys}"
        )
        return

    # Map selection -> dataframe rows
    try:
        if real_indices:
            subset = experiment.data.loc[real_indices]
        elif plot_df is not None:
            # Map positional plot indices to dataframe index
            try:
                # Handle downsampled plot_df
                real_indices = plot_df.iloc[plot_indices].index
                subset = experiment.data.loc[real_indices]
            except Exception:
                # Fallback: Maybe plot_df IS the subset
                subset = plot_df.iloc[plot_indices]
        else:
            subset = experiment.data.iloc[plot_indices]
    except Exception as e:
        st.error(
            f"Error mapping selection to data: {e}. Plot DF Size: {len(plot_df) if plot_df is not None else 'None'}"
        )
        return

    # Feature Prep
    is_x_log = (subset[x_col].min() < 0) or (subset[x_col].max() < 10)
    vals_x = subset[x_col].values
    vals_y = subset[y_col].values

    if not is_x_log:
        vals_x = np.log1p(vals_x + 1e-1) # Added epsilon safety
        vals_y = np.log1p(vals_y + 1e-1)

    X_new = np.column_stack([vals_x, vals_y])
    y_new = np.full(len(X_new), label)

    # Dynamic weighting based on selection size and user strength
    repetition_factor = int(strength) if strength is not None else 5
    repetition_factor = max(1, repetition_factor)

    effective_X = []
    effective_y = []

    for i in range(len(X_new)):
        X_repeated = np.tile(X_new[i], (repetition_factor, 1))
        y_repeated = np.full(repetition_factor, y_new[i])
        effective_X.append(X_repeated)
        effective_y.append(y_repeated)

    X_train = np.vstack(effective_X)
    y_train = np.concatenate(effective_y)

    print(f"[ActiveLearning:{model_type}] Updating with {len(X_new)} samples (Expanded to {len(X_train)} training points via strength {repetition_factor}).")

    # Single update step
    learner.teach(X_train, y_train)

    # Clear generic state that might impact viz
    if 'uncertain_indices' in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.uncertain_indices = []

    # Reset specific state to force re-render with new model
    if model_type == 'gating':
        if 'gated_experiment' in st.session_state:
            del st.session_state.gated_experiment
        if 'current_auto_boundary' in st.session_state:
            del st.session_state.current_auto_boundary
    elif model_type == 'singlet':
        # Don't delete 'doublets_removed' or we lose the page state completely?
        # Maybe we want to just invalidate the viz cache.
        pass

    # Increment plot ID to force clear selection state
    st.session_state.plot_id = st.session_state.get('plot_id', 0) + 1

    st.success(f"Updated model with {len(X_new)} points.")
    st.rerun()


==================================================
FILE: ui/plot/histogram_ui.py
==================================================
import streamlit as st
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import pandas as pd
import importlib
import copy
import warnings
import plotly.graph_objects as go
import numpy as np
from scipy.stats import gaussian_kde
from glow.plotting import plots
from glow.canvas.functions import add_plot_to_canvas
from .gating_component import render_gating_component, apply_gates_to_dataframe
import io

def download_btn(fig, key):
    buf = io.BytesIO()
    with warnings.catch_warnings():
        warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
        fig.savefig(buf, format="svg", bbox_inches="tight")
    buf.seek(0)
    st.download_button(
         label="Download SVG",
         data=buf.getvalue(),
         file_name=f"{key}.svg",
         mime="image/svg+xml",
         key=f"dl_{key}"
    )

def render_histogram_ui(filtered_experiment, channels, hue_var, conditions, scale):
    """
    Renders UI and plot logic for 1D Histogram.
    """
    # --- Gating (Unified Component) ---
    local_gates, analysis_gates_to_apply = render_gating_component(filtered_experiment, channels, key_prefix="hist_plot")

    # Create split view: Options (Left), Plot (Right)
    col_opts, col_plot = st.columns([1, 3])

    # --- LEFT COLUMN: Options ---
    with col_opts:
        st.markdown("#### Plot Settings")

        selected_channel = st.selectbox("Select Channel", channels)
        scale = st.selectbox("Scale", ["log", "linear", "logicle"], index=0, help="Axis scale transformation.")

        # User requested: 1D plot groupby should be fixed to Experiment Name if available
        target_cols = ["Experiment Name", "Experiment_Name", "Sample", "sample"]
        found_col = None
        for t in target_cols:
            if t in conditions:
                found_col = t
                break

        if found_col:
            hue_var = found_col
            st.info(f"Grouping by: {hue_var}")
        else:
             # Default to "None" for rendering options purposes (will be overridden by "Configuration" column selection)
             hue_var = None

        # Histogram specific settings
        st.markdown("---")
        st.markdown("**Rendering Options**")

        viz_mode = st.radio(
            "Visualization Style",
            ["Ridgeline (Matplotlib)", "Ridgeline (Overlay)", "Standard (Matplotlib)"],
            index=0,
            help="Ridgeline: Overlaid plots. Standard: Stacked separate plots."
        )

        ridgeline_offset = 0.5
        if "Ridgeline" in viz_mode:
            default_offset = 0.8 if viz_mode == "Ridgeline (Matplotlib)" else 0.5
            ridgeline_offset = st.slider("Overlay Offset", 0.0, 2.0, default_offset, 0.1)

        smooth_sigma = st.slider(
            "Smoothing (Matplotlib only)",
            min_value=0.0,
            max_value=10.0,
            value=0.5,
            step=0.5
        )

        normalization_mode = "total"
        if hue_var:
            normalization_mode = st.selectbox(
                "Merged Plot Normalization",
                options=[
                    ("Original (raw counts)", "original"),
                    ("Modal normalization (FlowJo-style)", "modal"),
                    ("Total count normalization", "total"),
                ],
                index=2,
                format_func=lambda x: x[0],
                help="Original: preserve event count differences. Modal: scale each group so peaks match. Total: scale each group so total area sums to 1."
            )[1]

        show_merged = st.checkbox("Show Merged/Main Plot", value=True)
        show_individual = st.checkbox("Show Individual Groups", value=False)
        show_yaxis = st.checkbox("Show Y-Axis", value=False, help="Display frequency/count axis for subplots.")

        legend_columns = None
        legend_font_size = 10
        # Legend configuration moved to col_plot (Configuration section) but variables needed for matplotlib call
        # Initialize defaults here, will be overwritten if hue_var is active

    # --- RIGHT COLUMN: Plot Display ---
    with col_plot:

        # --- Configuration Section (Right above Generate Plot) ---
        st.markdown("#### Configuration")

        # 1. Group By Selection
        # Re-implement Group By logic here
        # If hue_var was pre-determined by defaults (found_col), use it as index
        # Otherwise use defaults
        group_opts = ["None"] + conditions
        default_idx = 0
        if hue_var and hue_var in group_opts:
             default_idx = group_opts.index(hue_var)
        elif len(conditions) > 0:
             default_idx = 1 # Default to first condition if available

        compare_by = st.selectbox(
            "Color/Group By",
            group_opts,
            index=default_idx,
            help="Select attribute for coloring/grouping histograms"
        )
        hue_var = None if compare_by == "None" else compare_by

        # 2. Legend / Stats Configuration
        if hue_var:
             st.markdown("**Legend & Statistics**")
             c_leg1, c_leg2 = st.columns(2)

             with c_leg1:
                 # Metadata Cols (Grouping info)
                 # Default include grouping var + any other non-varying cols (heuristic?)
                 # For simplicity default to just the Group By variable
                 avail_meta = [hue_var] + [c for c in conditions if c != hue_var]
                 legend_meta_cols = st.multiselect(
                     "Metadata Columns",
                     avail_meta,
                     default=[hue_var],
                     help="Columns identifying the sample groups in the legend table."
                 )

             with c_leg2:
                 # Statistics Cols
                 avail_stats = ["Mean", "Median", "Count"]
                 legend_stats_cols = st.multiselect(
                     "Statistics",
                     avail_stats,
                     default=["Mean", "Count"],
                     help="Statistical metrics to display for each group."
                 )

             legend_columns = legend_meta_cols + legend_stats_cols

             # Font Size (Matplotlib only currently, but could apply to HTML table CSS)
             legend_font_size = st.number_input("Legend Font Size", min_value=5, max_value=20, value=10, step=1)

        replicate_col_selection = None
        show_replicates = False
        if viz_mode == "Ridgeline (Matplotlib)":
             show_replicates = st.checkbox("Show Replicates", value=False, help="Overlay individual replicates (e.g. files) on the same ridge.")
             if show_replicates:
                 # Attempt to identify potential replicate columns
                 # Use 'conditions' plus search for "filename" or "Experiment Name" if not present
                 options = list(conditions)

                 # Add other likely columns if they exist in the dataframe columns (checking filtered_experiment.data)
                 # Note: This runs before "Generate Plot", so we access the base data
                 candidates = ["replicate", "Replicate", "Experiment Name", "Experiment_Name", "filename", "File", "file", "Sample", "sample"]
                 existing_cols = set(filtered_experiment.data.columns)

                 for c in candidates:
                     if c in existing_cols and c not in options:
                         options.append(c)

                 if options:
                    # Default selection logic: Prioritize candidates order (e.g. replicate > Experiment Name)
                    default_idx = 0
                    for c in candidates:
                        if c in options:
                            default_idx = options.index(c)
                            break

                    replicate_col_selection = st.selectbox("Replicate Identifier", options, index=default_idx)
                 else:
                    st.warning("No suitable columns found to identify replicates.")

        st.write("") # Spacer

        if st.button("Generate Plot", type="primary", key="btn_hist"):
            st.session_state.hist_generated = True

        if st.session_state.get('hist_generated'):
            # Reload plots module
            from glow.plotting import histogram, plots as main_plots
            importlib.reload(histogram)
            importlib.reload(main_plots)

            with st.spinner("Generating plots..."):
                try:
                    # Apply Gates (Filtering + Scaling)
                    all_applied_gates = local_gates + analysis_gates_to_apply

                    filtered_df, processed_gates = apply_gates_to_dataframe(
                        filtered_experiment.data,
                        all_applied_gates,
                        experiment_metadata=getattr(filtered_experiment, 'metadata', None),
                        show_raw=st.session_state.get('plot_show_raw_vals', False)
                    )

                    if filtered_df.empty:
                        st.warning("No events remain after applying gates. Please adjust your gates.")
                        return

                    if scale == 'log':
                        # Check for positive values
                        if not (filtered_df[selected_channel] > 0).any():
                             st.warning(f"Data has no positive values in {selected_channel}, and therefore cannot be log-scaled. Use Linear or Logicle, or adjust gates.")
                             return

                    if processed_gates:
                        st.write(f"Applying {len(processed_gates)} filters...")

                    # Create temp experiment
                    plot_experiment = copy.copy(filtered_experiment)
                    plot_experiment.data = filtered_df

                    # Merged Plot
                    if show_merged:
                        st.subheader("Merged Plot")

                        if viz_mode == "Ridgeline (Overlay)":
                            try:
                                # --- Plotly Ridgeline Logic ---
                                fig = go.Figure()

                                plot_data = plot_experiment.data
                                groups = sorted(plot_data[hue_var].unique()) if hue_var and hue_var in plot_data.columns else ["All Events"]

                                import seaborn as sns
                                import matplotlib.colors as mc

                                if len(groups) > 1:
                                    # Match Matplotlib Logic: sns.color_palette("bright", n_groups)
                                    # Convert to Hex for Plotly
                                    palette = sns.color_palette("bright", len(groups))
                                    colors = [mc.to_hex(c) for c in palette]
                                else:
                                    # Single color fallback (bright blueish from palette or default)
                                    colors = [mc.to_hex(sns.color_palette("bright", 1)[0])]

                                all_vals = plot_data[selected_channel].values
                                all_vals = all_vals[~np.isnan(all_vals)]
                                use_log = (scale == 'log')

                                # Domain Calculation
                                if use_log:
                                    valid_vals = all_vals[all_vals > 0]
                                    if len(valid_vals) == 0:
                                        x_min, x_max = 1.0, 100
                                    else:
                                        x_min, x_max = np.percentile(valid_vals, 0.1), np.percentile(valid_vals, 99.9)
                                        if x_min <= 0:
                                            x_min = min(valid_vals) # Should not happen due to filter
                                        if x_min < 1.0:
                                            x_min = 1.0 # Enforce min 10^0

                                    # Fallback if range is 0
                                    if x_min >= x_max:
                                        x_max = x_min * 10

                                    x_log_domain = np.linspace(np.log10(x_min), np.log10(x_max), 200)
                                    x_domain = np.power(10, x_log_domain)
                                else:
                                    if len(all_vals) > 0:
                                        x_min, x_max = np.percentile(all_vals, 0.1), np.percentile(all_vals, 99.9)
                                    else:
                                        x_min, x_max = 0, 1

                                    if x_min >= x_max:
                                        x_max = x_min + 1.0
                                    pad = (x_max - x_min) * 0.1
                                    x_domain = np.linspace(x_min - pad, x_max + pad, 200)

                                for i, group in enumerate(groups):
                                    if hue_var and hue_var in plot_data.columns:
                                        g_vals = plot_data[plot_data[hue_var] == group][selected_channel].values
                                    else:
                                        g_vals = all_vals

                                    g_vals = g_vals[~np.isnan(g_vals)]
                                    if len(g_vals) < 2:
                                        continue

                                    try:
                                        if use_log:
                                            g_vals = g_vals[g_vals > 0]
                                            if len(g_vals) < 2:
                                                continue
                                            kde = gaussian_kde(np.log10(g_vals))
                                            y_vals = kde(np.log10(x_domain))
                                        else:
                                            kde = gaussian_kde(g_vals)
                                            y_vals = kde(x_domain)

                                        # Normalize
                                        if np.max(y_vals) > 0:
                                            y_vals = y_vals / np.max(y_vals)

                                        # Base and Offset
                                        base_y = i * ridgeline_offset
                                        y_plotted = y_vals + base_y

                                        # Color
                                        c = colors[i % len(colors)]

                                        # Create filled area (Pseudo-Ridgeline)
                                        # We use 'toself' with a closed polygon to fill relative to the baseline (base_y)
                                        x_poly = np.concatenate([x_domain, x_domain[::-1]])
                                        y_poly = np.concatenate([y_plotted, np.full_like(y_plotted, base_y)])

                                        fig.add_trace(go.Scatter(
                                            x=x_poly,
                                            y=y_poly,
                                            fill='toself',
                                            mode='lines',
                                            line=dict(color='white', width=0.1), # Invisible boundary for fill
                                            fillcolor=c,
                                            name=str(group),
                                            opacity=0.7,
                                            hovertemplate=f"<b>{group}</b><br>x: %{{x}}<br>Density: %{{y}}<extra></extra>"
                                        ))

                                        # Add Line on top for clarity (Black Outline)
                                        fig.add_trace(go.Scatter(
                                            x=x_domain,
                                            y=y_plotted,
                                            mode='lines',
                                            line=dict(color='black', width=1.0),
                                            showlegend=False,
                                            hoverinfo='skip'
                                        ))
                                    except Exception:
                                        continue

                                # Scale Height based on Offset to prevent squeezing
                                # Base height per plot (e.g. 50px) + Extra for stacking
                                # If offset is large, we need much more height.
                                # Total Height ~= (N_Groups * Offset * Scale_Factor) + Base_Per_Plot

                                # Heuristic: Start with 300px base.
                                # Add 30px per group if offset is small.
                                # If offset is large (>0.5), we are stacking high.
                                # Max value of y is roughly 1.0 per plot.
                                # Total Y range is roughly 1.0 + (N-1)*offset.

                                # To keep "visual height" of 1.0 unit constant (say 100 pixels),
                                # Total Pixels = (1.0 + (len(groups)-1)*ridgeline_offset) * 100

                                unit_height_px = 150 # Height for one histogram (normalized 0-1)
                                total_y_units = 1.0 + max(0, (len(groups) - 1)) * ridgeline_offset
                                calc_height = int(total_y_units * unit_height_px)

                                # Clamp sensible limits
                                calc_height = max(400, min(calc_height, 2000))

                                fig.update_layout(
                                    xaxis_type="log" if use_log else "linear",
                                    title=f"Ridgeline: {selected_channel} by {hue_var}" if hue_var else None,
                                    height=calc_height,
                                    template="plotly_white",
                                    legend=dict(orientation="h", yanchor="bottom", y=1.02, xanchor="right", x=1),
                                    margin=dict(l=20, r=20, t=40, b=20)
                                )
                                if not show_yaxis:
                                    fig.update_yaxes(showticklabels=False, title=None)

                                st.plotly_chart(fig, width="stretch")

                                # --- Separate Legend Table for Ridgeline ---
                                if hue_var and len(groups) > 0:
                                     # Calculate stats for the table
                                     table_data = []
                                     for idx, g in enumerate(groups):
                                         # Get subset
                                         sub_df = plot_data[plot_data[hue_var] == g]
                                         row = {}

                                         # Color indicator (can't easily do block in st.dataframe, maybe just use text or st.color_picker disabled?)
                                         # We'll just show the data

                                         # Metadata Cols
                                         # Re-use legend_meta_cols from config if available (need to ensure scope)
                                         # The variables legend_meta_cols and legend_stats_cols were defined in the Configuration block above
                                         # which is in the same scope (function body).

                                         # However, Streamlit reruns might clear locals if not careful, but this is all one run.
                                         # We reconstructed legend_columns = meta + stats

                                         # Let's iterate legend_columns to build the row
                                         if legend_columns:
                                              for col in legend_columns:
                                                   if col == "Mean":
                                                        vals = sub_df[selected_channel]
                                                        # Log scale aware stats? existing logic uses geometric mean if log
                                                        # Let's stick to standard arithmetic mean for simplicity or match matplotlib
                                                        # Matplotlib logic: if log_scale, geometric mean.
                                                        v = vals[vals > 0] if scale == 'log' else vals
                                                        if scale == 'log':
                                                             val = np.exp(np.log(v).mean()) if len(v) > 0 else 0
                                                        else:
                                                             val = v.mean()
                                                        row["Mean"] = f"{val:.2f}"

                                                   elif col == "Median":
                                                        row["Median"] = f"{sub_df[selected_channel].median():.2f}"

                                                   elif col == "Count":
                                                        row["Count"] = len(sub_df)

                                                   elif col == hue_var:
                                                        row[col] = g

                                                   elif col in sub_df.columns:
                                                        us = sub_df[col].unique()
                                                        row[col] = str(us[0]) if len(us) == 1 else "Mixed"
                                                   else:
                                                        row[col] = "-"
                                              table_data.append(row)

                                     if table_data:
                                          st.markdown("##### Statistics")
                                          st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(table_data), hide_index=True, width="stretch")

                                # Canvas Button
                                canvas_title = f"Ridgeline: {selected_channel} by {hue_var}" if hue_var else f"{selected_channel}"
                                if st.button("Add Merged Plot to Canvas", key="add_merged_ridgeline_to_canvas"):
                                    if add_plot_to_canvas(fig, canvas_title):
                                        st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                            except Exception as e:
                                st.error(f"Error generating Ridgeline plot: {e}")

                        elif viz_mode == "Ridgeline (Matplotlib)":
                            # New Matplotlib Ridgeline Implementation
                            try:
                                fig, stats = histogram.plot_ridgeline_matplotlib(
                                    plot_experiment,
                                    channel=selected_channel,
                                    hue=hue_var,
                                    scale=scale,
                                    offset=ridgeline_offset,
                                    smooth_sigma=smooth_sigma,
                                    normalization=normalization_mode,
                                    show_yaxis=show_yaxis,
                                    legend_cols=legend_columns,
                                    show_replicates=show_replicates,
                                    replicate_col=replicate_col_selection
                                )
                                with warnings.catch_warnings():
                                    warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning)
                                    # Explicitly disable container width scaling to respect calculated figsize
                                    st.pyplot(fig, width="content")

                                if stats:
                                    st.markdown("#### Statistics")
                                    st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(stats))

                                download_btn(fig, "hist_ridge_mpl")

                                canvas_title = f"Ridgeline (Mpl): {selected_channel}"
                                if st.button("Add Merged Plot to Canvas", key="add_mpl_ridge_canvas"):
                                    if add_plot_to_canvas(fig, canvas_title):
                                        st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                                plt.close(fig)
                            except Exception as e:
                                st.error(f"Error generating Matplotlib Ridgeline: {e}")

                        else:
                            # Standard Matplotlib Logic
                            fig, stats = main_plots.plot_1d_histogram(
                                plot_experiment,
                                channel=selected_channel,
                                hue=hue_var,
                                scale=scale,
                                legend_cols=legend_columns,
                                legend_font_size=legend_font_size,
                                smooth_sigma=smooth_sigma,
                                normalization=normalization_mode,
                                show_yaxis=show_yaxis
                            )
                            with warnings.catch_warnings():
                                warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
                                st.pyplot(fig)

                            if stats:
                                st.markdown("#### Statistics")
                                st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(stats))

                            download_btn(fig, "hist_merged")

                            canvas_title = f"Histogram: {selected_channel} by {hue_var}" if hue_var else f"Histogram: {selected_channel}"
                            if st.button("Add Merged Plot to Canvas", key="add_merged_hist_to_canvas"):
                                if add_plot_to_canvas(fig, canvas_title):
                                    st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                            if not getattr(fig, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                                plt.close(fig)

                    # Individual Plots
                    if show_individual and hue_var:
                        st.subheader("Individual Plots")
                        groups = sorted(plot_experiment.data[hue_var].unique())

                        import seaborn as sns
                        palette = sns.color_palette("bright", len(groups))

                        cols = st.columns(2)
                        for i, group in enumerate(groups):
                            sub_exp = plots.filter_experiment(plot_experiment, hue_var, [group])
                            with cols[i % 2]:
                                fig, _ = main_plots.plot_1d_histogram(
                                    sub_exp,
                                    channel=selected_channel,
                                    hue=None,
                                    scale=scale,
                                    color=palette[i],
                                    title=f"Experiment: {group}",
                                    smooth_sigma=smooth_sigma,
                                    normalization=normalization_mode,
                                    show_yaxis=show_yaxis
                                )
                                with warnings.catch_warnings():
                                    warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
                                    st.pyplot(fig)

                                download_btn(fig, f"hist_ind_{i}")
                                if st.button(f"Add \"{group}\" Histogram to Canvas", key=f"add_hist_ind_{i}_to_canvas"):
                                    if add_plot_to_canvas(fig, f"Histogram: {selected_channel} ({group})"):
                                        st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                                if not getattr(fig, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                                    plt.close(fig)
                except Exception as e:
                    st.error(f"Error: {e}")

==================================================
FILE: ui/plot/pairwise_ui.py
==================================================
import streamlit as st
import numpy as np
import seaborn as sns
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from typing import Literal, cast
from glow.canvas.functions import add_plot_to_canvas
from .gating_component import render_gating_component, apply_gates_to_dataframe

def render_pairwise_ui(experiment, channels, conditions, scale='log'):
    """
    Renders the UI for Pairwise Plots (Seaborn pairplot).
    """
    # --- Gating (Unified Component) ---
    local_gates, analysis_gates_to_apply = render_gating_component(experiment, channels, key_prefix="pairwise_plot")

    col_opts, col_plot = st.columns([1, 3])

    with col_opts:
        st.markdown("### Configuration")

        # 1. Settings
        # Channel Selection
        # Default to first few channels if available
        default_channels = channels[:4] if len(channels) >= 4 else channels
        selected_channels = st.multiselect(
            "Channels",
            channels,
            default=default_channels,
            help="Select at least 2 channels."
        )

        # Hue Selection
        hue_options = ["None"] + conditions
        # Add Sample if not present
        if "Sample" in experiment.data.columns and "Sample" not in hue_options:
            hue_options.append("Sample")

        selected_hue = st.selectbox("Group/Color", hue_options, index=0)

        # Scale Selector
        scale = st.selectbox("Scale", ["log", "linear"], index=0, help="Axis scale transformation.")

        # 2. Advanced / Performance
        st.markdown("---")
        st.markdown("**Advanced**")
        max_events = len(experiment.data)

        # Downsampling
        sample_size = st.number_input(
            "Sample Size",
            min_value=50,
            max_value=max_events,
            value=min(2000, max_events),
            step=100,
            help="Smaller sample size is faster. < 2000 recommended for KDE pairplots."
        )

        kind = st.selectbox("Plot Kind", ["scatter", "kde", "hist", "reg"], index=0, help="Type of plot for the off-diagonal subplots.")
        corner = st.checkbox("Corner Plot", value=False, help="Hide the upper triangle of the plot to save space.")

        # 3. Generate Button
        plot_key = "current_pairwise_fig"
        st.markdown("---")

    # 4. Display Result
    with col_plot:
        if st.button("Generate Pairwise Plot", type="primary"):
            st.session_state.run_pairwise_gen = True

        # Logic Process (Between columns or inside one? Better inside plot column or conditional)
        if st.session_state.get('run_pairwise_gen'):
            st.session_state.run_pairwise_gen = False

            if len(selected_channels) < 2:
                st.error("Please select at least 2 channels.")
                if plot_key in st.session_state:
                    del st.session_state[plot_key]
            else:
                with st.spinner("Generating plot... This may take a moment."):
                    try:
                        # Apply Gates (Filtering + Scaling)
                        all_applied_gates = local_gates + analysis_gates_to_apply

                        filtered_df, processed_gates = apply_gates_to_dataframe(
                            experiment.data,
                            all_applied_gates,
                            experiment_metadata=getattr(experiment, 'metadata', None),
                            show_raw=st.session_state.get('plot_show_raw_vals', False)
                        )

                        if filtered_df.empty:
                            st.warning("No events remain after applying gates. Please adjust your gates.")
                            # Stop execution of the plot generation block
                            # Since we are inside a loop flag, just returning or breaking out is tricky if not in a function top level.
                            # render_pairwise_ui IS a function.
                            return

                        if processed_gates:
                            st.write(f"Applying {len(processed_gates)} filters...")

                        # Prepare Data
                        df = filtered_df

                        # Sampling
                        if sample_size < len(df):
                            df = df.sample(n=int(sample_size), random_state=42)

                        # Columns to include
                        cols = list(selected_channels)
                        hue = None
                        if selected_hue != "None":
                            hue = selected_hue
                            if hue not in cols:
                                cols.append(hue)

                        plot_df = df[cols].copy()

                        # Apply Log Scale if requested
                        if scale == 'log':
                            # Filter positive values for selected channels
                            for col in selected_channels:
                                plot_df = plot_df[plot_df[col] > 0]

                            # Apply Log10
                            for col in selected_channels:
                                plot_df[col] = np.log10(plot_df[col])

                        plt.close('all') # Close open figures

                        # Define plot keywords
                        plot_kws = {}
                        if kind == "scatter":
                            plot_kws = {'s': 6, 'alpha': 0.6}
                        elif kind == "reg":
                            plot_kws = {'scatter_kws': {'s': 3, 'alpha': 0.4}}

                        grid = sns.pairplot(
                            plot_df,
                            hue=hue,
                            kind=cast(Literal["scatter", "kde", "hist", "reg"], kind),
                            corner=corner,
                            diag_kind="kde" if kind=="kde" else "auto",
                            plot_kws=plot_kws
                        )

                        # Store figure
                        st.session_state[plot_key] = grid.figure
                        st.session_state['pairwise_title'] = f"Pairwise ({kind}): {', '.join(selected_channels)}"

                    except Exception as e:
                        st.error(f"An error occurred: {e}")
                        if plot_key in st.session_state:
                            del st.session_state[plot_key]

        if plot_key in st.session_state and st.session_state[plot_key]:
            fig = st.session_state[plot_key]
            st.pyplot(fig)

            if st.button("Add to Canvas", key="btn_add_pairwise_canvas"):
                title = st.session_state.get('pairwise_title', "Pairwise Plot")
                add_plot_to_canvas(fig, title)
                st.success("Plot added to canvas!")


==================================================
FILE: ui/plot/gating_component.py

import streamlit as st
import importlib
import pandas as pd
from glow.plotting import utils

def render_gating_component(filtered_experiment, channels, key_prefix="plot"):
    """
    Renders the shared Gating UI component (Analysis Gates + Local Gates).
    Returns (local_gates, analysis_gates_resolved)
    """
    st.markdown("#### Gating (Optional)")

    # 1. Import Analysis Gates
    analysis_gates_to_apply = []
    if ('positive_gates' in st.session_state and st.session_state.positive_gates) or \
       ('cells' in filtered_experiment.data.columns) or ('singlets' in filtered_experiment.data.columns):

        with st.expander("Import Gates from Analysis Tab"):
            st.info("You can choose to apply gates defined in the Analysis tab to this plot.")

            # Fundamental Gates
            built_in_options = []
            if 'cells' in filtered_experiment.data.columns:
                built_in_options.append("Pre-Gate: Live Cells")
            if 'singlets' in filtered_experiment.data.columns:
                built_in_options.append("Pre-Gate: Singlets")

            # Positive Gates
            pos_gate_labels = [g['label'] for g in st.session_state.get('positive_gates', [])]

            gate_options = built_in_options + pos_gate_labels
            selected_analysis_labels = st.multiselect(
                "Select Analysis Gates to Apply",
                options=gate_options,
                default=built_in_options,
                key=f"{key_prefix}_analysis_gates_select"
            )

            # Map selected labels back to gate objects
            def resolve_gate_chain(labels_to_res):
                to_apply = []
                seen_labels = set()
                remaining = list(labels_to_res)
                while remaining:
                    lbl = remaining.pop(0)
                    if lbl in seen_labels:
                        continue
                    found = False
                    # Built-ins
                    if lbl == "Pre-Gate: Live Cells":
                        to_apply.append({'channel': 'cells', 'threshold': 0.5, 'label': lbl})
                        seen_labels.add(lbl)
                        found = True
                    elif lbl == "Pre-Gate: Singlets":
                        to_apply.append({'channel': 'singlets', 'threshold': 0.5, 'label': lbl})
                        seen_labels.add(lbl)
                        if "Pre-Gate: Live Cells" not in seen_labels:
                            remaining.append("Pre-Gate: Live Cells")
                        found = True
                    # Positive Gates
                    if not found:
                        for g in st.session_state.get('positive_gates', []):
                            if g['label'] == lbl:
                                to_apply.append(g)
                                seen_labels.add(lbl)
                                p = g.get('parent')
                                if p and p != "Singlets":
                                    if p not in seen_labels:
                                        remaining.append(p)
                                elif p == "Singlets":
                                    if "Pre-Gate: Singlets" not in seen_labels:
                                        remaining.append("Pre-Gate: Singlets")
                                    found = True
                                    break
                return to_apply

            analysis_gates_to_apply = resolve_gate_chain(selected_analysis_labels)

    # 2. Local Gates
    gate_key = f"{key_prefix}_local_gates"
    if gate_key not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state[gate_key] = []

    with st.expander("Active Local Gates", expanded=True):
        if not st.session_state[gate_key]:
            st.info("No local gates defined for this plot.")
        else:
            def del_gate_callback(index_to_del, g_key):
                if 0 <= index_to_del < len(st.session_state[g_key]):
                    st.session_state[g_key].pop(index_to_del)

            for i, gate in enumerate(st.session_state[gate_key]):
                gc1, gc2 = st.columns([4, 1])
                with gc1:
                    th = gate['threshold']
                    st.write(f"**Local Gate {i+1}**: {gate['channel']} ({th[0]:.2f} - {th[1]:.2f})")
                with gc2:
                    st.button(
                        "🗑️",
                        key=f"{key_prefix}_del_gate_{i}",
                        on_click=del_gate_callback,
                        args=(i, gate_key)
                    )

    with st.expander("Add New Local Gate"):
        importlib.reload(utils)

        # Ensure different new gate channel per plot type if possible to avoid clashes?
        # Actually standard widget keys should be uniqueified by prefix.

        # Suggest default
        default_marker_idx = 0
        if len(channels) > 1:
            # Simple heuristic, pick 2nd if available
            default_marker_idx = 1

        new_gate_channel = st.selectbox(
            "Marker Channel for New Local Gate",
            channels,
            index=default_marker_idx,
            key=f"{key_prefix}_new_gate_channel_select"
        )

        range_p_key = f"{key_prefix}_new_gate_range_p"
        raw_min_key = f"{key_prefix}_new_gate_raw_min"
        raw_max_key = f"{key_prefix}_new_gate_raw_max"

        if range_p_key not in st.session_state:
            st.session_state[range_p_key] = (95.0, 99.9)

        # Callbacks
        def on_new_slider_change():
            p_min, p_max = st.session_state[range_p_key]
            try:
                v_min = utils.get_percentile_threshold(filtered_experiment.data, new_gate_channel, p_min)
                v_max = utils.get_percentile_threshold(filtered_experiment.data, new_gate_channel, p_max)
                st.session_state[raw_min_key] = float(v_min)
                st.session_state[raw_max_key] = float(v_max)
            except Exception:
                pass

        def on_new_raw_change():
            v_min = st.session_state[raw_min_key]
            v_max = st.session_state[raw_max_key]
            try:
                p_min = utils.get_percentile_of_value(filtered_experiment.data, new_gate_channel, v_min)
                p_max = utils.get_percentile_of_value(filtered_experiment.data, new_gate_channel, v_max)
                p_min = max(0.0, min(100.0, p_min))
                p_max = max(0.0, min(100.0, p_max))
                if p_min > p_max:
                    p_min, p_max = p_max, p_min
                st.session_state[range_p_key] = (p_min, p_max)
            except Exception:
                pass

        # Trigger update if raw keys missing
        if raw_min_key not in st.session_state:
            on_new_slider_change()

        st.markdown("**Percentile Range**")
        st.slider(
            "Percentile", 0.0, 100.0,
            key=range_p_key,
            on_change=on_new_slider_change,
            step=0.1
        )

        st.markdown(f"**Value Range** ({new_gate_channel})")
        rc1, rc2 = st.columns(2)
        with rc1:
            st.number_input("Min", key=raw_min_key, on_change=on_new_raw_change)
        with rc2:
            st.number_input("Max", key=raw_max_key, on_change=on_new_raw_change)

        def add_gate_callback(channel, g_key, r_min_key, r_max_key):
             st.session_state[g_key].append({
                'channel': channel,
                'threshold': (st.session_state[r_min_key], st.session_state[r_max_key]),
                'is_data_units': True  # Mark this gate as being in data units already
            })

        if st.button(
            "Add This Gate",
            type="secondary",
            key=f"{key_prefix}_add_gate_btn",
            on_click=add_gate_callback,
            args=(new_gate_channel, gate_key, raw_min_key, raw_max_key)
        ):
            st.success(f"Added gate on {new_gate_channel}")

    return st.session_state[gate_key], analysis_gates_to_apply


def apply_gates_to_dataframe(df, gates, experiment_metadata=None, show_raw=False):
    """
    Applies a list of gates to a DataFrame.
    Handles scaling correction if experiment_metadata indicates high-res data.
    """
    plot_df = df.copy()

    # 1. Determine Scaling Logic
    scaling_factor = 1.0
    is_scaled = False
    if experiment_metadata and experiment_metadata.get('is_high_res', False):
        if not show_raw:
            scaling_factor = experiment_metadata.get('scaling_factor', 10000.0)
            is_scaled = True

    # 2. Process and Apply Gates
    scalable_channels = [c for c in plot_df.columns if pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(plot_df[c])]
    # Or rely on passed channels, but we don't have them here easily.
    # Using simple numeric check + exclusion of masks.

    processed_gates = []

    for g in gates:
        pg = g.copy()

        # Scale Thresholds if needed
        # Gates on 'cells'/'singlets' are never scaled (they are masks/probability 0-1)
        # Gates on Fluorophores/Scatter are scaled if data is scaled
        is_mask_gate = pg.get('channel') in ['cells', 'singlets', 'time', 'Time']
        is_data_units = pg.get('is_data_units', False)

        if is_scaled and not is_mask_gate and not is_data_units and pg.get('channel') in scalable_channels:
             if 'threshold' in pg:
                th = pg['threshold']
                if isinstance(th, (list, tuple)) and len(th) == 2:
                    pg['threshold'] = (th[0]/scaling_factor, th[1]/scaling_factor)
                elif isinstance(th, (int, float)):
                    pg['threshold'] = th / scaling_factor
             if 'min' in pg:
                 pg['min'] = pg['min'] / scaling_factor
             if 'max' in pg:
                 pg['max'] = pg['max'] / scaling_factor

        processed_gates.append(pg)

    # 3. Filtering Loop
    for gate in processed_gates:
        ch = gate.get('channel')
        th = gate.get('threshold')

        if ch in plot_df.columns and th is not None:
            if isinstance(th, (tuple, list)) and len(th) == 2:
                plot_df = plot_df[(plot_df[ch] >= th[0]) & (plot_df[ch] <= th[1])]
            else:
                # Directionality
                direction = gate.get('direction', 'positive')
                if direction in ['negative', 'Negative (<)']:
                    plot_df = plot_df[plot_df[ch] < th]
                else:
                    plot_df = plot_df[plot_df[ch] > th]

    return plot_df, processed_gates

FILE: ui/plot/base.py

import streamlit as st
from glow.plotting import plots

def render_data_selection(experiment):
    """
    Renders common data selection and filtering UI.
    Returns: (filtered_experiment, conditions)
    """
    st.subheader("1. Data Selection")
    conditions = plots.get_conditions(experiment)

    # 1. Choose categorization/filtering column
    filter_col = st.selectbox("Select Category to Filter By", ["None"] + conditions, index=1 if conditions else 0)

    filtered_experiment = experiment
    if filter_col != "None":
        unique_vals = sorted(experiment.data[filter_col].unique())
        selected_vals = st.multiselect(f"Select {filter_col} to Include", unique_vals, default=unique_vals)

        if not selected_vals:
            st.warning("No data selected.")
            return None, conditions

        filtered_experiment = plots.filter_experiment(experiment, filter_col, selected_vals)
        st.caption(f"Filtered data contains {len(filtered_experiment.data)} events.")
    else:
        st.caption(f"Using full dataset ({len(experiment.data)} events).")

    return filtered_experiment, conditions


==================================================
FILE: ui/plot/scatter_ui.py
==================================================
import streamlit as st
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import importlib
import copy
import io
import warnings
from glow.plotting import plots
from glow.canvas.functions import add_plot_to_canvas
from .gating_component import render_gating_component, apply_gates_to_dataframe

def download_btn(fig, key):
    buf = io.BytesIO()
    with warnings.catch_warnings():
        warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
        fig.savefig(buf, format="svg", bbox_inches="tight")
    buf.seek(0)
    st.download_button(
         label="Download SVG",
         data=buf.getvalue(),
         file_name=f"{key}.svg",
         mime="image/svg+xml",
         key=f"dl_{key}"
    )

def render_scatter_ui(filtered_experiment, channels, hue_var, facet_var, conditions, scale):
    """
    Renders UI and plot logic for 2D Scatter.
    """
    # --- Gating (Unified Component) ---
    local_gates, analysis_gates_to_apply = render_gating_component(filtered_experiment, channels, key_prefix="scatter_plot")

    # Create split view: Options (Left), Plot (Right)
    col_opts, col_plot = st.columns([1, 3])

    # Variables to track group selection
    selected_groups = None
    final_order = None

    with col_opts:
        st.markdown("#### Plot Settings")

        # Determine default indices for mCherry (X) and GFP (Y)
        default_x_index = 0
        default_y_index = 1 if len(channels) > 1 else 0

        for i, c in enumerate(channels):
            if "mcherry" in c.lower():
                default_x_index = i
                break

        for i, c in enumerate(channels):
            if "gfp" in c.lower():
                default_y_index = i
                break

        x_channel = st.selectbox("X Axis Channel", channels, index=default_x_index)
        y_channel = st.selectbox("Y Axis Channel", channels, index=default_y_index)
        scale = st.selectbox("Scale", ["log", "linear", "logicle"], index=0, help="Axis scale transformation.")

        # Scatter specific settings
        st.markdown("---")
        st.markdown("**Rendering Options**")

        show_trend = st.checkbox("Show Trend Line (Moving Mean)", value=True)

        c1, c2 = st.columns(2)
        with c1:
             show_dots = st.checkbox("Show Dots (Individual)", value=True)
        with c2:
             show_dots_merged = st.checkbox("Show Dots (Merged)", value=False)

        merged_size = st.slider("Merged Plot Size", min_value=2.0, max_value=8.0, value=3.5, step=0.5, help="Size of the merged comparison plot (width & height).")

        show_grid = st.checkbox("Show Grid", value=False)

        # Backend Selection
        backend = st.radio("Plot Backend", ["Matplotlib", "Plotly"], index=0, horizontal=True)

        show_merged = True
        show_individual = False

        # Logic for merging/individual view selection based on trend mode
        show_merged = st.checkbox("Show Merged/Main Plot", value=True)
        if not show_trend:
             # Plotly handles facets easily, but splitting by group manually is distinct.
             # Disable individual splits for Plotly for now to keep it simple, or allow it?
             # Let's allow it for consistency.
             show_individual = st.checkbox("Show Individual Groups", value=False)
        else:
             st.caption("Individual splitting handled by Facet selection")

        st.markdown("---")

    with col_plot:
        st.markdown("#### Grouping Configuration")

        # Filter conditions to remove 'cells' and 'singlets'
        filtered_conditions = [c for c in conditions if c not in ["cells", "singlets"]]

        # Determine defaults
        default_group_idx = 0
        default_facet_idx = 0

        # Candidates for defaults order: Experiment Name > Sample
        candidates = ["Experiment Name", "Experiment_Name", "Sample", "sample"]

        # Find best candidate present in filtered conditions
        best_candidate = None
        for c in candidates:
             if c in filtered_conditions:
                 best_candidate = c
                 break

        # Group Options
        group_options = ["None"] + filtered_conditions

        # Set defaults if candidate found
        if best_candidate:
            if best_candidate in group_options:
                 default_group_idx = group_options.index(best_candidate)
            if best_candidate in group_options:
                 default_facet_idx = group_options.index(best_candidate)

        gc1, gc2 = st.columns(2)
        with gc1:
            compare_by = st.selectbox(
                "Color/Group By",
                group_options,
                index=default_group_idx
            )
            hue_var = None if compare_by == "None" else compare_by

        with gc2:
            facet_by = st.selectbox(
                "Facet By (Subplots)",
                group_options,
                index=default_facet_idx
            )
            facet_var = None if facet_by == "None" else facet_by

        if hue_var:
            all_groups = sorted(filtered_experiment.data[hue_var].unique())

            # Use columns for layout
            sg1, sg2 = st.columns([3, 1])
            with sg1:
                selected_groups = st.multiselect("Select Groups to Plot", all_groups, default=all_groups)
            with sg2:
                st.write("") # Spacer
                st.write("")
                manual_order = st.checkbox("Manual Group Order", value=False)

            final_order = selected_groups

            if manual_order and selected_groups:
                order_text = st.text_area("Specify Order (comma separated)", value=", ".join(selected_groups), height=68)
                if order_text:
                    custom_order = [x.strip() for x in order_text.split(",") if x.strip()]
                    final_order = [x for x in custom_order if x in selected_groups]
                    remaining = [x for x in selected_groups if x not in final_order]
                    final_order.extend(remaining)

        st.write("") # Spacer

        if st.button("Generate Plot", type="primary", key="btn_scatter"):
            st.session_state.scatter_generated = True

        if st.session_state.get('scatter_generated'):
            from glow.plotting import scatter, plots as main_plots
            importlib.reload(scatter)
            importlib.reload(main_plots)

            base_title = f"Scatter: {x_channel} vs {y_channel}"
            if hue_var:
                base_title += f" by {hue_var}"
            if facet_var:
                base_title += f" (facet: {facet_var})"
            if show_trend:
                base_title += " + trend"
            if show_dots:
                base_title += " + dots"

            with st.spinner("Generating plots..."):
                try:
                    # Apply Gates (Filtering + Scaling)
                    all_applied_gates = local_gates + analysis_gates_to_apply

                    filtered_df, processed_gates = apply_gates_to_dataframe(
                        filtered_experiment.data,
                        all_applied_gates,
                        experiment_metadata=getattr(filtered_experiment, 'metadata', None),
                        show_raw=st.session_state.get('plot_show_raw_vals', False)
                    )

                    if filtered_df.empty:
                        st.warning("No events remain after applying gates. Please adjust your gates.")
                        return

                    if scale == 'log':
                        # Check for positive values to avoid matplotlib crash
                        x_ok = (filtered_df[x_channel] > 0).any()
                        y_ok = (filtered_df[y_channel] > 0).any()
                        if not x_ok or not y_ok:
                            st.warning(f"Data has no positive values in {x_channel} or {y_channel}, and therefore cannot be log-scaled. Consider switching to Linear or Logicle scale, or adjusting gates.")
                            return

                    if processed_gates:
                        st.write(f"Applying {len(processed_gates)} filters...")

                    # Apply Group Filter (if Groups Selected)
                    if hue_var and final_order is not None:
                        # Filter dataframe to only keep rows where hue_var is in selected groups
                        filtered_df = filtered_df[filtered_df[hue_var].isin(final_order)]
                        if filtered_df.empty:
                            st.warning("No events match the selected groups.")
                            return

                    # Create temp experiment for backend
                    plot_experiment = copy.copy(filtered_experiment)
                    plot_experiment.data = filtered_df

                    if show_merged:
                        if not show_trend:
                            st.subheader("Merged Plot")

                        if backend == "Matplotlib":
                            res = main_plots.plot_2d_scatter(
                                plot_experiment,
                                x_channel=x_channel,
                                y_channel=y_channel,
                                hue=hue_var,
                                facet=facet_var,
                                xscale=scale,
                                yscale=scale,
                                show_trend=show_trend,
                                show_dots=show_dots,
                                show_dots_summary=show_dots_merged,
                                show_grid=show_grid,
                                title=base_title,
                                merged_figsize=(merged_size, merged_size)
                            )

                            if isinstance(res, tuple):
                                # Correct unpacking for 3 items (facets, summary, legend) or 2 items
                                fig_legend = None
                                if len(res) == 3:
                                     fig_facets, fig_summary, fig_legend = res
                                else:
                                     fig_facets, fig_summary = res

                                st.subheader("Merged Comparison")

                                if fig_legend:
                                    # Legend on top, scaled proportionally (using a column to restrict width)
                                    # Use a ratio that creates a reasonable size (e.g., 20-25% of width)
                                    # This ensures it scales with browser but doesn't fill screen width (which would distort vertical legend)
                                    lc1, lc2 = st.columns([1.5, 4])
                                    with lc1:
                                        st.pyplot(fig_legend, width="content")

                                st.pyplot(fig_summary, width="content")

                                download_btn(fig_summary, "scatter_summary")
                                if st.button("Add Summary Plot to Canvas", key="add_scatter_summary_to_canvas"):
                                    if add_plot_to_canvas(fig_summary, base_title + " (summary)"):
                                        st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                                # Close figures to free memory
                                if not getattr(fig_summary, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                                    plt.close(fig_summary)
                                if fig_legend and not getattr(fig_legend, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                                    plt.close(fig_legend)

                                st.subheader("Separate (Detailed) Panels")
                                st.pyplot(fig_facets, width="content")
                                download_btn(fig_facets, "scatter_facets")
                                if st.button("Add Facet Plot to Canvas", key="add_scatter_facets_to_canvas"):
                                    if add_plot_to_canvas(fig_facets, base_title + " (facets)"):
                                        st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                                if not getattr(fig_facets, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                                    plt.close(fig_facets)
                            else:
                                st.pyplot(res, width="content")
                                download_btn(res, "scatter_plot")
                                if st.button("Add Plot to Canvas", key="add_scatter_to_canvas"):
                                    if add_plot_to_canvas(res, base_title):
                                        st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                                if not getattr(res, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                                    plt.close(res)
                        else:
                            # Plotly Backend
                            if show_trend:
                                st.warning("Trend lines not yet supported in Plotly mode. Using standard scatter.")

                            fig = scatter.plot_2d_scatter_plotly(
                                plot_experiment,
                                x_channel=x_channel,
                                y_channel=y_channel,
                                hue=hue_var,
                                facet=facet_var,
                                scale=scale,
                                show_grid=show_grid,
                                title=base_title
                            )
                            st.plotly_chart(fig, width="stretch")
                            if st.button("Add Plot to Canvas", key="add_plotly_scatter"):
                                 add_plot_to_canvas(fig, base_title)
                                 st.success("Added to Canvas!")


                    if show_individual and hue_var and not show_trend:
                        st.subheader("Individual Plots")
                        groups = sorted(filtered_experiment.data[hue_var].unique())
                        cols = st.columns(2)
                        for i, group in enumerate(groups):
                            sub_exp = plots.filter_experiment(plot_experiment, hue_var, [group])
                            with cols[i % 2]:
                                fig = main_plots.plot_2d_scatter(
                                    sub_exp,
                                    x_channel=x_channel,
                                    y_channel=y_channel,
                                    hue=None,
                                    xscale=scale,
                                    yscale=scale,
                                    show_trend=False,
                                    title=f"Experiment: {group}",
                                    figsize=(3, 3)
                                )
                                st.pyplot(fig, width="content")
                                download_btn(fig, f"scatter_ind_{i}")
                                if st.button(f"Add \"{group}\" to Canvas", key=f"add_scatter_ind_{i}_to_canvas"):
                                    if add_plot_to_canvas(fig, base_title + f" ({group})"):
                                        st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                                if not getattr(fig, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                                    plt.close(fig)

                except Exception as e:
                    st.error(f"Error: {e}")

FILE: ui/plot/view.py
