import streamlit as st
import pandas as pd
import copy
from glow.plotting import plots
from .base import render_data_selection
from .histogram_ui import render_histogram_ui
from .scatter_ui import render_scatter_ui
from .bar_ui import render_bar_ui
from .pairwise_ui import render_pairwise_ui

def render_plot_tab():
    st.header("Plotting")

    if 'analysis_experiment' in st.session_state and st.session_state.analysis_experiment is not None:
        experiment = st.session_state.analysis_experiment
    elif 'base_experiment' in st.session_state and st.session_state.base_experiment is not None:
        experiment = st.session_state.base_experiment
    else:
        st.warning("No experiment loaded. Please load data in the Gating tab first.")
        return
    if experiment is None:
        st.error("Experiment is None.")
        return

    # 1. Common Data Selection
    filtered_experiment, conditions = render_data_selection(experiment)
    if filtered_experiment is None:
        return # Selection failed

    # --- High Resolution Scaling Logic ---
    if hasattr(experiment, 'metadata') and experiment.metadata.get('is_high_res', False):
         st.warning("High-resolution data detected. Data logic is scaled by default (1/10000).")
         use_raw = st.toggle("Show Raw Values (Unscaled)", value=False, key="plot_show_raw_vals")

         if not use_raw:
             scaling_factor = experiment.metadata.get('scaling_factor', 10000.0)
             # Create a safe copy for modification
             if hasattr(filtered_experiment, 'clone'):
                 filtered_experiment = filtered_experiment.clone()
             else:
                 filtered_experiment = copy.copy(filtered_experiment)
                 # Ensure data is also a copy to avoid reference modification
                 filtered_experiment.data = filtered_experiment.data.copy()

             # Perform Scaling on Channels Only (Fluorophores + Scatter)
             # Instead of excluding specific metadata columns, we target declared channels.
             # This ensures metadata like 'Experiment_Name' or 'Replicate' are never scaled.

             target_cols = []

             # Get declared channels (measurements)
             # We prefer the explicit .channels property if available (Cytoflow or MockExperiment)
             try:
                 if hasattr(filtered_experiment, 'channels'):
                     declared_channels = list(filtered_experiment.channels)
                 else:
                     declared_channels = plots.get_channels(filtered_experiment)
             except Exception:
                 declared_channels = []

             # Extra safety: explicitly exclude Time if it appears in channels
             STRICT_EXCLUDE_TIME = ['Time', 'time', 'Time (ms)', 'Time (s)']

             for c in declared_channels:
                 if c in filtered_experiment.data.columns and c not in STRICT_EXCLUDE_TIME:
                     if pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(filtered_experiment.data[c]):
                         target_cols.append(c)

             if target_cols:
                 filtered_experiment.data[target_cols] = filtered_experiment.data[target_cols].astype(float) / scaling_factor

    # 2. Main Plot Type Selection
    all_available_channels = plots.get_channels(filtered_experiment)

    # Filter channels based on selection in Gating tab if available
    # Use persistent state to survive tab switch
    user_selected = st.session_state.get('persistent_selected_channels')

    # --- Compensation Logic (Plot Tab) ---
    all_cols = filtered_experiment.data.columns
    comp_cols = [c for c in all_cols if c.startswith("comp_")]
    has_comp = len(comp_cols) > 0
    use_comp = False

    if has_comp:
        use_comp = st.checkbox("Use Compensated Data", value=True, key="use_comp_plot")

    if user_selected is not None:
        raw_channels = [c for c in all_available_channels if c in user_selected]
    else:
        raw_channels = all_available_channels

    # Apply Substitution
    channels = []
    if use_comp:
        for c in raw_channels:
            comp_name = f"comp_{c}"
            if comp_name in all_cols:
                channels.append(comp_name)
            else:
                # Keep original if comp version doesn't exist (e.g. FSC/SSC)
                channels.append(c)
    else:
        # If not using comp, ensure we don't show comp_cols (if they leaked into all_available)
        channels = [c for c in raw_channels if not c.startswith("comp_")]

    with st.sidebar:
        st.subheader("Plot Settings")
        plot_type = st.selectbox(
            "Select Plot Type",
            ["1D Histogram", "2D Scatter", "Bar Plot", "Pairwise Plot"]
        )
        st.divider()

    # Exclude FSC/FC, SSC, and Metadata-like channels for Plotting
    if plot_type in ["1D Histogram", "2D Scatter", "Bar Plot", "Pairwise Plot"]:
        # Extended exclusion list based on user request (fluorophores only)
        # Excludes Scatter (FSC/SSC), Time, and metadata-like numeric columns (Replicate, etc.)
        exclude_patterns = ['fsc', 'fc', 'ssc', 'time', 'replicate', 'experiment', 'sample', 'well', 'plate']
        channels = [c for c in channels if not any(x in c.lower() for x in exclude_patterns)]

    # hue_var and facet_var will be handled inside the specific UI modules now
    hue_var = None
    facet_var = None

    # Scale is now handled inside individual UIs, default placeholder here
    scale = 'log'

    # 4. Delegate to specific sub-modules
    if plot_type == "1D Histogram":
        render_histogram_ui(filtered_experiment, channels, hue_var, conditions, scale)
    elif plot_type == "2D Scatter":
        render_scatter_ui(filtered_experiment, channels, hue_var, facet_var, conditions, scale)
    elif plot_type == "Bar Plot":
        render_bar_ui(filtered_experiment, channels, hue_var, conditions)
    elif plot_type == "Pairwise Plot":
        render_pairwise_ui(filtered_experiment, channels, conditions, scale)



==================================================
FILE: ui/plot/bar_ui.py

import streamlit as st
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import pandas as pd
import importlib
import io
import copy
import warnings
from glow.plotting import utils, categorical
from glow.canvas.functions import add_plot_to_canvas
from .gating_component import render_gating_component, apply_gates_to_dataframe

def download_btn(fig, key):
    buf = io.BytesIO()
    with warnings.catch_warnings():
        warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
        fig.savefig(buf, format="svg", bbox_inches="tight")
    buf.seek(0)
    st.download_button(
         label="Download SVG",
         data=buf.getvalue(),
         file_name=f"{key}.svg",
         mime="image/svg+xml",
         key=f"dl_{key}"
    )

def render_bar_ui(filtered_experiment, channels, hue_var, conditions):
    """
    Renders UI and plot logic for Bar Plot.
    """
    # --- Gating Logic ---
    # Sync old state if exists
    if 'bar_gates' in st.session_state and 'bar_plot_local_gates' not in st.session_state:
            st.session_state['bar_plot_local_gates'] = st.session_state.bar_gates

    local_gates, analysis_gates_to_apply = render_gating_component(filtered_experiment, channels, key_prefix="bar_plot")

    # Sync back for legacy access
    st.session_state.bar_gates = local_gates

    # Create split view: Options (Left), Plot (Right)
    col_opts, col_plot = st.columns([1, 3])

    # --- Grouping Settings (Variables to be shared) ---
    selected_groups = None
    final_order = None

    with col_plot:
        # Move Grouping Settings Here (Detailed Options)
        st.markdown("### Grouping Configuration")

        # Force Group By to Experiment_Name/Sample if possible, as requested
        target_group_cols = ["Experiment_Name", "Experiment Name", "Sample", "sample"]
        found_group_col = None
        for t in target_group_cols:
            if t in conditions:
                found_group_col = t
                break

        if found_group_col:
            hue_var = found_group_col
            # Show as info but allow override via checkbox? For now stick to logic
            st.info(f"Grouping by **{hue_var}**")
        else:
            hue_var = st.selectbox("Group By (X-Axis)", conditions)

        if hue_var:
            all_groups = sorted(filtered_experiment.data[hue_var].unique())

            # Use columns for layout in wider area
            gc1, gc2 = st.columns([3, 1])

            with gc1:
                selected_groups = st.multiselect("Select Groups to Plot", all_groups, default=all_groups)
            with gc2:
                st.write("") # Spacer to align checkbox
                st.write("")
                manual_order = st.checkbox("Manual Group Order", value=False)

            final_order = selected_groups

            if manual_order and selected_groups:
                    order_text = st.text_area("Specify Order (comma separated)", value=", ".join(selected_groups), height=68)
                    if order_text:
                        custom_order = [x.strip() for x in order_text.split(",") if x.strip()]
                        final_order = [x for x in custom_order if x in selected_groups]
                        remaining = [x for x in selected_groups if x not in final_order]
                        final_order.extend(remaining)

        st.divider()

    with col_opts:
        # --- Channel & Statistics (Stacked vertically, no columns to squeeze) ---
        st.markdown("#### Plot Settings")

        selected_channel = st.selectbox("Output Channel (Y-axis)", channels)

        # Default to t-test (uncorrected)
        selected_correction = "t-test"

        # --- Comparisons ---
        comparisons = []
        if hue_var and selected_groups:
            st.markdown("#### Comparisons")
            with st.expander("Add Comparisons"):
                if 'bar_comparisons' not in st.session_state:
                    st.session_state.bar_comparisons = []

                # Stacked UI for narrow column
                vis_groups = sorted(selected_groups)
                if len(vis_groups) >= 2:
                    st.selectbox("Group 1", vis_groups, key="g1")
                    st.selectbox("Group 2", vis_groups, key="g2")

                    def add_pair_callback():
                        p1 = st.session_state.get('g1')
                        p2 = st.session_state.get('g2')
                        if p1 and p2:
                            new_pair = (p1, p2)
                            if new_pair not in st.session_state.bar_comparisons and p1 != p2:
                                st.session_state.bar_comparisons.append(new_pair)

                    if st.button("Add Pair", on_click=add_pair_callback):
                        pass

                if st.session_state.bar_comparisons:
                    # Sanitize old session state data (remove labels if present)
                    valid_comparisons = []
                    for comp in st.session_state.bar_comparisons:
                        if len(comp) >= 2:
                            valid_comparisons.append(comp[:2])
                    st.session_state.bar_comparisons = valid_comparisons

                    st.markdown("**Selected Comparisons:**")
                    # Show as a table
                    comp_df = pd.DataFrame(st.session_state.bar_comparisons, columns=["Group 1", "Group 2"])
                    st.dataframe(comp_df, hide_index=True, width="stretch")

                    def clear_comps_callback():
                        st.session_state.bar_comparisons = []

                    if st.button("Clear Comparisons", on_click=clear_comps_callback):
                        pass

                comparisons = st.session_state.bar_comparisons

            # --- Correction Logic inside Comparisons Section ---
            st.markdown("###### Significance Correction")

            # Calculate state
            num_comps = len(st.session_state.get('bar_comparisons', []))
            is_correction_disabled = num_comps < 2

            if is_correction_disabled:
                 correction_options = ["t-test (None)"]
            else:
                 correction_options = ["Bonferroni Correction", "FDR (Benjamini-Hochberg)"]

            c_method = st.selectbox(
                "Method",
                correction_options,
                disabled=is_correction_disabled,
                help="Applies multiple testing correction to p-values. Requires at least 2 comparisons.",
                key="multi_corr_method"
            )

            if is_correction_disabled:
                 st.caption("Correction disabled (Define ≥2 comparisons).")
                 selected_correction = "t-test"
            else:
                 if "Bonferroni" in c_method:
                     selected_correction = "bonferroni"
                 elif "FDR" in c_method:
                     selected_correction = "fdr_bh"
                 else:
                     selected_correction = "t-test"

        # --- Style Settings ---
        st.markdown("#### Style Settings")

        # Stacked options to avoid squeezing
        # Keys added to persist state across reruns (e.g. when adding gates)
        show_dist = st.checkbox("Show Distributions", value=True, key="bar_style_show_dist")
        use_bars = st.checkbox("Plot as Bars", value=False, help="Draw bars indicating the mean instead of just lines.", key="bar_style_use_bars")
        log_scale = st.checkbox("Log Scale Y-Axis", value=True, key="bar_style_log_scale")

        font_size = st.number_input("Font Size", min_value=6, max_value=30, value=12, key="bar_style_font_size")
        step_fraction = st.slider("Bracket Spacing", 0.05, 0.30, 0.10, 0.01, help="Vertical distance between significance brackets.", key="bar_style_spacing")

        error_bar_type = st.selectbox("Error Bar", ["SEM", "SD", "None"], index=0, key="bar_style_error_bar")
        error_bar_val = error_bar_type.lower() if error_bar_type != "None" else None

        # Auto-detect Replicate Column
        replicate_col = None
        all_columns = filtered_experiment.data.columns.tolist()
        # Try to find 'Replicate'
        for c in all_columns:
            if c.lower() == 'replicate':
                replicate_col = c
                break

        # X-Axis Annotations
        # Filter conditions to exclude 'Experiment_Name' or 'Replicate'
        target_names = ["Experiment_Name", "Experiment Name", "Experiment_name", "experiment_name", "Sample", "sample"]
        valid_conditions = [c for c in conditions if c not in target_names and c != replicate_col]
        annotation_cols = st.multiselect("Additional Conditions for X-Axis Labels", valid_conditions)

        # Show event count for feedback
        event_count = len(filtered_experiment.data)
        st.info(f"Using {event_count:,} events for plotting.")


    with col_plot:
        if st.button("Generate Plot", type="primary", key="btn_bar"):
            st.session_state.bar_generated = True

        if st.session_state.get('bar_generated'):
            # reload to get latest backend changes
            importlib.reload(utils)
            importlib.reload(categorical)

            with st.spinner("Generating plots..."):
                try:
                    # --- Gating Application ---
                    all_applied_gates = local_gates + analysis_gates_to_apply

                    # Apply Gates (Filtering + Scaling)
                    filtered_df, processed_gates = apply_gates_to_dataframe(
                        filtered_experiment.data,
                        all_applied_gates,
                        experiment_metadata=getattr(filtered_experiment, 'metadata', None),
                        show_raw=st.session_state.get('plot_show_raw_vals', False)
                    )

                    if filtered_df.empty:
                        st.warning("No events remain after applying gates. Please adjust your gates.")
                        return

                    if log_scale:
                         if not (filtered_df[selected_channel] > 0).any():
                              st.warning(f"Data has no positive values in {selected_channel}, and therefore cannot be log-scaled. Uncheck Log Scale or adjust gates.")
                              return

                    if processed_gates:
                        st.write(f"Applying {len(processed_gates)} filters...")

                    # Create temp experiment for backend
                    plot_experiment = copy.copy(filtered_experiment)
                    plot_experiment.data = filtered_df

                    fig = categorical.plot_means_with_significance(
                        plot_experiment,
                        channel=selected_channel,
                        statistic='mean', # Always mean
                        group_by=hue_var,
                        comparisons=comparisons,
                        hue_order=final_order,
                        show_distribution=show_dist,
                        replicate_col=replicate_col,
                        log_scale=log_scale,
                        annotation_cols=annotation_cols,
                        correction_method=selected_correction,
                        use_bars=use_bars,
                        font_size=font_size,
                        bracket_spacing=step_fraction,
                        gates=[], # Gates are already applied
                        error_bar=error_bar_val if error_bar_val else "ci" # Default if None
                    )
                    st.pyplot(fig)
                    download_btn(fig, "bar_plot")

                    canvas_title = f"Bar Plot: {selected_channel} by {hue_var}" if hue_var else f"Bar Plot: {selected_channel}"
                    if st.button("Add Bar Plot to Canvas", key="add_bar_to_canvas"):
                        if add_plot_to_canvas(fig, canvas_title):
                            st.success("Plot added to canvas!")

                    # Close figure to free memory, unless it was stored as a direct reference in Canvas
                    if not getattr(fig, '_glow_is_direct_ref', False):
                        plt.close(fig)

                    # --- Export CSV Logic ---
                    st.divider()
                    st.markdown("#### Export Stats")

                    # Calculate applied gate strings using processed gates
                    gate_descriptions = []
                    for gate in processed_gates:
                         ch = gate.get('channel')
                         th = gate.get('threshold')
                         # Check direction? apply_gates_to_dataframe might have stripped it, or not?
                         # It returns original dict with potentially modified threshold
                         if isinstance(th, (tuple, list)) and len(th) == 2:
                             gate_descriptions.append(f"{ch} {th[0]:.1f}-{th[1]:.1f}")
                         else:
                             # Simplified description
                             gate_descriptions.append(f"{ch} > {th:.1f}" if gate.get('direction') == 'positive' else f"{ch} < {th:.1f}")

                    gates_applied_str = "; ".join(gate_descriptions) if gate_descriptions else "None"

                    # 2. Determine Grouping Columns
                    # Priority: Sample, Experiment, Replicate
                    group_candidates = ['Sample', 'Experiment_Name', 'Experiment Name', replicate_col]
                    actual_group_cols = [c for c in group_candidates if c and c in filtered_df.columns]

                    # Ensure hue_var is included if it's grouping the bars
                    if hue_var and hue_var in filtered_df.columns and hue_var not in actual_group_cols:
                        actual_group_cols.append(hue_var)

                    # Remove duplicates while preserving order
                    actual_group_cols = list(dict.fromkeys(actual_group_cols))

                    if actual_group_cols:
                        # compute mean
                        summary_df = filtered_df.groupby(actual_group_cols)[selected_channel].mean().reset_index()
                        summary_df.rename(columns={selected_channel: f"Mean_{selected_channel}"}, inplace=True)

                        # Add metadata
                        summary_df['Gates_Applied'] = gates_applied_str

                        # Reorder Columns: Group keys, then Mean, then Gates
                        final_cols = actual_group_cols + [f"Mean_{selected_channel}", "Gates_Applied"]
                        summary_df = summary_df[final_cols]

                        # Export
                        csv_data = summary_df.to_csv(index=False)
                        st.download_button(
                            label="Download Mean Values (CSV)",
                            data=csv_data,
                            file_name=f"bar_stats_{selected_channel}.csv",
                            mime="text/csv",
                            help="Export the mean values of the plotted bars."
                        )
                    else:
                        st.warning("Could not determine grouping columns for export.")

                except Exception as e:
                    st.error(f"Error: {e}")

==================================================
FILE: ui/canvas/view.py

import streamlit as st
import io
import numpy as np
import warnings
from glow.canvas.functions import get_canvas_plots, remove_plot_from_canvas, move_plot_in_canvas

def render_canvas_tab():
    st.header("Plot Canvas")

    plots = get_canvas_plots()
    if not plots:
        st.info("No plots added to canvas yet. Use 'Add to Canvas' buttons from other tabs.")
        return

    # Global Canvas Layout Settings
    with st.expander("Canvas Layout Settings", expanded=False):
        n_cols = st.slider("Number of Columns", 1, 4, 2, key="canvas_n_cols")

    # Creates columns for grid layout
    cols = st.columns(n_cols)

    # Iterate over plots
    # keys are now UUIDs, values are dicts containing 'title'
    for i, (plot_id, plot_data) in enumerate(plots.items()):
        col = cols[i % n_cols]

        # Extract Display Title
        title = plot_data.get("title", plot_id)

        with col:
            st.markdown(f"### {title}")

            if "settings" not in plot_data:
                plot_data["settings"] = {"font_scale": 1.0, "dot_scale": 1.0}
            settings = plot_data["settings"]

            # Control Toolbar
            # Use unique plot_id for keys to avoid collisions if titles were (hypothetically) same
            # although usually unique per run, now definitely unique.
            c1, c2, c3, c4 = st.columns([1, 1, 2, 1])
            with c1:
                if st.button("⬆", key=f"up_{plot_id}", help="Move Backward"):
                    move_plot_in_canvas(plot_id, "up")
                    st.rerun()
            with c2:
                if st.button("⬇", key=f"down_{plot_id}", help="Move Forward"):
                    move_plot_in_canvas(plot_id, "down")
                    st.rerun()

            with st.expander("Customize"):
                # Aspect Ratio Controls
                c_ar1, c_ar2 = st.columns(2)
                with c_ar1:
                    width_scale = st.slider("Width Scale", 0.5, 3.0, settings.get("width_scale", 1.0), 0.1, key=f"ws_{plot_id}")
                with c_ar2:
                    height_scale = st.slider("Height Scale", 0.5, 3.0, settings.get("height_scale", 1.0), 0.1, key=f"hs_{plot_id}")

                # Font & Markers
                font_scale = st.slider("Font/Zoom Scale", 0.5, 3.0, settings.get("font_scale", 1.0), 0.1, key=f"fs_{plot_id}")
                dot_scale = st.slider("Dot Scale", 0.1, 5.0, settings.get("dot_scale", 1.0), 0.1, key=f"ds_{plot_id}")

                # Introspection to determine controls
                has_mappable = False

                # We need to peek at the object to know what controls to show
                if plot_data.get("type") == "matplotlib":
                    f = plot_data["fig"]
                    for ax in f.axes:
                         for c in ax.collections:
                             # Check for ScalarMappable characteristics
                             # It must have a colormap and an array of values mapping to it
                             if hasattr(c, "get_cmap") and hasattr(c, "get_array"):
                                 arr = c.get_array()
                                 if arr is not None and len(arr) > 1: # Must have data to map
                                     has_mappable = True
                                     break
                         if has_mappable:
                             break

                elif plot_data.get("type") == "plotly":
                    f = plot_data["data"]
                    # Check traces
                    if hasattr(f, "data"):
                        for t in f.data:
                            if "marker" in t and ("color" in t.marker or "colorscale" in t.marker):
                                # check if color is array or colorscale present
                                if "colorscale" in t.marker or (isinstance(t.marker.color, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)) and len(t.marker.color) > 1):
                                     has_mappable = True
                                     break

                # Font Family
                font_families = ["default", "sans-serif", "serif", "monospace"]
                font_family = st.selectbox("Font Family", font_families, index=font_families.index(settings.get("font_family", "default")), key=f"ff_{plot_id}")

                # Colormap Override (If Mappable)
                # Ensure override is always active if detected
                main_cmap = "viridis"
                if has_mappable:
                    # User requested default to "viridis" (which is coincidentally detecting as default anyway)
                    # And allow changing.

                    cmaps = ["viridis", "plasma", "inferno", "magma", "cividis", "Greys", "Purples", "Blues", "Greens", "Oranges", "Reds", "YlOrBr", "YlOrRd", "OrRd", "PuRd", "RdPu", "BuPu", "GnBu", "PuBu", "YlGnBu", "PuBuGn", "BuGn", "YlGn", "jet", "rainbow", "Spectral"]
                    current_cmap = settings.get("main_cmap", "viridis")
                    if current_cmap not in cmaps:
                        current_cmap = "viridis"

                    main_cmap = st.selectbox("Select Colormap", cmaps, index=cmaps.index(current_cmap), key=f"mc_{plot_id}")

                # Save settings
                plot_data["settings"]["width_scale"] = width_scale
                plot_data["settings"]["height_scale"] = height_scale
                plot_data["settings"]["font_scale"] = font_scale
                plot_data["settings"]["dot_scale"] = dot_scale
                plot_data["settings"]["font_family"] = font_family
                plot_data["settings"]["main_cmap"] = main_cmap
                # Force cleanup of old keys if present
                if "override_color_en" in plot_data["settings"]:
                    del plot_data["settings"]["override_color_en"]
                if "override_line_color_en" in plot_data["settings"]:
                    del plot_data["settings"]["override_line_color_en"]

            with c4:
                if st.button("🗑", key=f"rm_{plot_id}", help="Remove Plot"):
                    remove_plot_from_canvas(plot_id)
                    st.rerun()

            # Render logic
            ptype = plot_data.get("type")
            try:
                if ptype == "matplotlib":
                    fig = plot_data["fig"]

                    # Logic to simulate "Font Scaling" by resizing the figure in inches
                    # while maintaining pixel density.
                    if not hasattr(fig, "_glow_orig_size"):
                        fig._glow_orig_size = fig.get_size_inches()

                    w_orig, h_orig = fig._glow_orig_size

                    # Apply Aspect Ratio & Font Scale
                    # To effectively scale font UP, we shrink the figure size (inches) so that relative text size is larger
                    # To stretch width, we multiply width inches
                    target_w = (w_orig * width_scale) / font_scale
                    target_h = (h_orig * height_scale) / font_scale

                    fig.set_size_inches(target_w, target_h)

                    # Update Font Family
                    # Changing global params often tricky, let's iterate text objects
                    if font_family != "default":
                        # Use introspection or a more robust find method if matplotlib.text is not directly exposed
                        # usually available as matplotlib.text.Text
                        import matplotlib.text as mpl_text
                        for txt in fig.findobj(match=mpl_text.Text):
                             txt.set_fontfamily(font_family)

                    # Apply Dot Scaling & Color Override for Matplotlib
                    # Iterate through axes and collections
                    for ax in fig.axes:
                        # Update Axis Labels/Title Colors if needed? Usually we just color data.

                        for collection in ax.collections:
                            # Skip PolyCollections (like fill_between/ridgelines) to preserve their specific facecolors
                            # Importing here to assume matplotlib is available if we have a fig
                            from matplotlib.collections import PolyCollection
                            if isinstance(collection, PolyCollection):
                                continue

                            # Only apply to PathCollections (Scatter) which have sizes
                            if hasattr(collection, "get_sizes") and hasattr(collection, "set_sizes"):
                                # Check if we stored original sizes
                                if not hasattr(collection, "_glow_orig_sizes"):
                                    sizes = collection.get_sizes()
                                    if len(sizes) > 0:
                                        collection._glow_orig_sizes = sizes.copy()
                                    else:
                                        collection._glow_orig_sizes = [10] # Default s=10

                                # Apply scale
                                if hasattr(collection, "_glow_orig_sizes"):
                                    new_sizes = [s * dot_scale for s in collection._glow_orig_sizes]
                                    collection.set_sizes(new_sizes)

                            # Color Override (Colormap) - Always Active if detected logic applies
                            # We re-check the mappable condition here to be safe and avoid breaking non-mappables
                            if hasattr(collection, "set_cmap") and hasattr(collection, "get_array"):
                                 arr = collection.get_array()
                                 if arr is not None and len(arr) > 1:
                                     collection.set_cmap(main_cmap)
                        # Automatic "Enabling" without user action might be intrusive if they already liked their plot.
                        # Showing the option is the "Enablement".

                    # Force redraw after all modifications to ensure artists are updated
                    # This is critical for proper rendering of polygons/fills on log scale after resizing
                    fig.canvas.draw()

                    # Render to PNG buffer
                    buf = io.BytesIO()
                    # Increase DPI with scale to maintain sharpness/resolution
                    with warnings.catch_warnings():
                         warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
                         fig.savefig(buf, format="png", bbox_inches="tight", dpi=100 * font_scale)

                    st.image(buf, width="stretch")

                    # Reset buffer for downloading SVG (optional, or reuse fig)
                    # Stack buttons vertically
                    buf_svg = io.BytesIO()
                    try:
                        with warnings.catch_warnings():
                            warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
                            fig.savefig(buf_svg, format="svg", bbox_inches="tight")
                        st.download_button("Download SVG", buf_svg.getvalue(), f"{title}.svg", "image/svg+xml", key=f"dl_svg_{plot_id}")
                    except Exception:
                        pass

                    buf_png = io.BytesIO()
                    try:
                        with warnings.catch_warnings():
                            warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
                            fig.savefig(buf_png, format="png", bbox_inches="tight", dpi=300)
                        st.download_button("Download PNG", buf_png.getvalue(), f"{title}.png", "image/png", key=f"dl_png_{plot_id}")
                    except Exception:
                        pass

                    buf_pdf = io.BytesIO()
                    try:
                        with warnings.catch_warnings():
                            warnings.filterwarnings("ignore", category=UserWarning, message="This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout")
                            fig.savefig(buf_pdf, format="pdf", bbox_inches="tight")
                        st.download_button("Download PDF", buf_pdf.getvalue(), f"{title}.pdf", "application/pdf", key=f"dl_pdf_{plot_id}")
                    except Exception:
                        pass

                elif ptype == "plotly":
                    fig = plot_data["data"]

                    # Update Font Size & Family
                    update_font = dict(size=12 * font_scale)
                    if font_family != "default":
                        update_font["family"] = font_family

                    fig.update_layout(font=update_font)

                    # Color Override - Always active for mappables
                    # Detection logic was: has colorscale or color array.
                    # We blindly update trace marker colorscale if it exists or we can infer it should?
                    # Safer: Update colorscale on ALL traces that theoretically support it.
                    # If they don't have scalar data, colorscale is ignored by plotly usually.
                    fig.update_traces(marker=dict(colorscale=main_cmap))

                    # Update Markers logic for plotly
                    if dot_scale != 1.0:
                        # We don't have easy original size tracking here like mpl object reference
                        # We just apply a fixed size modifier or assume default=8
                        # Simplification: Set size explicitly
                        fig.update_traces(marker=dict(size=8 * dot_scale), selector=dict(type='scatter'))

                    # Usage of Aspect Ratio
                    # We always use container width to ensure fitting

                    layout_updates = {}

                    # To adjust aspect ratio while fitting width:
                    # Width is fixed (Container Width).
                    # If WidthScale increases (Wants wider), we must DECREASE HEIGHT.
                    # If HeightScale increases (Wants taller), we INCREASE HEIGHT.

                    base_h = 450 # Default Plotly height standard

                    # New Height = Base * HeightScale / WidthScale
                    new_height = base_h * (height_scale / width_scale)
                    layout_updates["height"] = int(new_height)
                    layout_updates["width"] = None # Let container handle it

                    fig.update_layout(**layout_updates)

                    st.plotly_chart(fig, width="stretch")

                    # Download Buttons for Plotly
                    # Stack vertically
                    try:
                        # Requires kaleido
                        img_bytes = fig.to_image(format="png", scale=2)
                        st.download_button("Download PNG", img_bytes, f"{title}.png", "image/png", key=f"dl_p_png_{plot_id}")
                    except Exception:
                        pass
                        # st.caption("PNG export needs 'kaleido'")

                    try:
                        img_bytes = fig.to_image(format="pdf")
                        st.download_button("Download PDF", img_bytes, f"{title}.pdf", "application/pdf", key=f"dl_p_pdf_{plot_id}")
                    except Exception:
                        pass
                        # st.caption("PDF export needs 'kaleido'")

                elif ptype == "asperatio":
                    # Custom rendering for asperatio data
                    asp_data = plot_data["data"]
                    st.write("**Asperatio Plot**")
                    st.dataframe(asp_data)

                else:
                    st.warning(f"Unsupported plot type: {ptype}")

            except Exception as e:
                st.error(f"Error rendering plot: {e}")

            st.divider()
